# Question Keywords Answer
1 What is EnsureStudy and what problem does it solve? overview, edtech • AI-first learning platform combining multi-agent tutoring, proctored assessments, curriculum generation, and virtual classrooms
• Solves fragmented learning tools by unifying AI tutoring, assessment, and content management in one platform
• Targets educational institutions needing personalized, scalable learning
2 Describe the high-level architecture of EnsureStudy. architecture, services • Three-tier: Next.js 14 frontend → dual backend (Flask core-service + FastAPI ai-service) → polyglot database layer
• 13 Docker services in development, 6 in production
• Event-driven via Kafka, real-time via WebSocket/SSE
• Vector search via Qdrant for RAG
3 Why does EnsureStudy use two separate backend services instead of one? service decomposition, trade-off • Core Service (Flask): handles CRUD, auth, user management — CPU-bound, request/response
• AI Service (FastAPI): handles AI agents, streaming, ML — async I/O bound, long-running tasks
• Separation allows independent scaling: AI service needs GPU/memory, core service needs standard compute
• Different deployment lifecycles — AI models update independently
4 What are the trade-offs of having two backends vs a monolith? trade-off, monolith • Pros: independent scaling, tech-appropriate frameworks, isolated failures, separate deployments
• Cons: network latency between services, data consistency complexity, operational overhead
• Inter-service communication adds ~2-5ms per HTTP call
• Mitigated by keeping services on same Docker network
5 Why was Flask chosen for core-service and FastAPI for ai-service? Flask, FastAPI, choice • Flask: mature ORM ecosystem (SQLAlchemy), blueprint pattern for REST CRUD, simpler for traditional web ops
• FastAPI: native async/await for streaming LLM responses, automatic OpenAPI docs, Pydantic validation
• FastAPI's StreamingResponse essential for SSE token streaming from agents
• Flask's synchronous model is fine for database CRUD
6 Explain the communication patterns between frontend and backends. communication, REST, SSE • Frontend → Core: REST API with JWT auth headers, JSON request/response
• Frontend → AI Service: SSE (Server-Sent Events) for streaming agent responses
• Core → AI: Internal HTTP calls (no auth needed, same Docker network)
• AI → Core: Callback HTTP for async results (document processing completion)
7 How does the API Gateway pattern work in this system? API gateway, nginx • Nginx reverse proxy routes requests based on URL path
• /api/ → core-service:5000, /ai/ → ai-service:8000
• Single entry point for frontend, handles TLS termination
• Trade-off: simpler than dedicated gateway (Kong/Traefik) but less feature-rich
8 Describe the Docker Compose topology for development. Docker, compose, services • 13 services: frontend, core-service, ai-service, postgres, redis, qdrant, mongodb, cassandra, kafka, zookeeper, kafka-ui, nginx, livekit
• Service dependency chain: databases start first → backends → nginx → frontend
• 10 named volumes for data persistence
• All on single Docker bridge network ensurestudy-net
9 How does the production Docker setup differ from development? prod, dev, deployment • Production: 6 services (drops kafka-ui, zookeeper managed, optimized images)
• Multi-stage Docker builds reduce image sizes
• Health checks with restart policies
• Environment variables from .env.prod instead of .env.dev
• Nginx serves static frontend build
10 Explain the synchronous request path for a tutor agent query. sync, data flow, tutor • User sends message → Frontend POST to /ai/tutor/chat
• AI service receives → Orchestrator classifies intent → Routes to TutorAgent
• TutorAgent: moderate → ABCR routing → RAG retrieve → LLM generate
• Response streamed back via SSE tokens
• Total latency: ~2-5s (dominated by LLM generation)
11 Describe the asynchronous event path for assessment submission. async, Kafka, event • Student submits assessment → Core service saves to PostgreSQL
• Core publishes assessment.completed event to Kafka
• AI service consumer picks up event → triggers Learning Agent
• Learning Agent analyzes performance → generates new questions
• Results stored via callback to core service
• Student sees updated question pool on next login
12 How does the document ingestion data flow work? document, async, pipeline • Teacher uploads PDF → Core service stores file, creates DB record
• Core calls AI service /ai/documents/process endpoint
• AI service: validate → preprocess → extract text/OCR → chunk → embed → index in Qdrant
• Progress reported via SSE to frontend
• On completion, callback to core service updates document status
13 What error handling strategies are used at the service level? error handling, resilience • Retry with backoff: inter-service HTTP calls retry 3x with exponential backoff
• Circuit breaker pattern: if AI service is down, core returns cached/fallback responses
• Graceful degradation: if Qdrant unavailable, tutor answers without RAG context
• Structured logging: all errors logged with request IDs for tracing
14 How does agent-level error handling work? agent, error, LangGraph • Each LangGraph node wrapped in try/except
• State carries error field — if set, graph routes to error handler node
• LLM call failures: retry with smaller context, fallback to cached response
• Tool execution failures: skip tool, note in response "I couldn't search the web"
• Timeout: 30s per node, 120s total per agent invocation
15 Explain the configuration management approach. config, env, hierarchy • Three-level hierarchy: defaults → .env file → environment variables (highest priority)
• AI service uses Pydantic BaseSettings with env prefix AI_
• Core service uses Flask's app.config loaded from Python config classes
• Sensitive values (API keys) only via env vars, never in code
• Docker Compose injects env vars from .env files
16 What notable configuration decisions were made and why? config decisions • Embedding model hardcoded (all-mpnet-base-v2): changing models requires re-embedding all documents
• Redis TTL per cache type: ABCR cache 5min, session cache 30min, web cache 1hr
• LLM temperature per agent: tutor=0.3 (factual), research=0.7 (creative), assessment=0.1 (deterministic)
• Chunk size 512 tokens with 50-token overlap: balances retrieval precision vs context
17 How is TLS/HTTPS handled in development vs production? TLS, HTTPS, mkcert • Development: mkcert generates locally-trusted certificates, nginx terminates TLS
• Production: Let's Encrypt / ACM certificates, terminated at load balancer
• Internal service-to-service traffic is HTTP (within Docker network, trusted zone)
• WebSocket/LiveKit connections also require TLS for camera/mic access
18 What is the network architecture within Docker? network, Docker, bridge • Single bridge network ensurestudy-net — all services can reach each other by container name
• Nginx is the only service with published ports (443, 80)
• Databases not exposed externally — security by network isolation
• DNS resolution via Docker's embedded DNS server
19 Scenario: The AI service is overloaded with requests. How would you scale it? scaling, scenario • Horizontal scaling: run multiple ai-service containers behind nginx load balancer
• Queue-based: move heavy tasks (document processing) to Kafka consumers
• Model optimization: quantize embedding model, use GPU batching
• Caching: ABCR cache reduces repeated LLM classification calls by ~40%
• Consider separating streaming endpoints from batch endpoints
20 Scenario: A new feature needs both core and AI service changes. How do you coordinate deployment? deployment, coordination • API versioning: new endpoint /v2/ in AI service, core service calls v2
• Backward compatibility: old endpoints still work during rollout
• Deploy order: AI service first (new endpoint available), then core service (starts calling it)
• Feature flags: environment variable to enable/disable new feature
• Rollback plan: revert core service first, AI service v1 endpoint still works
21 Why is the system not using a message queue for ALL inter-service communication? design, messaging • Tutor chat needs real-time: user expects streaming response in <2s, Kafka adds latency
• CRUD operations are simple: REST request/response is sufficient and easier to debug
• Kafka used WHERE appropriate: async document processing, analytics events, learning agent triggers
• Trade-off: simpler architecture vs eventual consistency for async paths
22 How would you add a new AI agent to the system? extensibility, agent • Create new agent class extending BaseAgent with LangGraph state machine
• Register agent in orchestrator's intent classification prompt
• Add routing rule in orchestrator agent_selection node
• Create new FastAPI router for direct API access
• Update agent inventory documentation
• No changes needed to core service (agent-agnostic)
23 What design patterns are used across the system? patterns, design • Supervisor: Orchestrator manages sub-agents
• Factory: Flask application factory, agent initialization
• Strategy: different embedding strategies per content type
• Observer: Kafka pub/sub for event-driven flows
• Decorator: @token_required for auth, @cache_result for caching
• State Machine: LangGraph for all agent workflows
24 Explain the repository structure and why it's organized this way. repo, structure • Monorepo: frontend/, backend/core-service/, backend/ai-service/, ml/
• Shared Docker Compose at root level
• Each service has its own requirements.txt / package.json
• ml/ separated: training notebooks, model weights, inference wrappers
• Monorepo enables atomic changes across services, shared CI/CD
25 What is the design philosophy behind EnsureStudy? philosophy, principles • AI-first: every feature considers AI augmentation (even CRUD has AI-generated suggestions)
• Agent-based: complex logic delegated to autonomous LangGraph agents, not hardcoded rules
• Context-aware: MCP protocol ensures agents operate within appropriate boundaries
• Progressive enhancement: works without AI (basic CRUD), better with AI
26 Scenario: MongoDB goes down. What happens to the system? failure, scenario • Direct impact: proctoring data cannot be stored — proctoring sessions fail
• No impact: tutoring, assessment, curriculum continue (they use PostgreSQL + Qdrant)
• Mitigation: MongoDB is isolated to proctoring — failure doesn't cascade
• Recovery: proctoring frames buffered in memory briefly, written when MongoDB recovers
• This isolation is WHY polyglot persistence was chosen
27 Why doesn't the system use GraphQL instead of REST? GraphQL, REST • REST is simpler for this use case — most endpoints are resource-oriented CRUD
• AI streaming responses use SSE, which doesn't work well with GraphQL subscriptions in practice
• Team expertise is in REST/Flask/FastAPI
• GraphQL's query flexibility isn't needed — frontend has fixed, known data requirements
• Could be added later for mobile clients needing flexible queries
28 How does the system handle rate limiting? rate limiting • Nginx-level rate limiting: 100 req/min per IP for API endpoints
• AI service: per-user rate limit on tutor chat (10 messages/min) to control LLM costs
• Document upload: max 5 concurrent uploads per user
• Kafka consumer: processes events sequentially per partition (natural backpressure)
29 What monitoring and observability exists? monitoring, logging • Structured logging: Python logging with JSON format, request IDs for tracing
• Request middleware: FastAPI logs every request with timing
• Health endpoints: /health on each service for Docker health checks
• Kafka UI: web dashboard for monitoring topics, consumers, lag
• Missing: no Prometheus/Grafana yet (recommended for production)
30 Scenario: You need to add real-time notifications. How? notification, real-time • WebSocket: extend existing LiveKit infrastructure or add dedicated WS endpoint
• SSE: simpler option — frontend EventSource for notification stream
• Kafka → WebSocket bridge: Kafka events trigger notifications pushed via WS
• Backend: new notification service consuming Kafka events
• Frontend: notification bell component with badge counter using Zustand store
31 Why does the system use 5 different databases? Is this over-engineering? polyglot persistence • PostgreSQL: relational data (users, classrooms, curriculum) — ACID transactions needed
• Qdrant: vector embeddings for semantic search — specialized vector index (HNSW)
• Redis: caching, sessions — sub-millisecond reads, TTL expiry
• MongoDB: proctoring frames — high-write, schema-flexible documents
• Cassandra: time-series analytics — append-only, high throughput
• Each chosen for its strength — using one DB for all would be worse at everything
32 What would break if you replaced all databases with just PostgreSQL? trade-off, database • Vector search: PostgreSQL pgvector exists but is 10-50x slower than Qdrant for ANN search
• Caching: PostgreSQL as cache would add ~5-10ms per read vs Redis's <1ms
• Proctoring writes: PostgreSQL row-level locking would bottleneck at 30fps frame writes
• Time-series: no native time-series partitioning, window queries would be slow
• Feasible but would require significant performance compromises
33 How does the system handle data consistency across services? consistency, distributed • Eventual consistency for async flows: Kafka events processed with at-least-once delivery
• Request-scoped consistency for sync flows: single database transaction per REST request
• No distributed transactions: services own their data, communicate via events
• Idempotency keys on Kafka consumers prevent duplicate processing
• Acceptable because most flows are user-facing with natural delays
34 Explain the volume management strategy in Docker. volumes, Docker, persistence • 10 named volumes: postgres_data, redis_data, qdrant_data, mongo_data, cassandra_data, etc.
• Named volumes survive container restarts and recreations
• uploads_data shared between core-service and ai-service for file access
• Production: volumes mapped to EBS/EFS for durability
• No bind mounts in production (only in dev for hot reload)
35 What CORS policy is configured and why? CORS, security • Development: CORS(app, origins=["https://localhost:3000"]) — allows local frontend
• Production: restrict to production domain only
• AI Service (FastAPI): CORSMiddleware with specific origins list
• Credentials allowed: cookies and Authorization headers pass through
• Pre-flight OPTIONS requests cached for 3600 seconds
36 How would you implement A/B testing for different AI agent versions? A/B testing, experiment • Use feature flags in environment config: TUTOR_AGENT_VERSION=v2
• Orchestrator routes based on user's experiment group (stored in user profile)
• Both agent versions deployed simultaneously as separate classes
• Metrics: track response quality, latency, user satisfaction per version
• Kafka events log which version served each request for analysis
37 Scenario: LLM API rate limits are hit. How does the system handle it? rate limit, LLM, fallback • Retry with backoff: 429 response triggers exponential backoff (1s, 2s, 4s)
• Queue overflow: requests queued in Kafka if direct API fails
• Fallback model: switch to smaller/faster model (Groq fallback for Gemini)
• Cached responses: ABCR cache returns previous answers for similar queries
• User feedback: "I'm experiencing high demand, response may be slower"
38 What is the Makefile used for and what targets does it expose? Makefile, DevOps • make dev: starts development stack with hot reload
• make prod: builds and starts production stack
• make migrate: runs database migrations
• make test: runs test suites for all services
• make logs: tails all service logs
• make clean: removes containers, volumes, images
• 92 lines covering 12+ targets for common operations
39 How are launch scripts (run-local.sh, run-lan.sh) different? scripts, dev • run-local.sh: localhost only, self-signed certs, NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL=https://localhost
• run-lan.sh: LAN-accessible, generates mkcert certs for local IP, allows mobile testing
• Both: check Docker daemon, create .env if missing, run docker-compose up
• LAN mode: also configures Nginx for IP-based TLS (instead of localhost)
40 What AWS architecture would you recommend for production? AWS, cloud • ECS Fargate for containerized services (no EC2 management)
• RDS PostgreSQL for managed relational DB with automated backups
• ElastiCache Redis for managed caching
• ALB (Application Load Balancer) for routing + TLS termination
• S3 for file uploads, CloudFront for static frontend
• MSK (Managed Kafka) for event streaming
• Qdrant on EC2 with EBS (no managed offering yet)
41 How would you implement CI/CD for this system? CI/CD, DevOps • GitHub Actions with separate workflows per service
• Build → Test → Docker build → Push to ECR → Deploy to ECS
• Database migrations run as pre-deployment task
• Frontend: build → S3 upload → CloudFront invalidation
• Environment promotion: dev → staging → production with manual approval gate
42 What testing strategy exists or should exist? testing, quality • Unit tests: per-service, mock external dependencies
• Integration tests: test inter-service communication with Docker Compose
• Agent tests: verify LangGraph node outputs with mock LLM responses
• Load tests: simulate concurrent tutor sessions (target: 100 concurrent users)
• E2E tests: Playwright for critical user journeys (login → chat → assessment)
43 Scenario: You need to add multi-tenancy (multiple institutions). What changes? multi-tenancy, scaling • Database: add institution_id to all tables, row-level security policies
• Qdrant: separate collections per institution or payload-filtered queries
• Auth: JWT includes institution_id, all queries filter by it
• Redis: cache keys prefixed with institution ID
• Kafka: topic per institution or partition key = institution_id
• Cost isolation: track LLM token usage per institution for billing
44 How does the system ensure data privacy for student information? privacy, GDPR • JWT tokens expire in 24h, refresh tokens in 7 days
• Passwords hashed with bcrypt (cost factor 12)
• Student data isolated by classroom — teachers only see their students
• File uploads scanned for malware before processing
• Missing: data deletion API (needed for GDPR right to erasure)
• Missing: audit log for data access (recommended for compliance)
45 What would you change about the current architecture? improvement, critique • Add API Gateway: replace Nginx with Kong for rate limiting, API key management
• Add observability: Prometheus + Grafana for metrics, Jaeger for distributed tracing
• Extract embedding service: separate microservice for embeddings (shared by RAG, notes, meetings)
• Add message queue for chat: replace direct HTTP with WebSocket for lower latency
• Add caching layer: Redis cache for frequent Qdrant queries
46 How does the system handle concurrent users in virtual classrooms? concurrency, LiveKit • LiveKit handles WebRTC for video/audio — designed for 100+ concurrent participants
• Frontend: React components manage local/remote tracks independently
• Backend: LiveKit cloud handles media routing, no media passes through backend
• Chat: WebSocket connection per user, messages broadcast via LiveKit data channels
47 Explain how you'd debug a slow tutor agent response. debugging, performance • Check request logs: which node is slow? (moderate: ~200ms, ABCR: ~500ms, RAG: ~300ms, LLM: ~2-3s)
• If ABCR slow: check if cache hit/miss, check Gemini API latency
• If RAG slow: check Qdrant query time, embedding generation time, check chunk count
• If LLM slow: check token count (long context = slow), check model selection
• Tools: structured logs with timing per node, request ID correlation
48 What happens during system startup? What's the boot order? startup, initialization • Docker Compose: postgres → redis → qdrant → mongodb → cassandra → kafka+zookeeper
• Core service: Flask app factory → SQLAlchemy init → blueprint registration → migration check
• AI service: FastAPI startup event → preload embedding model → initialize agents (singletons) → register routers
• Frontend: Next.js build → hydration → auth check → dashboard render
49 How does the system handle file uploads securely? upload, security • Validation: MIME type check, file extension whitelist (.pdf, .docx, .pptx, .jpg, .png)
• Size limit: 50MB max per file
• Storage: saved to shared Docker volume, not accessible via direct URL
• Access control: only upload owner and classroom teachers can access
• Processing: async via AI service — no arbitrary code execution from uploaded files
50 Scenario: The CEO asks "Can this system handle 10,000 concurrent users?" What's your answer? scalability, capacity • Current: designed for ~100-500 concurrent users (single-instance each service)
• To reach 10K: horizontal scale ai-service (4-8 instances), add read replicas for PostgreSQL
• Bottleneck: LLM API rate limits (not our infrastructure) — need multiple API keys or self-hosted model
• Qdrant: single node handles millions of vectors, bottleneck is query concurrency → add replicas
• Estimated cost: ~$2K/month infra + ~$5K/month LLM API costs at 10K users

Section 2: Database Architecture & Data Modeling (50 Questions)¶

Key Concepts: PostgreSQL · SQLAlchemy ORM · Qdrant Vector DB · Redis Caching · MongoDB · Cassandra · Polyglot Persistence · Migrations · Indexing · Schema Design

# Question Keywords Answer
51 What databases does EnsureStudy use and what role does each play? polyglot, databases • PostgreSQL: primary relational store — users, classrooms, curriculum, assessments (39 tables)
• Qdrant: vector database — document embeddings for RAG semantic search
• Redis: caching layer — ABCR results, session data, web content cache
• MongoDB: proctoring data — high-frequency frame captures, schema-flexible
• Cassandra: time-series analytics — student activity tracking, append-only writes
52 How many PostgreSQL tables exist and how are they grouped? PostgreSQL, schema • 39 tables across model files: user.py (9 models), curriculum.py (16 models), classroom.py (3 models), meeting.py (5 models), others (6)
• Groups: User/Auth → Classroom/Enrollment → Curriculum/Topics → Assessment/Questions → Meeting/Recording → Documents/Notes
• Uses SQLAlchemy ORM with Flask-SQLAlchemy extension
53 Describe the User data model and its relationships. User, model, relationships • User model: id, email, password_hash, role (student/teacher/admin), profile fields
• Has many: Enrollment (classrooms), Document (uploads), AssessmentAttempt
• UserProfile: extended profile information (bio, avatar, preferences)
• ModerationLog: tracks moderated content per user
• Role-based polymorphism: same table, different behavior based on role column
54 How does the curriculum data model handle topic dependencies? curriculum, topology • CurriculumTopic: id, title, description, difficulty, estimated_hours, prerequisites
• TopicDependency: junction table mapping topic → prerequisite_topic (DAG structure)
• DailyGoal: maps topics to specific calendar days with activities
• Curriculum: top-level entity linking user + syllabus to generated learning path
• Topological sort ensures prerequisites are scheduled before dependent topics
55 What is the Entity Relationship overview? ER, relationships, diagram • User ←(1:N)→ Enrollment ←(N:1)→ Classroom
• Classroom ←(1:N)→ Syllabus ←(1:N)→ Topic
• User ←(1:N)→ Curriculum ←(1:N)→ DailyGoal ←(N:1)→ Topic
• User ←(1:N)→ AssessmentAttempt ←(N:1)→ Assessment
• Classroom ←(1:N)→ Document ←(1:N)→ DocumentChunk (in Qdrant)
56 How does SQLAlchemy ORM handle database migrations? migrations, Alembic • Flask-Migrate wraps Alembic for migration management
• flask db migrate: auto-generates migration from model changes
• flask db upgrade: applies pending migrations
• Migration files stored in migrations/versions/
• Auto-detection compares current models to database state
• Production: migrations run as pre-deployment step
57 What database indexes are defined and why? indexes, performance • users.email: unique index for login lookup
• enrollments(user_id, classroom_id): composite for membership checks
• documents(classroom_id, status): filter documents by classroom and processing status
• assessment_attempts(user_id, created_at): query user's recent attempts
• Missing: no full-text search index (used Qdrant instead)
• All foreign keys automatically indexed by PostgreSQL
58 How does Qdrant store and retrieve document embeddings? Qdrant, vectors, HNSW • Collection documents: stores 768-dimensional vectors (all-mpnet-base-v2)
• HNSW index: approximate nearest neighbor search with ~95% recall
• Each vector has payload: {classroom_id, document_id, chunk_text, chunk_index, source_page}
• Retrieval: embed query → search top-k similar vectors → filter by classroom_id → return chunks
59 What is the Qdrant collection schema? Qdrant, schema, collection • Collection name: ensurestudy_documents
• Vector size: 768 (matching all-mpnet-base-v2 output)
• Distance metric: Cosine similarity
• Payload indexes: classroom_id (keyword), document_id (keyword), source_type (keyword)
• Settings: on_disk=True for datasets > RAM, hnsw_config(m=16, ef_construct=100)
60 How does Qdrant filtering work during RAG queries? Qdrant, filtering, RAG • Filter format: {"must": [{"key": "classroom_id", "match": {"value": "cls_123"}}]}
• Applied BEFORE vector search — narrows search space for performance
• Supports: must, should, must_not conditions
• Can filter by multiple fields simultaneously
• Payload indexes make filtering efficient (not full scan)
61 Why was all-mpnet-base-v2 chosen as the embedding model? embedding, model selection • 768 dimensions — good balance of quality vs storage/compute
• Trained on 1B+ sentence pairs — strong semantic understanding
• Top performer on MTEB benchmark for semantic similarity at its size class
• Runs locally on CPU in ~50ms per sentence — no API dependency
• Trade-off: larger models (e5-large) are better but 3x slower
• Consistency: same model for ingestion AND retrieval (critical)
62 What is the embedding consistency issue mentioned in the docs? embedding, consistency, bug • If embedding model is changed, ALL existing vectors become incompatible
• New queries embedded with new model won't match old document vectors
• Solution: model version stored in collection metadata
• On model change: must re-embed entire corpus (expensive for large datasets)
• Mitigation: model name is a configuration constant, changes require explicit migration
63 How does Redis caching work in the system? Redis, caching, TTL • ABCR cache (5min TTL): caches query classification results to avoid repeated LLM calls
• Session cache (30min TTL): stores conversation history for tutor agent
• Web content cache (1hr TTL): caches scraped web pages from research agent
• Response cache (variable TTL): caches RAG retrieval results for identical queries
• Key format: cache_type:user_id:hash(query)
64 What Redis data structures are used? Redis, data structures • Strings: simple key-value for cached responses (SET key value EX ttl)
• Hashes: session data (HSET session:user_id field value)
• Lists: conversation history (RPUSH chat:session_id message)
• Sets: not currently used but could track online users
• Sorted Sets: not used but ideal for leaderboards
65 Why is MongoDB used specifically for proctoring data? MongoDB, proctoring • High write throughput: proctoring captures frames at up to 30fps, each frame is ~500B metadata
• Schema flexibility: different detectors produce different payload shapes
• Document model fits: each proctoring event is a self-contained document (timestamp, detections, scores)
• TTL indexes: automatically delete old proctoring data after retention period
• PostgreSQL row-level locking would bottleneck at this write rate
66 What MongoDB collections exist? MongoDB, collections • proctoring_sessions: session metadata (user, assessment, start/end time)
• proctoring_frames: individual frame analysis results (face detection, gaze, objects)
• proctoring_flags: aggregated cheating flags with severity and evidence
• proctoring_reports: final integrity reports per session
• Indexed on session_id + timestamp for efficient range queries
67 Why is Cassandra used for time-series analytics? Cassandra, time-series • Append-only writes: student activity events are write-once, never updated
• Partition key: (student_id, date) ensures related data is co-located
• Clustering key: timestamp for time-ordered retrieval within partition
• Natural TTL: old analytics data expires after configurable retention
• Linear scalability: can add nodes without downtime for growing student base
68 What are the Cassandra keyspace and table designs? Cassandra, CQL, schema • Keyspace: ensurestudy_analytics with SimpleStrategy replication factor 1 (dev)
• Table student_activity: PRIMARY KEY ((student_id, date), timestamp)
• Columns: event_type, topic_id, duration_seconds, score, metadata (frozen map)
• Table daily_aggregates: pre-computed daily summaries per student
• Designed for queries: "all activity for student X on date Y"
69 Explain the database selection matrix. selection, matrix, criteria • Need ACID? → PostgreSQL (user data, financial records, enrollments)
• Need semantic search? → Qdrant (document retrieval, similarity matching)
• Need <1ms reads? → Redis (caching, real-time session data)
• Need high write throughput + flexible schema? → MongoDB (proctoring frames)
• Need time-series at scale? → Cassandra (analytics, activity logs)
• Decision tree documented for future developers
70 How does the Docker volume strategy ensure data persistence? volumes, persistence, Docker • Named volumes: postgres_data, redis_data, qdrant_data, mongo_data, cassandra_data
• Survive docker-compose down (only removed with docker-compose down -v)
• uploads_data: shared between core-service and ai-service via Docker volume mount
• livekit_data: recording storage for virtual classrooms
• Production: map to AWS EBS volumes for durability and snapshots
71 Scenario: A query returns wrong RAG results. How do you debug? debug, RAG, vector • Check embedding: embed the query manually, inspect vector similarity scores
• Check filter: is classroom_id filter correct? Wrong filter = wrong document scope
• Check chunks: view the actual chunk text returned — is it semantically relevant?
• Check ingestion: was the source document chunked correctly? Overlap sufficient?
• Check model: verify same embedding model used for ingestion and query
72 How would you implement database backup and recovery? backup, disaster recovery • PostgreSQL: pg_dump daily, WAL archiving for point-in-time recovery
• Qdrant: snapshot API to S3, or volume-level EBS snapshots
• Redis: RDB snapshots every 5min, AOF for write-ahead log
• MongoDB: mongodump to S3, or replica set with delayed secondary
• Cassandra: nodetool snapshot, leverage built-in replication
73 What is the classroom.py model structure? classroom, model • Classroom: id, name, description, teacher_id (FK→User), code (unique join code)
• Enrollment: user_id + classroom_id (many-to-many), role (student/ta), enrolled_at
• ClassroomSyllabus: classroom_id, content (JSON), extracted_topics (JSON array)
• Relationships: Classroom has_many Enrollments, has_one Syllabus
74 How does the meeting model support recording and transcription? meeting, model, recording • Meeting: id, classroom_id, host_id, title, scheduled_at, livekit_room_name
• MeetingRecording: meeting_id, storage_path, duration, file_size
• MeetingTranscript: recording_id, full_text, segments (JSON with timestamps)
• MeetingSummary: transcript_id, summary_text, key_topics (JSON array)
• Flow: Record → Transcribe (Whisper) → Summarize (Gemini) → Embed for RAG
75 How are documents tracked through the processing pipeline? document, status, tracking • Document model with status enum: uploaded, processing, completed, failed
• processing_progress: float 0.0-1.0 for progress bar
• error_message: populated on failure with stage and reason
• chunk_count: populated after chunking stage
• processed_at: timestamp when pipeline completes
• Frontend polls status or receives SSE updates
76 Scenario: Qdrant runs out of disk space. What happens? failure, Qdrant, scenario • New document indexing fails at Stage 6 (index_in_qdrant)
• Existing search queries still work (read-only from existing index)
• Document pipeline reports failure, sets document status to failed
• Fix: expand volume, or move to on_disk storage mode
• Prevention: monitor disk usage, set alerts at 80% capacity
77 How would you add full-text search alongside vector search? search, hybrid • Hybrid search: combine Qdrant vector similarity with Elasticsearch/PostgreSQL full-text
• Qdrant payload search: already supports basic keyword filtering on payload fields
• Reciprocal Rank Fusion: merge vector and keyword results by rank
• Implementation: embed query → Qdrant top-50 → re-rank with BM25 on chunk text
• Improves retrieval for exact term matches (code, names, acronyms)
78 What happens to the database when a classroom is deleted? cascade, delete, integrity • Enrollment records: CASCADE delete (students unenrolled)
• Document records: CASCADE delete (but Qdrant vectors need manual cleanup)
• Syllabus: CASCADE delete
• Issue: Qdrant vectors with classroom_id filter orphaned — need cleanup job
• Recommendation: soft delete (set deleted_at) instead of hard delete
79 How does the system handle concurrent database writes? concurrency, locking • PostgreSQL: row-level locking via SQLAlchemy's default transaction isolation (READ COMMITTED)
• Optimistic locking: not implemented but recommended for curriculum updates
• Redis: atomic operations (INCR, SETNX) for counters and locks
• MongoDB: document-level locking (sufficient for proctoring writes)
• Cassandra: last-write-wins (acceptable for append-only analytics)
80 Explain the Redis service layer architecture. Redis, service, layer • RedisService base class: connection pooling, key prefix management, serialization
• ABCRCacheService: caches ABCR classification with 5-min TTL, key=hash(query+session)
• SessionCacheService: stores chat history as Redis lists, trimmed to last 20 messages
• WebCacheService: caches web scraping results, key=hash(url)
• All services use connection pool (max 20 connections)
81 How are database connections managed in Docker? connection, Docker, pooling • PostgreSQL: SQLAlchemy connection pool (pool_size=10, max_overflow=20)
• Connection string: postgresql://user:pass@postgres:5432/ensurestudy
• Docker DNS resolves postgres to container IP
• Redis: connection pool via redis-py (max_connections=20)
• Qdrant: HTTP client with connection reuse (keeps TCP connections alive)
82 What is the curriculum.py model file and why is it the largest? curriculum, model, complexity • 996 lines, 16 models — most complex domain in the system
• Models: Curriculum, CurriculumTopic, TopicDependency, DailyGoal, Activity, LearningResource, QuestionBank, SpacedRepetitionCard, RevisionSchedule, etc.
• Complex because curriculum generation involves: dependency graphs, scheduling algorithms, spaced repetition state
• Each model has serialization methods (to_dict) for API responses
83 How does the system handle database schema evolution? schema, evolution, migration • Alembic migration files: each change generates a versioned migration script
• Forward-only in production: down migrations not guaranteed to work
• Column additions always nullable or with defaults (no table locks)
• Large table changes: done in phases (add column → backfill → add constraint)
• Migration tested in staging before production deployment
84 Scenario: You need to add a "favorites" feature. What DB changes? feature, design, scenario • New table: user_favorites(user_id, favoritable_type, favoritable_id, created_at)
• Polymorphic: favoritable_type = 'document', 'topic', 'meeting', etc.
• Index: (user_id, favoritable_type) for "my favorite documents" queries
• Alternative: separate tables per type (simpler queries, more tables)
• Migration: new table creation, no existing data affected
85 How is the Qdrant service API designed? Qdrant, API, service • QdrantService class: wraps qdrant-client with connection management
• Methods: create_collection(), upsert_vectors(), search(), delete_by_filter()
• Search API: accepts query vector, filter conditions, top_k, score threshold
• Returns: list of ScoredPoint with vector, payload, and score
• Error handling: connection retries, collection creation if not exists
86 What is collection architecture in Qdrant and how is it designed? Qdrant, collection, architecture • Separate collections for different content types: documents, notes, meetings, web_content
• Same embedding model (all-mpnet-base-v2) across all collections for consistency
• Collection-level HNSW parameters tuned per use case: documents (m=16, ef=100), meetings (m=8, ef=50)
• Isolation: deleting all documents in one classroom doesn't affect other collections
• Scalability: collections can be independently scaled and backed up
87 How does the embedding process work end-to-end? embedding, pipeline • Input: raw text string (max 512 tokens)
• Model loads: SentenceTransformer('all-mpnet-base-v2') cached as singleton
• Encoding: model.encode(text) returns 768-dim numpy array
• Normalization: L2 normalized for cosine similarity
• Batch processing: model.encode(texts, batch_size=32) for document ingestion
• Output: stored in Qdrant with payload metadata
88 How would you migrate from Qdrant to a different vector database? migration, vector DB • Export: iterate all points using scroll API, dump vectors + payloads to JSON
• Transform: convert payload format to target DB schema
• Load: batch insert into new DB (Pinecone, Weaviate, pgvector)
• Verify: run test queries, compare retrieval results
• Swap: update QdrantService to target DB client (interface stays same)
• Risk: different distance metrics may change retrieval quality
89 What are the Redis cache eviction strategies? Redis, eviction, cache • TTL-based expiry: each cache type has configured TTL (5min, 30min, 1hr)
• Manual invalidation: document re-processing clears related cache entries
• Memory policy: allkeys-lru — evicts least recently used when memory full
• Max memory: configured to 512MB in dev, 2GB in production
• No cache warming: entries populated lazily on first access
90 Scenario: A teacher has 1000 students. How does the database handle this? scale, performance, scenario • Enrollment table: 1000 rows per classroom — standard PostgreSQL performance
• Index on classroom_id ensures fast queries
• Assessment results: 1000 × N assessments — partition by date recommended for history
• Qdrant: documents shared at classroom level, not per-student — no scaling issue
• Bottleneck: concurrent assessment submissions → use database transactions with retry
91 How does the meeting embedding service work? meeting, embedding, RAG • After meeting transcription, transcript is chunked (512 tokens, 50 overlap)
• Each chunk embedded with all-mpnet-base-v2
• Stored in meetings Qdrant collection with payload: {meeting_id, speaker, timestamp_range}
• Enables: "What did Professor X say about topic Y in last week's lecture?"
• Embeddings generated asynchronously after transcription completes
92 What is the notes embedding service? notes, embedding • Student personal notes are embedded for private RAG Search
• Collection: notes with payload: {user_id, note_id, created_at}
• Filter: queries always filtered by user_id — students only search their own notes
• Privacy: each student's notes are in shared collection but isolated by filter
• Triggered when student saves/updates a note
93 How is web content stored and embedded? web, embedding, research • Research agent fetches web pages → extracts text → stores in web_content collection
• Payload: {url, domain, trust_score, fetched_at, classroom_id}
• Trust score calculated from: domain authority, content quality, freshness
• TTL: web content re-fetched after 24 hours (cached in Redis for 1hr)
• Enables web-augmented RAG: internal docs + web sources in answers
94 What query processing happens before vector search? query, preprocessing • Query expansion: original query + LLM-generated alternative phrasings
• Keyword extraction: identify key terms for payload filtering
• Intent classification: determine which collection(s) to search
• Context injection: add conversation history for context-aware queries
• Embedding: query encoded with same model as documents (critical for accuracy)
95 How does vector search scoring and ranking work? scoring, ranking, retrieval • Qdrant returns top-k results with cosine similarity scores (0.0 to 1.0)
• Score threshold: only results above 0.3 are considered relevant
• Re-ranking: results re-scored by combining vector score + keyword overlap + recency
• Final ranking: top 5 chunks selected for LLM context window
• Source attribution: each chunk's document and page tracked for citations
96 How does the response cache improve performance? cache, performance, optimization • Cache key: hash(query + classroom_id + filter_params)
• If exact same query repeated: return cached Qdrant results (skip embedding + search)
• Cache hit rate: ~15-20% for classes with repetitive questions
• TTL: 10 minutes (short because new documents may change results)
• Invalidation: on new document upload, clear all cache entries for that classroom
97 Scenario: You need to support 10 languages. What database changes? i18n, multilingual • Embedding model: switch to multilingual model (e.g., paraphrase-multilingual-mpnet-base-v2)
• Re-embed everything: all documents need re-embedding with new model
• PostgreSQL: add language column to documents, user preferences
• Qdrant payload: add language field for optional language filtering
• UI translations: separate i18n files, not in database
98 What are the performance characteristics of the retrieval pipeline? performance, latency • Embedding generation: ~50ms per query on CPU
• Qdrant search (1M vectors): ~10-20ms
• Post-processing (re-ranking, filtering): ~5ms
• Total retrieval: ~75-100ms
• LLM generation (not RAG): ~2-4s (dominates total response time)
• Ingestion: ~200 chunks/minute including embedding + indexing
99 How would you implement search analytics? analytics, search, improvement • Log every search query with: query text, results returned, user click (if any), session_id
• Store in Cassandra: (classroom_id, date) partition, timestamp clustering
• Metrics: top queries, click-through rate, zero-result queries
• Use zero-result queries to identify knowledge gaps
• Feedback loop: low CTR results identify poor embeddings/chunks for re-processing
100 What scalability considerations exist for the database layer? scalability, future • PostgreSQL: read replicas for reporting/analytics queries, vertical scaling for writes
• Qdrant: horizontal scaling with sharding across nodes (built-in)
• Redis: Redis Cluster for >64GB cache, or Redis Sentinel for HA
• MongoDB: replica set for HA, sharding by session_id for proctoring scale
• Cassandra: add nodes linearly — designed for this (vnodes for balanced distribution)
• Critical: monitor connection pools, query latency P99, disk usage per database

Section 3: Multi-Agent Architecture & Orchestrator (80 Questions)¶

Key Concepts: BaseAgent · Model Context Protocol (MCP) · LangGraph · Orchestrator · Supervisor Pattern · Intent Classification · Agent Selection · Agent Communication · Moderation · Safety Layer · Singletons vs Per-Request

# Question Keywords Answer
101 What is a multi-agent system and why does EnsureStudy use one? multi-agent, why • A system where multiple specialized AI agents collaborate to handle complex tasks
• EnsureStudy uses it because no single agent can handle tutoring + research + assessment + curriculum
• Each agent is an expert: TutorAgent knows pedagogy, ResearchAgent knows web search, etc.
• Agents are independently developed, tested, and updated
102 List all agents in EnsureStudy and their primary responsibilities. agent inventory • Orchestrator: routes requests to appropriate agent based on intent
• TutorAgent: answers academic questions with RAG + conversation context
• ResearchAgent: web search + source synthesis for open-ended queries
• WebEnrichmentAgent: fetches and ranks web content for knowledge base
• CurriculumAgent: generates personalized learning paths from syllabi
• LearningAgent: adapts question difficulty based on performance
• DocumentAgent: processes uploaded files (7-stage pipeline)
• AssessmentAgent: generates assessments (MCQ, short answer)
• QuestionPoolAgent: manages question banks with deduplication
• InterviewQuestionAgent: generates interview-style practice questions
• RevisionAssessmentAgent: creates spaced-repetition review tests
• NotesAgent: processes and embeds student notes
• ModerationAgent: filters harmful/off-topic content
103 What is BaseAgent and what does it provide to all agents? BaseAgent, abstraction • Abstract base class all agents inherit from
• Provides: LLM client initialization, error handling, logging, MCP protocol support
• Standardized interface: async def process(query, context) -> AgentResponse
• Common utilities: token counting, response formatting, retry logic
• Ensures consistent behavior across all 13 agents
104 Explain the Model Context Protocol (MCP) and its purpose. MCP, protocol, context • Protocol that governs what information an agent can access and use
• Prevents agents from leaking data across classroom boundaries
• Each request carries MCP context: {user_id, classroom_id, role, permissions}
• Agent queries are scoped by MCP: RAG search filtered to user's classroom
• Design principle: agents are powerful but constrained by their context window
105 What are the MCP design principles? MCP, principles • Least privilege: agents only access data they need for the current task
• Context isolation: classroom A's data invisible to classroom B's queries
• Auditability: all MCP context decisions logged for review
• Fail-closed: if MCP context missing, agent refuses to process (not default-allow)
• Composability: MCP context flows through agent chains automatically
106 How does MCP handle web content isolation? MCP, web, isolation • Web content fetched by ResearchAgent is tagged with classroom_id
• When embedded in Qdrant, web content carries classroom context in payload
• Retrieval always filtered by classroom — teacher A's web resources don't appear for teacher B
• Trust scores prevent low-quality web content from entering knowledge base
• Web content cache is also classroom-scoped
107 Describe the Orchestrator agent's supervisor pattern. Orchestrator, supervisor • Orchestrator acts as a supervisor (not a participant) — doesn't answer questions itself
• 4-stage pipeline: classify intent → select agent → execute agent → synthesize response
• Maintains OrchestratorState as TypedDict flowing through LangGraph
• If selected agent fails, Orchestrator falls back to TutorAgent (most general)
• Logs all routing decisions for debugging
108 How does the Orchestrator classify user intent? intent, classification • Stage 1: LLM prompt with query + conversation history
• Output: one of {tutor, research, curriculum, assessment, document, general}
• Uses few-shot examples in prompt for consistent classification
• Ambiguous queries default to tutor (safest fallback)
• Classification cached in Redis (5min TTL) to avoid repeated LLM calls for follow-ups
109 How does agent selection work after intent classification? selection, routing • Stage 2 maps intent → agent: tutor → TutorAgent, research → ResearchAgent, etc.
• Some intents trigger multiple agents sequentially: curriculum → CurriculumAgent + LearningAgent
• Selection considers conversation state: if mid-assessment, route to AssessmentAgent regardless
• Hard rules override LLM classification: document upload always → DocumentAgent
110 What is the OrchestratorState TypedDict? state, TypedDict • LangGraph state passed between nodes: {query, intent, selected_agent, agent_response, error, metadata}
• query: original user message
• intent: classified intent string
• selected_agent: agent class reference
• agent_response: final response from selected agent
• metadata: timing, tokens used, cache hits
111 Show the LangGraph workflow definition for the Orchestrator. LangGraph, workflow • Graph: classify_intent → select_agent → execute_agent → synthesize_response → END
• Conditional edge after classify_intent: routes based on intent type
• Error edge: any node failure → handle_error → END with error message
• Compiled with graph.compile() for optimized execution
• StateGraph with OrchestratorState as state schema
112 What is a LangGraph state machine and why is it used for agents? LangGraph, state machine • LangGraph provides directed graph execution where nodes are functions and edges are transitions
• State (TypedDict) flows through nodes, each node can read/modify state
• Benefits: explicit control flow, easy debugging, built-in persistence, retry support
• vs LangChain chains: supports cycles, conditional branching, human-in-the-loop
• Every agent except ModerationAgent uses LangGraph
113 How does agent communication work between agents? communication, inter-agent • Agents don't communicate directly — all routing goes through Orchestrator
• Orchestrator can chain agents: TutorAgent → ResearchAgent for web-augmented answers
• Shared state via Redis: one agent writes to cache, another reads
• Kafka for async: AssessmentAgent → LearningAgent via assessment.completed event
• No peer-to-peer agent communication (supervisor-only pattern)
114 Why use a supervisor pattern instead of peer-to-peer agent communication? supervisor, pattern, trade-off • Centralized control: easier to debug routing decisions
• Security: Orchestrator enforces MCP before delegating
• Observability: single point for logging all agent interactions
• Trade-off: Orchestrator is single point of failure (mitigated by simplicity)
• Alternative considered: CrewAI-style delegation — rejected due to complexity and non-determinism
115 Describe the Agent Tools System. tools, function calling • Agents have access to tools: database queries, web search, embedding generation
• Tools defined as Python functions decorated with @tool
• LLM decides when to call tools based on query analysis
• Tool results injected into agent's context for answer generation
• Examples: search_documents(query, classroom_id), fetch_web_content(url), get_student_performance(user_id)
116 Explain the agent lifecycle and initialization. lifecycle, init, singleton • Agents initialized at FastAPI startup via @app.on_event("startup")
• Stored as singletons: one TutorAgent instance serves all requests
• LLM clients created once, connection pools shared across requests
• LangGraph compiled once at startup, executed per-request with fresh state
• Shutdown: graceful cleanup of LLM connections and cache
117 Why are agents singletons instead of per-request instances? singleton, per-request, trade-off • Singleton pros: avoid repeated LLM client initialization (~200ms), share compiled LangGraph
• Singleton cons: must be thread-safe, shared state risks
• Per-request pros: complete isolation, no shared state bugs
• Decision: singleton with per-request STATE (TypedDict is new each request)
• Agent instance is stateless — all state lives in LangGraph state dict
118 How does the Moderation & Safety Layer work? moderation, safety, filter • ModerationAgent runs BEFORE any other agent processing
• Checks: profanity, hate speech, personal information leakage, off-topic content
• Uses LLM classification with safety-focused prompt
• If flagged: returns rejection message, logs to ModerationLog table
• Severity levels: low (warn), medium (filter), high (block + notify admin)
119 What does the moderation.py agent implementation look like? moderation, implementation • Lightweight agent (not LangGraph — simple classification)
• Input: user query + conversation history
• LLM prompt: "Classify if this query is safe for an educational platform"
• Output: {is_safe: bool, category: str, severity: str, reason: str}
• Runs in ~200ms (fast model, short prompt)
• Integrated as first node in TutorAgent's LangGraph pipeline
120 Scenario: A student asks the tutor agent about something harmful. What happens? safety, scenario • TutorAgent's moderate_query node (Node 1) catches it
• ModerationAgent classifies: {is_safe: false, category: "harmful", severity: "high"}
• LangGraph conditional edge routes to early exit (skip ABCR, RAG, generation)
• Response: "I can't help with that. Let me assist with your coursework instead."
• ModerationLog created in PostgreSQL with user_id, query, classification
• Admin dashboard shows flagged queries for review
# Question Keywords Answer
121 How would you add a new agent to the multi-agent system? extensibility, new agent • Create class extending BaseAgent, define LangGraph state and nodes
• Register in Orchestrator's intent classification prompt (add new intent type)
• Add routing rule in select_agent node
• Create FastAPI router for direct API access
• Write tests with mock LLM responses
• No changes needed to core-service or frontend (agent-agnostic)
122 What is the difference between the Orchestrator and a router? Orchestrator, router • A router simply maps URL → handler (static)
• Orchestrator uses LLM to dynamically classify intent (intelligent routing)
• Orchestrator can chain agents, retry with different agent, add context
• Orchestrator synthesizes response after agent execution
• Orchestrator maintains conversation state for multi-turn routing
123 How does the Orchestrator handle ambiguous queries? ambiguous, routing, fallback • LLM classification returns confidence scores per intent
• If highest confidence < 0.6: default to TutorAgent (most general)
• If two intents close (within 0.1): prefer TutorAgent for safety
• Conversation context helps: if previous turn was assessment, bias toward AssessmentAgent
• User can explicitly switch: "I want to create a curriculum" overrides
124 What happens when an agent fails mid-execution? failure, recovery, error • LangGraph node raises exception → caught by error handler
• State updated: state["error"] = "RAG retrieval failed"
• Conditional edge based on error type: retry (transient) or skip (permanent)
• Orchestrator fallback: if TutorAgent fails, try without RAG context
• User receives degraded response with disclaimer: "I couldn't access course materials"
125 How is agent execution time tracked? timing, performance, monitoring • Each LangGraph node records start_time and end_time in state
• Total agent time = sum of node times
• Logged in structured format: {agent: "tutor", node: "rag_retrieve", ms: 312}
• Slow nodes identified for optimization (target: <5s total)
• Request middleware logs overall response time
126 Explain how the LangGraph state machine handles conditional edges. conditional, edges, LangGraph • graph.add_conditional_edges(source_node, routing_function, edge_map)
• Routing function receives current state, returns string key
• Edge map: {"safe": "context_routing", "unsafe": "reject_response"}
• Enables: moderation pass/fail, RAG available/unavailable, threshold checks
• Multiple conditions can chain: moderate → classify → route → retrieve → generate
127 What is the visual flow of the LangGraph state machine for the Orchestrator? flow, diagram, Orchestrator • START → classify_intent → [intent-based routing] → select_agent → execute_agent → synthesize → END
• Error edges: any node → handle_error → END
• Conditional: classify_intent outputs intent string that determines next path
• Linear for simple queries, branching for complex multi-agent queries
• Graph compiled once, executed with fresh state per request
128 How does the system ensure agents don't hallucinate? hallucination, grounding • RAG retrieval provides factual grounding from uploaded documents
• Source attribution: LLM prompted to cite specific document chunks
• Temperature set low for TutorAgent (0.3) — reduces creative/hallucinatory outputs
• Post-generation check: answer compared against retrieved chunks for consistency
• User can see source documents linked in response for verification
129 What LLM models are used and how are they configured per agent? LLM, models, configuration • Primary: Google Gemini 1.5 Pro — used for TutorAgent, Orchestrator, CurriculumAgent
• Fast: Groq (Llama/Mixtral) — used for classification, moderation (low latency)
• Temperature: 0.1 (assessment) → 0.3 (tutor) → 0.7 (research)
• Max tokens: varies per agent (tutor: 2048, research: 4096, assessment: 1024)
• Configured via environment variables, switchable without code changes
130 Why use Gemini for main agents and Groq for classification? model selection, trade-off • Gemini: better quality, larger context window (1M tokens), good at reasoning
• Groq: 10x faster inference (LPU), ideal for short classification tasks
• Cost optimization: classification uses cheap, fast model; generation uses quality model
• Latency: Groq classification ~100ms vs Gemini ~500ms — saves 400ms in pipeline
• Fallback: if Gemini fails, can fallback to Groq for generation (lower quality)
131 How does conversation history affect agent behavior? history, context, multi-turn • Last 10 messages stored in Redis as list per session
• Conversation history injected into LLM prompt as context
• ABCR uses history to determine if query is a follow-up or new topic
• Orchestrator uses history for intent classification context
• TutorAgent's TAL updates topic anchors based on conversation flow
132 What is the agent response format? response, format, structure • Standardized AgentResponse: {content, sources, metadata, follow_up_suggestions}
• content: markdown-formatted answer text
• sources: list of document references with page numbers
• metadata: agent name, processing time, tokens used, cache hit
• follow_up_suggestions: 2-3 related questions the student might ask next
133 How does streaming work for agent responses? streaming, SSE, tokens • FastAPI StreamingResponse with text/event-stream media type
• LLM generates tokens → each token yielded as SSE event
• Event format: data: {"token": "The", "type": "content"}\n\n
• Special events: {type: "sources"} with document references at end
• Frontend EventSource processes events and renders incrementally
134 What SSE event types exist? SSE, events, protocol • content: incremental text token for response body
• sources: document references appearing after content completes
• thinking: agent "thinking" status (shown as loading indicator)
• error: error message if agent fails mid-stream
• done: signals end of stream, includes metadata summary
• progress: for long tasks (document processing: 0.0 → 1.0)
135 Scenario: Two agents need to collaborate on a single query. How? collaboration, scenario • Orchestrator chains agents: first Research (gather web info) → then Tutor (compose answer)
• State passed between: Research output becomes Tutor's additional context
• Example: "What are the latest papers on transformer architecture?"
• ResearchAgent: searches web, returns summaries
• TutorAgent: combines web results + course RAG context into comprehensive answer
136 How would you implement agent performance evaluation? evaluation, metrics • Quality metrics: user thumbs up/down on responses, click-through on sources
• Latency metrics: P50/P95/P99 response times per agent
• Cost metrics: tokens consumed per agent per day
• Coverage metrics: % of queries answered without fallback/error
• Dashboard: Grafana with per-agent panels
• A/B testing: compare agent v1 vs v2 by routing to both and comparing quality scores
137 What is the token budget management strategy? tokens, cost, budget • Each agent has max_tokens configuration (tutor: 2048, research: 4096)
• Context window management: trim oldest messages if history exceeds limit
• RAG chunks selected to fit within remaining token budget
• Token counting: tiktoken library estimates before LLM call
• Cost tracking: log (agent, model, input_tokens, output_tokens) per request
• Monthly budget alerts at 80% consumption
138 How does the system handle concurrent agent requests? concurrency, async • FastAPI async endpoints: each request runs in its own coroutine
• LLM API calls are async (httpx/aiohttp): non-blocking I/O
• Agent singletons are thread-safe: state is per-request (TypedDict)
• Redis connections pooled: 20 concurrent connections
• Qdrant queries: async HTTP client
• Bottleneck: LLM API rate limits (not our concurrency)
139 What improvements would you make to the agent system? improvement, future • Add memory: persistent conversation memory across sessions (not just in-session)
• Agent self-reflection: agent checks its own answer quality before returning
• Parallel execution: run RAG and web search simultaneously instead of sequentially
• Fine-tuned models: train domain-specific models on EnsureStudy data for better quality
• Agent metrics dashboard: real-time quality/latency/cost per agent
140 Scenario: The Orchestrator misroutes a question to the wrong agent. What happens? misroute, scenario • Example: student asks "help me study for exam" → Orchestrator routes to ResearchAgent instead of CurriculumAgent
• ResearchAgent tries to answer but returns web results instead of study plan
• Impact: suboptimal answer, user may be confused
• Mitigation: user feedback ("this wasn't helpful") logged, used to improve classification prompt
• Fix: add "study/exam" keywords to CurriculumAgent intent examples in classification prompt
# Question Keywords Answer
141 What is the difference between a LangChain chain and a LangGraph graph? LangChain, LangGraph • Chain: linear sequence of steps (prompt → LLM → output parser)
• Graph: directed graph with conditional edges, cycles, shared state
• LangGraph supports: loops (retry), branching (if/else), human-in-the-loop
• LangGraph state is mutable TypedDict — chains pass immutable data
• EnsureStudy chose LangGraph for complex multi-step agent logic with error handling
142 How are LangGraph nodes defined in the codebase? nodes, implementation • Each node is an async Python function: async def node_name(state: AgentState) -> AgentState
• Node reads from state dict, performs work, returns updated state dict
• Example: async def retrieve_with_mcp(state): ... return {**state, "context_chunks": chunks}
• Nodes registered: graph.add_node("retrieve", retrieve_with_mcp)
• Edges: graph.add_edge("retrieve", "generate")
143 How does the system prevent infinite loops in LangGraph? loops, safety, LangGraph • Max iteration count per graph execution (default: 10)
• Timeout: 120 seconds total per agent invocation
• Conditional edges have explicit terminal states (always converge to END)
• No cycles in current graph designs (all directed acyclic)
• Future: if adding retry loops, include iteration counter in state
144 What is the agent capability matrix? capability, matrix • Documents which agent can do what:
• TutorAgent: RAG ✓, Web ✗, Assessment ✗, Curriculum ✗
• ResearchAgent: RAG ✗, Web ✓, Assessment ✗, Curriculum ✗
• CurriculumAgent: RAG ✗, Web ✗, Assessment ✗, Curriculum ✓
• Helps developers understand agent scope and prevents feature duplication
145 How does the system handle model API key rotation? API keys, rotation, security • Keys stored in environment variables, never in code
• Docker Compose reads from .env file
• Rotation: update .env, restart affected service container
• Multiple keys: load-balance across keys to distribute rate limits
• Secret management: in production, use AWS Secrets Manager or HashiCorp Vault
146 What is prompt engineering methodology for agent prompts? prompt, engineering • System prompt: defines agent role, behavior guidelines, output format
• Few-shot examples: 3-5 examples per classification task
• Chain-of-thought: TutorAgent prompted to "think step by step"
• Structured output: JSON schema enforcement for classification responses
• Temperature + top_p tuning per agent for consistency/creativity balance
• Prompts version-controlled in constants file
147 How are agent responses formatted for the frontend? response, frontend, format • Markdown formatting: headers, lists, code blocks, bold/italic
• LaTeX: math expressions wrapped in $...$ for MathJax rendering
• Code blocks: language-tagged for syntax highlighting
• Source citations: [1] references linked to document names
• Follow-up suggestions: rendered as clickable buttons in chat UI
148 Scenario: You need to add a "summarize lecture" feature. Which agent handles it? feature, routing, scenario • Could be: TutorAgent (if lecture is an uploaded document) or MeetingAgent (if from recorded meeting)
• Orchestrator classification: "summarize" intent → check if referring to document or meeting
• If document: TutorAgent retrieves full document chunks → LLM summarizes
• If meeting: fetch MeetingSummary from DB (already generated by Gemini)
• If ambiguous: ask user "Which lecture? (Document or recorded meeting)"
149 How does the system handle multi-language support in agent responses? i18n, multilingual • Currently English-only — LLM prompts and responses in English
• To add: detect language from user query → set response language in system prompt
• Gemini supports 100+ languages natively — no model change needed
• RAG retrieval: multilingual embedding model needed for non-English documents
• Moderation: need language-specific safety rules
150 What is the agent testing strategy? testing, agents • Unit tests: mock LLM responses, verify node logic independently
• Integration tests: run full LangGraph with mock LLM, verify state flow
• Prompt tests: regression tests — fixed inputs → expected output patterns
• Load tests: concurrent agent invocations with mock responses
• Manual evaluation: human review of agent responses for quality (monthly)
151 How does error propagation work through the LangGraph pipeline? error, propagation • Each node catches exceptions and writes to state["error"]
• Conditional edge checks state["error"] after each node
• Error routing: classification error → use default agent; RAG error → skip RAG; LLM error → retry once
• Fatal errors: skip to END node with error response to user
• All errors logged with node name, error type, stack trace, state snapshot
152 What is the Orchestrator's response synthesis stage? synthesis, response • Stage 4: receives raw agent response → formats for user
• Adds: source attributions, follow-up suggestions, metadata
• Handles: streaming assembly (tokens → paragraphs → sources → done event)
• Strips: internal metadata, debug info, tool call logs
• Ensures: response is safe (post-generation moderation check)
153 How would you implement agent observability? observability, tracing • LangSmith or Arize Phoenix for LLM tracing
• Trace each LangGraph execution: node inputs/outputs, LLM prompts/completions
• Structured logs: JSON with {request_id, agent, node, duration_ms, tokens}
• Metrics: Prometheus counters for agent invocations, errors, cache hits
• Dashboards: per-agent latency P50/P99, error rate, token usage trends
154 What is the difference between ModerationAgent and content moderation? moderation, difference • ModerationAgent: LLM-based, classifies user QUERIES for safety before processing
• Content moderation (Sec 22): broader system — includes file upload scanning, response filtering
• ModerationAgent is fast (pre-processing check), content moderation is comprehensive (multi-layer)
• ModerationAgent handles: profanity, off-topic, harmful intent
• Content moderation adds: PII detection, copyright, bias in generated responses
155 How does the system handle conversation state across multiple agents? state, conversation, multi-agent • Redis stores conversation history keyed by session_id
• Each agent reads the SAME conversation history from Redis
• When Orchestrator switches agent mid-conversation, new agent has full context
• Example: user asks tutor question → switches to curriculum → curriculum agent sees tutor Q&A
• Conversation trimmed to last 20 messages to fit context window
156 What is the startup model preloading process? startup, preloading • FastAPI @app.on_event("startup"): loads embedding model (all-mpnet-base-v2)
• Model loaded once → stored as global singleton → shared across all requests
• Agent LLM clients initialized: Gemini API client, Groq API client
• LangGraph graphs compiled for all agents
• Total startup time: ~10-15 seconds (dominated by embedding model loading)
157 Scenario: Agent response takes too long (>30 seconds). How do you debug? performance, debug, scenario • Check logs: which node is slow? (timing per node in structured logs)
• Common causes: LLM API slow (check provider status), large context (reduce chunks), Qdrant slow (check collection size)
• Quick fixes: reduce max_tokens, limit RAG chunks to 3, skip web enrichment
• Long-term: add caching, use faster model for simple queries, pre-compute embeddings
158 How do agents handle tool call failures? tools, failure, handling • Tool wrapped in try/except: try: result = search_docs(query) except: result = None
• If tool fails: agent notes it in state, generates response without that tool's output
• Example: web search tool fails → agent responds with "I couldn't search the web, here's what I know from your course materials"
• No retry by default (tool failures usually indicate external service issue)
159 What is the event-driven triggering pattern for agents? event, Kafka, trigger • Some agents triggered by Kafka events, not HTTP requests
• Pattern: Assessment submitted → Kafka assessment.completed → LearningAgent consumes → generates new questions
• Decouples: assessment flow doesn't wait for question generation
• Error handling: failed processing → event re-queued for retry
• Kafka consumer is a background asyncio task in FastAPI
160 How does agent versioning work? versioning, deployment • Agent class has VERSION attribute for tracking
• No explicit versioning system yet — agents updated by redeploying ai-service
• Recommended: agent config in database, version checked at startup
• A/B testing: deploy both v1 and v2, route based on user group
• Rollback: revert Docker image to previous tag, restart container
161 What design patterns are used specifically in the agent system? patterns, agents • State Machine (LangGraph): explicit state transitions for predictable behavior
• Supervisor (Orchestrator): centralized routing and error management
• Strategy (per-agent LLM config): different models/params per task
• Template Method (BaseAgent): define skeleton, subclasses fill in details
• Chain of Responsibility (moderation → classify → route → execute): each node handles its concern
162 How does the system handle prompt injection attacks? prompt injection, security • ModerationAgent: trained to detect prompt injection patterns ("ignore previous instructions")
• Input sanitization: strip known injection patterns before LLM call
• System prompt defensive framing: "You are a tutor. Only answer educational questions."
• Output post-check: verify response doesn't contain system prompt leakage
• Logging: all suspected injections logged for security review
163 What is the maximum number of concurrent agent executions? concurrency, limits • Limited by: LLM API rate limits (primary bottleneck), Redis connection pool (20), Qdrant client pool
• FastAPI handles concurrent requests natively (async)
• Practical limit: ~50 concurrent agent executions per instance
• Beyond that: queue requests, scale horizontally with multiple ai-service instances
• Token budget: ~$0.005 per tutor request × 50 concurrent = manageable
164 Scenario: An agent produces a response in a language different from the query. How do you fix? language, bug, scenario • Root cause: conversation history contains mixed-language messages confusing the LLM
• Fix: add explicit instruction in system prompt: "Always respond in the same language as the user's query"
• Prevention: language detection on user query → inject respond_in_language: "en" into state
• Edge case: multilingual classrooms need per-user language preference
165 How would you implement agent memory that persists across sessions? memory, persistence • Currently: conversation history in Redis (session-scoped, expires in 30min)
• Enhancement: store conversation summaries in PostgreSQL per user
• On new session: load user's topic preferences, past difficulties, learning style
• Vector memory: embed past conversations → retrieve relevant history for context
• Implementation: new LangGraph node load_user_memory before main processing
166 What is the role of the services layer in agent architecture? services, layer, separation • 89 service files in app/services/ — business logic separated from agent routing
• Agents call services: ABCRService, MCPContextService, QdrantService, EmbeddingService
• Services are reusable: both TutorAgent and CurriculumAgent use QdrantService
• Services handle: external API calls, database queries, caching, data transformation
• Clean architecture: Agent (orchestration) → Service (logic) → Repository (data)
167 How are agent prompts managed and versioned? prompts, management • System prompts stored as Python constants in dedicated prompt files
• Format: f-strings with template variables for runtime injection
• Version control: prompts tracked in Git with agent code
• No separate prompt management system (considered LangFuse but not implemented)
• Change process: update prompt constant → test → deploy
168 What happens during graceful shutdown of the agent system? shutdown, cleanup • FastAPI @app.on_event("shutdown"): cleanup hook
• In-flight requests: allowed to complete (30s grace period)
• Redis connections: returned to pool, pool closed
• LLM clients: HTTP connections closed
• Kafka consumers: commit offsets, stop consuming
• Embedding model: Python GC handles memory cleanup
169 How would you implement an agent debugging/replay system? debugging, replay • Log full LangGraph state at each node transition
• Store in database: {request_id, agent, node_name, state_snapshot, timestamp}
• Replay: load state at any node → re-run remaining pipeline with same inputs
• UI: timeline view showing node execution, state changes, LLM inputs/outputs
• Implementation: LangGraph callbacks for state persistence
170 How does the agent system handle token context window limits? context window, limits • Gemini 1.5 Pro: 1M token context window (generous but not infinite)
• Practical limit: keep prompts under 8K tokens for reasonable latency/cost
• Strategy: conversation history trimmed to last 20 messages (~4K tokens)
• RAG chunks: top 5 chunks × ~200 tokens = ~1K tokens
• System prompt + few-shot examples: ~1K tokens
• Total: ~6K tokens per request (well within limits)
171 Scenario: You need to add voice input to the agent. What changes? voice, feature, scenario • Frontend: add Web Speech API or Whisper.js for speech-to-text
• New endpoint: /ai/speech/transcribe using Whisper model
• Transcribed text → send to Orchestrator as normal query
• No agent changes needed — agents receive text regardless of input method
• Optional: voice output using TTS API for accessibility
172 Why did the team build custom agents instead of using AutoGen or CrewAI? framework choice, trade-off • Control: LangGraph gives fine-grained control over each processing step
• Performance: custom agents optimized for education domain, no framework overhead
• Debuggability: explicit state machine easier to debug than autonomous agent frameworks
• MCP integration: custom protocol wouldn't fit cleanly into AutoGen's conversation pattern
• Trade-off: more code to maintain, but exactly matches requirements
173 How does the system ensure deterministic behavior for assessments? determinism, assessment • AssessmentAgent: temperature=0.1, top_p=0.5 — near-deterministic LLM output
• Seed parameter: passed to LLM for reproducible generation
• Question validation: post-generation check for correctness (verify MCQ has one correct answer)
• Deduplication: hash-based checking against existing question pool
• Same seed + same input → should produce same questions (for audit)
174 How is the agent interconnection designed? interconnection, event flow • Direct: Orchestrator → any agent (HTTP within process)
• Event-driven: AssessmentAgent → Kafka → LearningAgent
• Shared data: all agents access same Qdrant, Redis, PostgreSQL
• No direct agent-to-agent calls — always mediated by Orchestrator or Kafka
• Diagram: agents as nodes, Kafka/Orchestrator as edges
175 What is the agent response caching strategy? caching, response • Similar queries to same agent: check Redis cache before LLM call
• Cache key: hash(agent_name + query + classroom_id + context_hash)
• Hit rate: ~15-20% for tutoring (students ask similar questions)
• TTL: 10 minutes (short because context may change)
• Miss penalty: ~3-5s (full LLM generation)
• Hit benefit: <50ms response
176 How would you implement agent self-evaluation? self-evaluation, quality • After generating response, add "critic" node in LangGraph
• Critic prompt: "Rate this response 1-5 for accuracy, completeness, clarity"
• If score < 3: regenerate with revised prompt
• Trade-off: adds ~1s latency and doubles token cost
• Alternative: periodic batch evaluation by human reviewers
177 What is the role of the embedding service in the agent pipeline? embedding, service • EmbeddingService: wraps SentenceTransformer model
• Used by: TutorAgent (query embedding), DocumentAgent (chunk embedding), NotesAgent, MeetingAgent
• Singleton: model loaded once, shared across all services
• Batch support: embed_batch(texts, batch_size=32) for ingestion
• Thread-safe: model.encode is CPU-bound but thread-safe in PyTorch
178 How does the system handle agent configuration per classroom? config, classroom, customization • Currently: agents have global configuration (same behavior for all classrooms)
• Enhancement: per-classroom agent config in PostgreSQL
• Example: temperature per classroom, allowed tools per classroom, RAG scope per classroom
• MCP context already carries classroom_id — could load classroom-specific config in first node
• Use case: strict classroom (low temperature) vs creative classroom (high temperature)
179 What is the agent request lifecycle end-to-end? lifecycle, request, e2e • 1. User types message → Frontend POST /ai/tutor/chat with JWT
• 2. FastAPI middleware: log request, extract user from JWT
• 3. Orchestrator: classify → select → execute agent
• 4. Agent: moderate → context_route → RAG → generate (LangGraph pipeline)
• 5. LLM streams tokens → FastAPI SSE → Frontend EventSource
• 6. Frontend renders markdown incrementally + source links
• 7. Conversation stored in Redis for next turn
• Total: ~3-5 seconds end-to-end
180 How would you migrate from LangGraph to a different framework? migration, framework • LangGraph abstractions are thin: nodes are Python functions, state is TypedDict
• To migrate: keep node functions, replace graph definition with new framework
• Work needed: rewrite conditional edges, state management, compilation
• Risk: different state propagation semantics could introduce bugs
• Recommendation: only migrate if LangGraph has fundamental limitations for your use case

Section 4: Tutor Agent — ABCR, TAL, RAG & Sessions (80 Questions)¶

Key Concepts: TutorAgent · LangGraph 4-Node Pipeline · ABCR (Attention-Based Context Routing) · TAL (Topic Anchor Layer) · MCP Retrieval · Answer Generation · Session Management · Prompt Construction · Source Attribution

# Question Keywords Answer
