181 What is the TutorAgent and what makes it the most important agent? TutorAgent, overview • Primary student-facing agent handling academic Q&A
• 687 lines of code — second largest agent
• Combines: content moderation, context routing, RAG retrieval, LLM generation
• Handles ~70% of all student queries (default fallback from Orchestrator)
• Maintains conversation context across turns for coherent tutoring
182 Describe the TutorAgent's 4-node LangGraph pipeline. pipeline, LangGraph, nodes • Node 1: moderate_query — safety check via ModerationAgent
• Node 2: context_routing — ABCR classification + TAL topic anchoring
• Node 3: retrieve_with_mcp — RAG retrieval scoped by MCP context
• Node 4: generate_answer — LLM response with source attribution
• Conditional edge after Node 1: unsafe → reject; safe → continue
183 What is TutorState and what fields does it contain? TutorState, TypedDict • query: original user question
• session_id: conversation session identifier
• mcp_context: classroom_id, user_id, permissions
• is_safe: boolean from moderation check
• abcr_decision: context routing classification
• topic_anchors: current conversation topic state
• context_chunks: retrieved RAG documents
• response: final generated answer
• sources: document references for attribution
• error: error state for conditional routing
184 How does Node 1 (moderate_query) work? moderation, Node 1 • Calls ModerationAgent with user query + last 3 conversation turns
• ModerationAgent classifies: safe/unsafe with category and severity
• If safe: state["is_safe"] = True → proceed to Node 2
• If unsafe: state["is_safe"] = False → conditional edge skips to rejection response
• Latency: ~200ms (fast Groq model for classification)
• Design decision: moderation FIRST to avoid wasting compute on harmful queries
185 What is ABCR (Attention-Based Context Routing) and why is it needed? ABCR, routing, context • ABCR determines HOW to answer: use RAG docs, conversation history, or general knowledge
• Without ABCR: every query would trigger full RAG pipeline (wasteful for "hello" or "thanks")
• Classification categories: document_query, follow_up, general_chat, off_topic
• Benefits: saves ~40% of RAG calls, reduces latency for simple queries
• Source: abcr_service.py (16,852 bytes)
186 Describe the ABCR decision flowchart. ABCR, flowchart, decision • Input: query + conversation history + available document IDs
• Step 1: Is query a follow-up to previous answer? → if yes, use conversation context
• Step 2: Does query mention specific document/topic? → if yes, search that document
• Step 3: Is query educational? → if yes, full RAG retrieval
• Step 4: Is query general chat? → respond without RAG (save resources)
• Output: ABCRDecision(route, confidence, reasoning)
187 What classification signals does ABCR use? ABCR, signals, classification • Lexical signals: query keywords matching document titles/topics
• Contextual signals: conversation history — recent topics, follow-up patterns
• Semantic signals: embedding similarity between query and document descriptions
• Temporal signals: recency of conversation messages (follow-up likely if <2min gap)
• Signals combined via LLM prompt, not hard-coded rules
188 What is the ABCR decision output format? ABCR, output • ABCRDecision(route: str, confidence: float, reasoning: str, document_filter: Optional[str])
• route: one of rag_full, rag_scoped, conversation_only, general
• confidence: 0.0-1.0, used for fallback logic
• reasoning: human-readable explanation (logged for debugging)
• document_filter: specific document_id if query targets one document
189 How does ABCR integrate into the context_routing node? ABCR, integration, Node 2 • Node 2 calls ABCRService.classify(query, history, mcp_context)
• Result stored in state["abcr_decision"]
• Conditional routing after Node 2: rag_full → full retrieval, general → skip RAG
• Also calls TAL (Topic Anchor Layer) in same node — dual classification
• Total Node 2 latency: ~500ms (ABCR ~300ms + TAL ~200ms)
190 What are ABCR performance characteristics? ABCR, performance • Cache hit rate: ~25% (repeated similar queries within 5 minutes)
• Classification accuracy: ~90% (validated against human labels)
• Latency: ~300ms uncached, <10ms cached
• Token usage: ~200 tokens per classification call
• Cost: ~$0.001 per classification (Groq, very cheap)
• Most impactful optimization: prevents unnecessary RAG calls
191 What is TAL (Topic Anchor Layer) and its purpose? TAL, topic, anchor • Tracks the conversation's current topic across multiple turns
• Without TAL: each query treated independently, losing topic continuity
• Example: Q1="Explain photosynthesis" → anchor=photosynthesis. Q2="Why is it important?" → TAL knows "it" = photosynthesis
• Enables: pronoun resolution, topic-scoped RAG, coherent multi-turn tutoring
• Source: topic_anchor_service.py (16,248 bytes)
192 What is an anchor structure in TAL? anchor, structure, TAL • TopicAnchor(topic: str, subtopics: List[str], confidence: float, established_at: int)
• topic: main topic string ("machine learning")
• subtopics: related subtopics mentioned in conversation ("gradient descent", "neural networks")
• confidence: how strongly this is the current topic (decays over turns)
• established_at: conversation turn number when anchor was set
193 How does TAL handle topic shifts in conversation? TAL, topic shift • Each turn: TAL analyzes if query continues current topic or introduces new one
• Same topic: confidence maintained or increased, subtopics may expand
• New topic: old anchor demoted (confidence reduced), new anchor created
• Gradual shift: if query bridges two topics, both anchors active temporarily
• Example: "photosynthesis" → "chloroplasts" (related, same anchor expanded) → "French Revolution" (new anchor)
194 How does keyword extraction work in TAL? keywords, extraction, TAL • LLM extracts key terms from user query: nouns, technical terms, named entities
• Keywords compared against existing anchors' subtopics for overlap
• High overlap → same topic, low overlap → potential topic shift
• Keywords also used as search terms for Qdrant payload filtering
• Extraction prompt: "Extract 3-5 key academic terms from this query"
195 How do TAL operations update the anchor state? TAL, operations, update • anchor_query(query): analyze query against current anchors
• update_anchor(topic, subtopics): create or update anchor with new terms
• decay_anchors(): reduce confidence of inactive anchors (called each turn)
• get_active_anchor(): return highest-confidence anchor for RAG scoping
• clear_anchors(): reset on new session or explicit topic change
196 How does Node 3 (retrieve_with_mcp) work? RAG, MCP, retrieval, Node 3 • Two-phase retrieval process:
• Phase 1: embed query → search Qdrant with MCP filter (classroom_id)
• Phase 2: apply ABCR scope — if rag_scoped, filter by specific document_id
• MCP ensures: only documents from student's classroom returned
• Result: top 5 relevant chunks with similarity scores
• Stored in state["context_chunks"] for generation node
197 What are the MCP filtering rules during retrieval? MCP, filtering, rules • Must filter: classroom_id — never return documents from other classrooms
• Optional filter: document_id — if ABCR scoped to specific document
• Score threshold: chunks below 0.3 cosine similarity excluded
• Recency boost: recently uploaded documents get 10% score boost
• Diversity: maximum 2 chunks from same document (prevent single-source dominance)
198 What is anchor-boosted retrieval? anchor, boosted, retrieval • TAL's current topic anchor used to enhance retrieval query
• Original query augmented with anchor keywords: query + " " + anchor.topic + " " + " ".join(anchor.subtopics)
• Boosts relevance: "Why is it important?" becomes "Why is photosynthesis chloroplasts important?"
• Only applied when anchor confidence > 0.7 (avoid noise from stale anchors)
• Improves retrieval precision by ~15% in internal testing
199 How does Node 4 (generate_answer) construct the LLM prompt? generation, prompt, Node 4 • System prompt: tutor persona, educational guidelines, output format
• Context section: retrieved RAG chunks (numbered for citation)
• Conversation history: last 10 messages for multi-turn coherence
• User query: current question
• Instructions: "cite sources as [1], [2]", "explain step-by-step", "ask follow-up if unclear"
• Temperature: 0.3 for factual accuracy
200 How does source attribution work in generated answers? sources, attribution, citation • RAG chunks injected with labels: [Source 1: Chapter 3, Page 42], [Source 2: Lecture Notes]
• LLM prompted to cite sources inline: "According to [1], photosynthesis..."
• Post-processing: extract [N] references, map to document metadata
• Response includes sources array: [{doc_name, page, chunk_text_preview}]
• Frontend renders: clickable source links that open the original document
# Question Keywords Answer
201 What learning enhancement features does the TutorAgent add? learning, enhancement • Follow-up suggestions: "Want me to explain X further?" based on topic complexity
• Socratic mode: asks questions back to student instead of giving direct answers
• Difficulty adaptation: simpler explanations for struggling students (based on LearningAgent data)
• Related topics: suggests adjacent topics from curriculum
• Visual aids: includes ASCII diagrams, code examples where appropriate
202 How does the session management system work? session, management • In-memory session store using Redis (session:{session_id} key)
• Session created: on first message, UUID generated, stored with user_id + classroom_id
• Session timeout: 30 minutes of inactivity → session cleared
• Session data: conversation history (list), topic anchors (hash), ABCR cache (string)
203 What is session intelligence? session, intelligence • Tracks conversation patterns within a session: topics covered, questions asked, difficulty level
• Informs agent behavior: if student asking many follow-ups on same topic → suggest simpler explanation
• Session summary generated on close: "Student studied photosynthesis. Struggled with light reactions."
• Summary stored for LearningAgent to analyze performance trends
204 How does the session cache improve performance? session cache, performance • ABCR classification cached per session: similar queries skip reclassification
• TAL anchors persisted in session: no recalculation on each turn
• Last retrieval results cached: follow-up questions reuse chunks from previous turn
• Cache hit saves: ~800ms per request (skip ABCR 300ms + embedding 50ms + Qdrant 200ms + TAL 200ms)
• Cache invalidated on topic shift (TAL detects new anchor)
205 What is the TutorAgent class interface? interface, API, class • async def process(query, session_id, mcp_context) -> StreamingResponse
• async def get_session_history(session_id) -> List[Message]
• async def clear_session(session_id) -> None
• async def get_agent_info() -> AgentInfo (version, capabilities)
• Internal: _build_graph() (LangGraph compilation), _format_response() (markdown)
206 How does the ABCR cache layer work? ABCR, cache • Key: abcr:{session_id}:{hash(query[:100])}
• Value: serialized ABCRDecision object
• TTL: 5 minutes (matches session activity window)
• Lookup: before calling ABCR LLM, check Redis cache
• Hit rate: ~25% within sessions (follow-up queries often have similar routing)
• Saves ~$0.001 per hit (negligible per query, significant at scale)
207 Describe the end-to-end request flow for a tutor query. e2e, flow, request • 1. User: "Explain binary search" → POST /ai/tutor/chat
• 2. Moderate: safe ✓ (200ms)
• 3. ABCR: rag_full — educational query needs document context (300ms)
• 4. TAL: anchor = "binary search, algorithms" (200ms)
• 5. RAG: embed query → Qdrant search → 5 chunks from data structures PDF (300ms)
• 6. Generate: Gemini with chunks + history → streaming markdown (2-3s)
• 7. Stream: tokens via SSE → frontend renders live
• Total: ~3-4 seconds
208 Scenario: Student asks "Can you explain what we discussed last class?" scenario, session, RAG • ABCR classifies: rag_scoped — needs specific context
• TAL: no specific topic anchor → query is vague
• Challenge: "last class" is temporal reference, not keyword-searchable
• Solution: check if meeting recordings exist → fetch last MeetingSummary
• If no recording: retrieve most recently uploaded document for this classroom
• Response: summarize recent content with disclaimer "Based on your latest materials..."
209 How does the TutorAgent handle code-related questions? code, programming • ABCR classifies as educational → full RAG retrieval
• RAG may return code examples from uploaded materials
• LLM prompted to include: code blocks with language tags, step-by-step explanation, time/space complexity
• Post-processing: ensure code is properly formatted (triple backticks, syntax highlighting)
• Example: "How does quicksort work?" → algorithm explanation + Python implementation + complexity analysis
210 How does the TutorAgent handle math questions? math, LaTeX • LLM outputs LaTeX notation: $\frac{-b \pm \sqrt{b^2-4ac}}{2a}$
• Frontend renders LaTeX via MathJax or KaTeX
• Multi-step solutions: numbered steps with equations
• RAG may provide relevant formulas from uploaded textbook chapters
• System prompt includes: "Use LaTeX for mathematical expressions"
211 What LLM configuration does the TutorAgent use? LLM, config, tutor • Model: Google Gemini 1.5 Pro (main generation)
• Temperature: 0.3 (factual, low creativity)
• Max output tokens: 2048
• Top-p: 0.8 (focused sampling)
• Stop sequences: none (let model complete naturally)
• System prompt: ~500 tokens defining tutor persona and behavior
212 How does the Tutor handle "I don't understand" responses? pedagogy, adaptation • TAL maintains current topic anchor → knows what was being explained
• Re-explanation strategy: simpler words, more examples, analogies
• LLM prompt includes: "The student didn't understand. Re-explain at a simpler level."
• May suggest: "Would you like me to break this into smaller steps?"
• After 3 failed explanations: suggest related resources or notify teacher
213 Scenario: RAG returns no relevant chunks for a query. What happens? no results, fallback, scenario • Score threshold: if all chunks score < 0.3, treated as "no results"
• Agent behavior: answers from general knowledge (LLM without RAG context)
• Disclaimer added: "I don't have specific course materials on this, but here's what I know..."
• Suggests: "Your teacher might have more specific materials. Try uploading relevant documents."
• Logged: query marked as "no RAG match" for teacher visibility
214 How does the TutorAgent handle multi-turn conversations? multi-turn, history • Redis stores last 20 messages per session as list
• Each turn: RPUSH chat:{session_id} {serialized_message}
• Trimming: LTRIM chat:{session_id} -20 -1 keeps only last 20
• History injected into LLM prompt: [{"role": "user", "content": "..."}, {"role": "assistant", "content": "..."}]
• Enable: pronoun resolution, context carryover, coherent conversation flow
215 What happens when conversation history exceeds the token limit? token limit, history, truncation • Token counting: tiktoken estimates history token count
• Budget: ~4000 tokens allocated for history (out of ~8000 total prompt)
• Strategy 1: remove oldest messages first
• Strategy 2: keep first message (sets context) + last 10 messages
• Strategy 3: summarize old messages into single paragraph (more expensive but preserves context)
• Current implementation: Strategy 1 (simplest)
216 How does the TutorAgent's Socratic mode work? Socratic, pedagogy • Triggered by: teacher configuration or specific prompt keywords
• Instead of direct answers: agent asks guiding questions
• Example: "What is O(n log n)?" → "What do you think happens when we split a problem in half each time?"
• LLM system prompt: "Do not give direct answers. Guide through questions."
• Limited to 3 Socratic turns → then reveals answer if student still struggling
217 How does the system handle concurrent tutoring sessions? concurrent, sessions • Each session has unique session_id → Redis keys are isolated
• Agent singleton processes requests concurrently (async)
• No shared mutable state between sessions
• LangGraph state is per-request (new TypedDict each invocation)
• Bottleneck: LLM API concurrency limit, not our architecture
218 How would you implement tutor response quality scoring? quality, scoring • Automatic: check if response cites retrieved sources (grounded=good)
• Automatic: length check (too short=incomplete, too long=verbose)
• User feedback: thumbs up/down after each response
• Periodic: sample responses for human evaluation (accuracy, helpfulness)
• Metrics: groundedness score, citation rate, user satisfaction rate
219 What is the difference between TutorAgent and ResearchAgent? comparison, agents • TutorAgent: answers from uploaded course documents (RAG), educational tone, Socratic mode
• ResearchAgent: searches the web, synthesizes multiple sources, informational tone
• Scope: Tutor is classroom-scoped (MCP), Research is web-wide
• Trust: Tutor cites verified course materials, Research cites web (with trust scores)
• Use case: Tutor for coursework, Research for general knowledge
220 How does the TutorAgent handle questions outside the course scope? out of scope, handling • ABCR classifies as off_topic if query doesn't match any document topics
• Response: "This doesn't seem related to your current course. I can help with [list course topics]."
• If borderline: answer briefly, then redirect to course content
• Configurable: teacher can set strict mode (reject off-topic) or lenient mode (answer anything)
• Off-topic queries logged for teacher review
# Question Keywords Answer
221 Scenario: Two students ask identical questions 5 min apart. What's the flow? caching, scenario • Student A: full pipeline — moderate → ABCR → TAL → RAG → generate (4s)
• Student B (5 min later, same classroom): moderate → ABCR cache hit → same RAG chunks still cached → generate (2s)
• If student B is in DIFFERENT classroom: full pipeline (MCP prevents cross-classroom cache sharing)
• Response may differ slightly due to different conversation histories
• ABCR cache hits reduce latency by ~40%
222 How does the TutorAgent handle document-specific questions? document, specific • ABCR classifies as rag_scoped with document_filter = "doc_123"
• RAG search filtered to that specific document in Qdrant
• Example: "What does Chapter 3 of the textbook say about DNA replication?"
• TAL extracts: anchor="DNA replication", subtopic="Chapter 3"
• More precise retrieval: only chunks from specified document
223 How is the TutorAgent prompt constructed end-to-end? prompt, construction • System prompt (~500 tokens): "You are an educational AI tutor. You assist students with their coursework..."
• RAG context (~1000 tokens): "Here are relevant excerpts from course materials: [1] ... [2] ..."
• Conversation history (~4000 tokens): last 20 messages
• User query (~100 tokens): current question
• Instructions (~200 tokens): "Cite sources, be accurate, suggest follow-ups"
• Total: ~5800 tokens per request
224 How does the TutorAgent handle image-based questions? multimodal, images • Currently: text-only input/output
• Enhancement: Gemini supports multimodal — could process uploaded images
• Use case: student photographs whiteboard, asks "Explain this diagram"
• Implementation: image sent as base64 in API request, passed to Gemini Pro Vision
• Challenges: MCP context needed (which classroom), image moderation
225 What error states can occur in each TutorAgent node? errors, states, nodes • Node 1 (moderate): LLM API failure → assume safe, proceed with warning
• Node 2 (ABCR/TAL): classification failure → default to rag_full
• Node 3 (RAG): Qdrant unavailable → skip RAG, answer from general knowledge
• Node 4 (generate): LLM timeout → retry once with smaller context
• Each error state has explicit handling — no silent failures
226 How would you add citation verification to the TutorAgent? citation, verification • After generation: extract cited source numbers [1], [2] from response
• Verify: does the claim actually appear in the cited chunk?
• Method: embed claim + embed chunk → cosine similarity > 0.7 = verified
• Unverified citations: remove or mark as "unverified"
• Trade-off: adds ~500ms latency and doubles embedding compute
227 How does the TutorAgent handle ambiguous pronouns? pronouns, resolution • TAL provides topic context: "it" → current anchor topic
• Conversation history provides entity references: "she" → person mentioned earlier
• LLM naturally handles pronouns when given sufficient context
• Problem case: ambiguous across topics → TAL picks highest-confidence anchor
• Example: "Explain it in detail" → TAL anchor = "linked lists" → resolved
228 What is the ABCR service architecture? ABCR, service, architecture • ABCRService class with singleton pattern
• Methods: classify(query, history, context), clear_cache(session_id)
• Internal: LLM prompt template, cache layer, confidence threshold
• Dependencies: Groq LLM client, Redis client
• 16,852 bytes — handles classification logic, caching, fallback
229 How does the system handle conflicting information between documents? conflict, documents, handling • RAG may retrieve chunks from different documents with contradictory info
• LLM prompted: "If sources conflict, acknowledge the difference and explain both perspectives"
• Response example: "According to [1], X is true. However, [2] suggests Y. The key difference is..."
• Teacher responsibility: ensure uploaded documents are consistent
• Enhancement: flag conflicting chunks automatically via embedding similarity
230 Scenario: The ABCR cache fills up and starts evicting entries. What's the impact? cache eviction, scenario • Redis allkeys-lru policy evicts oldest entries when memory full
• Impact: more ABCR classifications go to LLM (increased latency ~300ms per miss)
• Cost impact: more Groq API calls (~$0.001 per call — minimal)
• Resolution: increase Redis memory limit or reduce TTL
• Alert: monitor cache hit rate — if drops below 10%, investigate
231 How does the topic anchor service manage multiple active anchors? TAL, multiple anchors • Max 3 active anchors at any time (current topic + 2 recent topics)
• Sorted by confidence score — highest confidence = primary anchor
• Old anchors decay: confidence reduced by 0.1 per turn without reinforcement
• Anchor dropped when confidence < 0.2 (effectively forgotten)
• Use case: student switches between two topics — both anchors maintained
232 How does the TutorAgent handle requests for summaries? summary, request • ABCR classifies summary requests as rag_full (need document context)
• RAG retrieves all chunks for the specified scope (document, chapter, topic)
• LLM prompt: "Summarize the following content in [number] bullet points"
• Challenge: large documents may need multiple RAG queries to cover fully
• Solution: retrieve top-k by topic, not just similarity — ensures coverage
233 What is the MCP context service and how does it work? MCP, context service • MCPContextService: builds and validates MCP context for each request
• Source: mcp_context.py (15,161 bytes)
• Input: JWT token → extract user_id, classroom_id, role
• Output: MCPContext(user_id, classroom_id, role, permissions, allowed_document_ids)
• Validation: verify user is enrolled in classroom, check permissions
• Passed to every agent node for data scoping
234 How does the MCP context flow through the 4-node pipeline? MCP, flow, pipeline • Created once at request entry → injected into TutorState
• Node 1 (moderate): uses MCP for moderation logging (who asked what)
• Node 2 (ABCR/TAL): uses classroom_id for document scope analysis
• Node 3 (RAG): Qdrant filter = MCP.classroom_id (critical for isolation)
• Node 4 (generate): includes classroom context in prompt for relevant answers
• MCP is read-only — no node modifies the context
235 Scenario: Student sends rapid-fire messages (10 in 5 seconds). How does the system respond? rate limit, scenario • Rate limit: 10 messages/minute per user (FastAPI middleware)
• First 10: processed normally (each gets its own LangGraph execution)
• 11th message: HTTP 429 "Too many requests, please slow down"
• Previous responses still streaming when new messages arrive
• Frontend UX: disable send button while awaiting response
• No queue: messages beyond rate limit are rejected, not queued
236 How would you add a "study mode" to the TutorAgent? study mode, feature • New ABCR route: study_mode triggered by user command "/study [topic]"
• Behavior: agent generates flashcard-style Q&A, asks student to answer first
• Integration: LearningAgent provides weak topics, TutorAgent focuses on those
• LangGraph: add study_mode_node after ABCR classification
• State: add study_mode: bool, study_topic: str, questions_asked: int
237 What is the response streaming implementation detail? streaming, implementation • LLM client: async for chunk in gemini.generate_stream(prompt)
• Each chunk: {text: "The", finish_reason: None}
• FastAPI: yield f"data: {json.dumps({'token': chunk.text})}\n\n"
• End of stream: yield f"data: {json.dumps({'type': 'sources', 'data': sources})}\n\n"
• Final: yield f"data: {json.dumps({'type': 'done', 'metadata': {...}})}\n\n"
• Frontend: EventSource.onmessage = (event) => appendToken(event.data)
238 How does the TutorAgent handle exam preparation scenarios? exam prep, scenario • Student: "Help me prepare for the midterm on Chapter 1-5"
• ABCR: rag_full with broad document scope
• RAG: retrieves key concepts across chapters (diverse chunks)
• LLM: generates structured review — key topics, common questions, practice problems
• Follow-up: offers to quiz on specific topics or explain weak areas
• Integration: checks LearningAgent data for student's weak topics
239 How are follow-up suggestions generated? follow-up, suggestions • After main response generated, second LLM call: "Based on this Q&A, suggest 3 follow-up questions"
• Suggestions ranked by: educational value, topic continuity, progressive difficulty
• Rendered as clickable buttons in chat UI
• If clicked: treated as new query, goes through full pipeline
• Purpose: encourages deeper learning, maintains engagement
• Cost: ~$0.0005 per call (short prompt, short response)
240 How does the TutorAgent handle comparison questions? comparison, pedagogy • "Compare and contrast X and Y" → ABCR classifies as rag_full
• TAL: dual anchors for X and Y
• RAG: searches for chunks about both X and Y
• LLM prompted: structured comparison format (table, pros/cons, key differences)
• Source attribution: "[1] for X perspective, [2] for Y perspective"
• Example: "Compare TCP and UDP" → table with headers, explanations for each
241 Scenario: The Gemini API is down. How does the TutorAgent respond? API failure, scenario • LLM call fails → retry once with shorter context
• Second failure → switch to fallback model (Groq Llama)
• If Groq also fails → return cached response for similar query (if exists)
• Last resort: "I'm experiencing technical difficulties. Please try again in a few minutes."
• All failures logged with alert to admin
• ABCR/moderation still work if only generation model is down
242 How would you implement personalized tutoring based on learning style? personalization, learning style • Store learning style preference per user: visual, textual, example-based
• TutorAgent reads preference from MCP context
• Prompt adaptation: "Explain with diagrams" vs "Explain with code examples" vs "Explain step-by-step"
• Research: visual learners get more RAG chunks with diagrams/images
• Implementation: add learning_style to UserProfile model, inject into system prompt
243 What is the prompt construction for source attribution? prompt, attribution • System prompt includes: "When using information from the provided sources, cite them using [1], [2] format."
• RAG chunks labeled: "Source [1] (Document: Data_Structures.pdf, Page: 42): ..."
• LLM trained to naturally cite inline: "Binary search has O(log n) complexity [1]."
• Post-processing regex: extract all \[\d+\] references from response
• Map back to source metadata for frontend display
244 How does the TutorAgent integrate with the assessment system? integration, assessment • TutorAgent doesn't directly generate assessments (that's AssessmentAgent)
• Integration: TutorAgent can reference assessment results when advising
• Example: "You scored 3/10 on recursion in your last quiz. Let me help you understand it better."
• Data flow: AssessmentAgent stores results → LearningAgent analyzes → TutorAgent reads learning data
• No direct communication: data shared via PostgreSQL/Redis
245 How would you implement conversation export? export, feature • New endpoint: GET /ai/tutor/session/{id}/export?format=pdf
• Read: conversation history from Redis
• Format: Markdown → PDF using weasyprint or reportlab
• Include: questions, answers, sources, timestamps
• Storage: save to S3/uploads volume, generate download link
• Alternative: export as markdown file for note-taking
246 How does the 4-node pipeline handle edge cases? edge cases, pipeline • Empty query: rejected at FastAPI validation level (400 Bad Request)
• Very long query (>500 words): truncated to first 500 words with notice
• Non-English query: passed through (Gemini handles ~100 languages)
• Special characters: escaped in RAG search, preserved in response
• Emoji-only query: ABCR classifies as general_chat, responds casually
247 What metrics should be tracked for TutorAgent performance? metrics, monitoring • Latency: per-node timing and total response time (P50, P95, P99)
• Quality: user feedback (thumbs up/down), citation rate, source groundedness
• Usage: queries per day, unique students, topics queried
• Errors: error rate per node, fallback rate, timeout rate
• Cost: tokens used per request, daily LLM API spend
• Coverage: % queries with RAG support vs general knowledge only
248 How is the TutorAgent tested? testing, tutor • Unit tests: each node tested independently with mock state
• ABCR tests: fixed queries → expected routes (regression suite)
• TAL tests: conversation sequences → expected anchor states
• RAG tests: known documents → expected retrieval results
• Integration: full pipeline with mock LLM, verify state flow
• Quality: monthly human evaluation of 50 random responses
249 Scenario: A teacher uploads 500 pages of PDF. How does RAG handle it? large document, scenario • Document ingestion: split into ~1000 chunks (512 tokens each, 50 overlap)
• Embedding: batch processing at 200 chunks/min = ~5 minutes
• Qdrant index: 1000 vectors added (~768KB of storage)
• Retrieval: Qdrant handles 1000 vectors easily (designed for millions)
• Query time: unchanged (~20ms) — HNSW index scales logarithmically
• Challenge: ensuring chunk boundaries don't split important concepts
250 How does the TutorAgent handle requests for practice problems? practice, problems • ABCR classifies: educational query → rag_full
• RAG retrieves: relevant theory chunks for the topic
• LLM generates: practice problems based on retrieved content
• Difficulty: matched to student level (from LearningAgent performance data)
• Follow-up: "Want me to check your answer?" → evaluates student response
• Note: for formal assessments, Orchestrator routes to AssessmentAgent instead
251 What is the difference between session cache and ABCR cache? cache, comparison • Session cache: stores conversation history, topic anchors — session-scoped
• ABCR cache: stores query classification results — query-scoped
• Session TTL: 30 minutes of inactivity
• ABCR TTL: 5 minutes fixed
• Session key: session:{session_id}, ABCR key: abcr:{session_id}:{query_hash}
• Different purposes: session for continuity, ABCR for duplicate classification prevention
252 How does the TutorAgent handle multi-part questions? multi-part, complex queries • Example: "Explain DFS, compare it with BFS, and give code examples for both"
• ABCR: classifies as single rag_full request
• TAL: extracts two anchors/subtopics: DFS, BFS
• RAG: searches for both topics, returns chunks for each
• LLM: structures response with clear sections: (1) DFS explanation, (2) BFS explanation, (3) Comparison, (4) Code
• Longer response: max_tokens may need increase for complex queries
253 How would you implement a feedback loop from student responses to improve the tutor? feedback loop, improvement • Collect: thumbs up/down per response + optional text feedback
• Store: TutorFeedback(response_id, rating, comment, query, response_text)
• Analyze: weekly report of low-rated responses → identify patterns
• Improve: update system prompt based on common failure modes
• A/B test: try new prompt version → compare ratings
• Long-term: fine-tune model on high-rated response examples
254 What is the TutorAgent's role in the broader learning pipeline? pipeline, learning, role • Entry point: student's primary interaction with the AI system
• Connects to: LearningAgent (performance data), CurriculumAgent (learning path), AssessmentAgent (quiz results)
• Produces: conversation data that LearningAgent analyzes for learning patterns
• Consumes: student performance data to adapt tutoring difficulty
• Position: most used agent, gateway to all other learning features
255 How does the system handle when a student asks the same question twice? duplicate, query • ABCR cache may hit for identical question within 5 minutes
• RAG results likely identical (same documents)
• LLM may generate slightly different response (non-zero temperature)
• No explicit duplicate detection — each query processed independently
• Enhancement: detect exact duplicate → offer: "I answered this recently. Want me to rephrase or go deeper?"
256 Scenario: ABCR misclassifies a document query as general chat. What happens? misclassification, scenario • Impact: RAG retrieval skipped → answer lacks document-specific information
• Student gets generic answer instead of course-specific one
• Detection: if student says "But what about the textbook?" → ABCR reclassifies next turn
• Fix: improve ABCR prompt with more examples of document queries
• Monitoring: track ABCR override rate (how often user needs to rephrase)
257 How does the TutorAgent handle citation-heavy topics? citations, dense content • RAG returns multiple relevant chunks — may have 10+ good matches
• Chunk selection: top 5 by score, with diversity constraint (max 2 per document)
• LLM manages: may cite all 5 sources or select most relevant 2-3
• Long responses: multiple paragraphs with different sources each
• Post-processing: verify all cited numbers exist in source list
258 How would you implement collaborative tutoring (multiple students, one agent)? collaborative, multi-user • Group session: shared session_id, multiple user_ids
• Chat history: merged stream from all participants
• MCP: shared classroom context, but student permissions may vary
• Challenge: conversation can diverge — multiple topics simultaneously
• TAL: may need multiple anchor sets (one per active participant)
• Use case: study group asking agent questions together
259 What is the TutorAgent's approach to handling sensitive educational topics? sensitive, ethical, topics • Moderation: checks for harmful content, not for topic sensitivity
• Educational freedom: agent explains any academic topic honestly (biology, history, etc.)
• Age-appropriate: system prompt includes "explain at appropriate academic level"
• Teacher control: teachers can restrict certain topics per classroom
• No political opinions: agent presents balanced views with multiple perspectives
260 How does the TutorAgent ensure educational quality? quality, educational • Grounding: responses based on uploaded course materials (not hallucinated)
• Citations: traceable claims back to source documents
• Accuracy: low temperature (0.3) reduces creative/inaccurate responses
• Pedagogy: Socratic mode, difficulty adaptation, follow-up suggestions
• Feedback: user ratings drive continuous improvement
• Expert review: teacher can review and flag incorrect agent responses

Section 5: RAG Pipeline & Vector Search (60 Questions)¶

Key Concepts: Retrieval-Augmented Generation · Embedding Strategy · all-mpnet-base-v2 · Qdrant · HNSW Index · Semantic Chunking · Document Ingestion · Retrieval Pipeline · Scoring & Ranking · Specialized RAG Variants

# Question Keywords Answer
261 What is RAG and why does EnsureStudy use it? RAG, overview • Retrieval-Augmented Generation: retrieve relevant documents first, then generate answer using them as context
• Without RAG: LLM answers from training data only (may be outdated/wrong for course-specific content)
• With RAG: LLM answers grounded in uploaded course materials (accurate, citable)
• Reduces hallucination by providing factual evidence in the prompt
262 Describe the end-to-end RAG architecture. architecture, pipeline • Offline: documents uploaded → chunked → embedded → indexed in Qdrant
• Online: query → embedded → Qdrant similarity search → top-k chunks retrieved → injected into LLM prompt → answer generated
• Two phases: ingestion (offline, batch) and retrieval (online, real-time)
• Components: EmbeddingService, QdrantService, ChunkingService, LLM client
263 Why was all-mpnet-base-v2 chosen as the embedding model? embedding, model • 768 dimensions — good balance of quality vs storage/compute
• Top performer on MTEB benchmark at its size class
• Trained on 1B+ sentence pairs for strong semantic understanding
• Runs locally on CPU (~50ms per sentence) — no external API dependency
• Trade-off: larger models (e5-large) are better but 3x slower
264 What is HNSW and how does Qdrant use it? HNSW, vector index • Hierarchical Navigable Small World — approximate nearest neighbor algorithm
• Creates layered graph of vectors: top layers sparse (fast navigation), bottom layers dense (precise)
• Parameters: m=16 (connections per node), ef_construct=100 (build quality)
• Search: ~95% recall at 10-20ms for 1M vectors
• Trade-off: higher m/ef = better recall but more memory and slower build
265 How does semantic chunking work in the document pipeline? chunking, semantic • Documents split into 512-token chunks with 50-token overlap
• Overlap prevents: losing context at chunk boundaries (sentence split mid-idea)
• Chunking strategy: split on paragraph boundaries when possible
• Fallback: if paragraph > 512 tokens, split on sentence boundaries
• Metadata per chunk: source document, page number, chunk index
266 Why 512 tokens with 50-token overlap? Why not different sizes? chunk size, trade-off • Too small (128): fragments context, retrieval returns incomplete ideas
• Too large (1024): retrieval returns too much irrelevant text with the relevant part
• 512: fits well in embedding model's context window (max 512 for mpnet-base)
• 50-token overlap: ~10% redundancy, ensures key sentences at boundaries aren't lost
• Tested internally: 512/50 gave best retrieval F1 score
267 What are the 7 stages of the document ingestion pipeline? ingestion, stages • Stage 1: Validation (file type, size, format)
• Stage 2: Preprocessing (text extraction, cleaning)
• Stage 3: Text extraction/OCR (for scanned documents)
• Stage 4: Semantic chunking (split into 512-token chunks)
• Stage 5: Embedding generation (all-mpnet-base-v2)
• Stage 6: Qdrant indexing (store vectors + payload)
• Stage 7: Completion notification (update DB status, notify user)
268 How does the retrieval pipeline score and rank results? scoring, ranking • Qdrant returns top-k with cosine similarity scores (0.0 to 1.0)
• Score threshold: chunks below 0.3 excluded (not relevant enough)
• Re-ranking: vector score (70%) + keyword overlap (20%) + recency (10%)
• Diversity: max 2 chunks from same document to avoid dominance
• Final: top 5 chunks selected for LLM context window
269 What specialized RAG variants exist? variants, specialized • Document RAG: primary — course materials, textbooks, lecture notes
• Notes RAG: student personal notes — filtered by user_id
• Meeting RAG: lecture recordings transcribed and embedded — searchable by meeting_id
• Web Content RAG: web pages fetched by research agent — with trust scores
• Each uses same embedding model but separate Qdrant collections
270 How does the Notes Embedding Service work? notes, embedding • Student saves note → backend triggers embedding in background
• Note split into chunks → embedded → stored in notes collection
• Payload: {user_id, note_id, created_at}
• Queries always filtered by user_id — students only search their own notes
• Privacy: shared collection but isolated by mandatory payload filter
271 How does Meeting RAG work? meeting, RAG, transcription • Meeting recorded via LiveKit → audio → Whisper transcription
• Transcript chunked and embedded → stored in meetings collection
• Payload: {meeting_id, speaker, timestamp_start, timestamp_end}
• Query: "What did professor say about recursion?" → semantic search within meeting chunks
• Time-scoped: can search specific meeting or all meetings in classroom
272 How does web content embedding work? web, embedding, trust • ResearchAgent fetches web pages → WebIngestService extracts text
• Text chunked → embedded → stored in web_content collection
• Payload includes trust_score calculated from domain authority, content quality
• Low trust (< 0.3) pages not embedded — filtered out
• TTL: web content re-fetched after 24 hours for freshness
273 What is the embedding consistency issue and how is it mitigated? consistency, embedding • If embedding model changes, ALL existing vectors become incompatible
• New model produces different vector space — old vectors useless for search
• Mitigation: model name stored as collection metadata constant
• Model change requires: create new collection → re-embed all documents → swap
• Never hot-swap models — always full migration
274 Describe the query processing pipeline before vector search. query, processing • Step 1: Original query cleaned (strip whitespace, normalize unicode)
• Step 2: TAL keyword augmentation (add topic anchor terms)
• Step 3: Embed query using same model as documents (critical!)
• Step 4: Construct Qdrant filter (classroom_id from MCP)
• Step 5: Execute search with top_k=10, score_threshold=0.3
• Step 6: Post-filter and re-rank results
275 How does the response cache work for RAG? cache, RAG, response • Cache key: rag:{hash(query + classroom_id + filters)}
• Stores: serialized list of chunk results
• TTL: 10 minutes (short because new documents may change results)
• Hit rate: ~15-20% (students in same class ask similar questions)
• Invalidation: on new document upload, clear all cache for that classroom
• Saves: ~300ms per hit (skip embedding + Qdrant search)
276 What are the performance characteristics of the RAG pipeline? performance, latency • Embedding generation: ~50ms per query
• Qdrant search (1M vectors): ~10-20ms
• Post-processing and re-ranking: ~5ms
• Total retrieval: ~75-100ms
• Ingestion rate: ~200 chunks/minute (CPU-only)
• Storage: ~3KB per 768-dim vector + payload
277 Scenario: Search returns relevant chunks but from wrong document. Why? debugging, scenario • Cause 1: document topics overlap — two documents discuss same subject
• Cause 2: chunk boundaries split context — chunk matches superficially
• Cause 3: embedding model groups similar concepts
• Fix: use document_id filter (ABCR scoped search) for specific document queries
• Fix: add document title to chunk text for better differentiation
278 How would you implement hybrid search (vector + keyword)? hybrid, search • Vector search: Qdrant cosine similarity (semantic matching)
• Keyword search: BM25 on chunk text (exact term matching)
• Combination: Reciprocal Rank Fusion — merge results by rank position
• Score = 1/(k + rank_vector) + 1/(k + rank_keyword), k=60
• Benefits: catches exact terms (code, names) that embeddings miss
• Implementation: add Elasticsearch or use Qdrant's upcoming hybrid search
279 How does the scalability of the RAG pipeline look? scalability, RAG • Qdrant: single node handles millions of vectors, sharding for billions
• Embedding model: CPU-bound, scale with more instances
• API: async FastAPI handles concurrent queries well
• Bottleneck: LLM generation (not retrieval) dominates latency
• Storage: ~3KB per chunk × 1M chunks = ~3GB (manageable)
• Growth: add Qdrant replicas for read scaling
280 What is the data flow summary for RAG? data flow, summary • Ingestion: Upload → Validate → Extract → Chunk → Embed → Index → Done
• Retrieval: Query → Embed → Search → Filter → Rank → Select top-k → Return
• Generation: Chunks + Query + History → LLM prompt → Stream response → Attribute sources
• Feedback: User rating → Store → Analyze → Improve prompts/chunking
# Question Keywords Answer
281 How does chunking handle tables and structured data in PDFs? tables, structured, chunking • Tables extracted as text rows using PDF parsing library (pdfplumber/PyMuPDF)
• Challenge: table context lost when split into chunks
• Solution: keep entire table as one chunk if < 512 tokens
• Large tables: split by rows with header repeated in each chunk
• Metadata: chunk tagged as type: "table" for special handling
282 How does the system handle duplicate document uploads? duplicate, upload • File hash (SHA-256) computed on upload
• Checked against existing documents in same classroom
• If duplicate: return existing document reference, skip re-processing
• If modified: new version created, old vectors optionally replaced
• Prevents: wasted compute on re-embedding identical documents
283 How would you implement feedback-driven retrieval improvement? feedback, improvement • Track: which chunks were cited in highly-rated responses
• Track: which chunks were in responses with negative feedback
• Boost: chunks with positive citations get score multiplier
• Penalize: chunks that consistently appear in bad responses
• Re-chunk: documents where chunk boundaries consistently cause issues
284 What happens when a document is deleted? delete, cleanup • PostgreSQL: document record soft-deleted (status → "deleted")
• Qdrant: all vectors with document_id filter deleted
• Redis: clear any cached retrieval results for that document
• Challenge: deletion is async — slight delay before vectors removed
• User experience: document disappears from UI immediately, RAG catches up
285 How does the embedding batch processing work for ingestion? batch, embedding • Chunks collected in batches of 32 for efficient GPU/CPU utilization
• model.encode(texts, batch_size=32, show_progress_bar=True)
• SentenceTransformer handles batching internally with padding
• Progress reported: processed {i}/{total} chunks via SSE
• Total time: ~5 minutes for 1000 chunks on CPU
286 What is the difference between vector search and keyword search for RAG? vector vs keyword • Vector search: finds semantically similar text (meaning-based)
• Keyword search: finds exact term matches (word-based)
• Vector wins: "How does a car engine work?" matches "internal combustion mechanism"
• Keyword wins: "What is the O(n log n) complexity?" — exact notation matters
• Best: hybrid approach combining both for comprehensive retrieval
287 How would you evaluate RAG quality systematically? evaluation, quality • Retrieval metrics: Precision@k, Recall@k, MRR (Mean Reciprocal Rank)
• Generation metrics: BLEU, ROUGE (against reference answers), faithfulness score
• End-to-end: user satisfaction, citation accuracy, answer correctness
• Test dataset: manually curated Q&A pairs with expected source documents
• A/B testing: compare chunking strategies, embedding models, prompt templates
288 How does the system handle multi-format documents? formats, document • Supported: PDF, DOCX, PPTX, TXT, images (JPG, PNG)
• PDF: PyMuPDF for text extraction, fallback to OCR for scanned pages
• DOCX: python-docx for text and table extraction
• PPTX: python-pptx for slide text extraction
• Images: OCR pipeline (Tesseract + EasyOCR)
• All formats: converted to plain text before chunking
289 Scenario: Retrieval returns chunks but LLM ignores them. Why? LLM ignore, scenario • Cause 1: chunks poorly formatted — LLM can't parse garbled OCR text
• Cause 2: chunks not relevant enough — LLM prioritizes general knowledge
• Cause 3: prompt doesn't emphasize source usage strongly enough
• Fix: stronger prompt instruction "You MUST use the provided sources"
• Fix: improve chunk quality (better OCR, better chunking boundaries)
• Fix: add "If sources don't help, say so" instruction
290 How does the filtering capability in Qdrant work? Qdrant, filtering • Pre-filter: applied BEFORE vector search (narrows search space)
• Syntax: {"must": [{"key": "classroom_id", "match": {"value": "cls123"}}]}
• Multi-filter: {"must": [...], "must_not": [...], "should": [...]}
• Payload indexes: keyword indexes on filterable fields for efficiency
• Use case: filter by classroom, document, content type, date range
291 What is the Qdrant service API design? Qdrant, API • create_collection(name, vector_size, distance) — initialize new collection
• upsert_vectors(collection, points) — add/update vectors with payloads
• search(collection, query_vector, filter, top_k) — similarity search
• delete_by_filter(collection, filter) — remove vectors matching conditions
• scroll(collection, filter, limit) — iterate all vectors (for export/migration)
292 How does the recency boost work in scoring? recency, boost, scoring • Recently uploaded documents get 10% score boost: final = score * (1 + 0.1 * recency_factor)
• recency_factor: 1.0 for today, 0.5 for this week, 0.0 for older
• Purpose: newer course materials likely more relevant than old ones
• Teacher uploaded new lecture notes → automatically surface in results
• Configurable: boost factor adjustable per classroom
293 How would you add cross-classroom RAG? cross-classroom, scenario • Currently: strict MCP filtering — classroom A can't access classroom B docs
• Enhancement: shared "library" collection accessible to all classrooms
• Implementation: teacher marks documents as "shared" → separate collection
• Search: query user's classroom collection + shared library collection
• Merge results using weighted scoring (classroom docs get priority)
294 What is the Qdrant collection architecture and naming scheme? collection, naming • ensurestudy_documents — course documents per classroom
• ensurestudy_notes — student personal notes
• ensurestudy_meetings — lecture recording transcripts
• ensurestudy_web — web content from research agent
• Convention: ensurestudy_{content_type} for consistency
• All use same embedding model for cross-collection compatibility
295 How does re-ranking improve retrieval quality? re-ranking, quality • Stage 1: Qdrant returns top-20 by cosine similarity
• Stage 2: re-rank using: vector score (70%) + keyword overlap (20%) + recency (10%)
• Keyword overlap: count matching query terms in chunk text
• Re-ranking catches: semantically similar but keyword-irrelevant chunks
• Final: top-5 after re-ranking shown to LLM
• Future: cross-encoder re-ranking for even better precision
296 Scenario: Embedding model needs to be upgraded. What's the migration plan? migration, model upgrade • Create new Qdrant collection with new model's vector dimensions
• Re-embed all documents in batches (queue via Kafka for reliability)
• Run both old and new collections in parallel during migration
• Compare: search quality metrics on test dataset between old and new
• If new is better: update config → switch code to new collection → delete old
• Estimated time: 100K chunks at 200/min = ~8 hours
297 How does the system handle multilingual documents? multilingual, documents • Currently: all-mpnet-base-v2 is English-focused
• For multilingual: switch to paraphrase-multilingual-mpnet-base-v2
• Same architecture, trained on 50+ languages
• Trade-off: slightly lower English quality, much better cross-lingual retrieval
• Allows: English query → retrieve French document chunks
• Requires: re-embedding all existing documents after model switch
298 What is the chunk diversity constraint and why is it important? diversity, constraint • Rule: maximum 2 chunks from the same document in retrieval results
• Without constraint: one highly relevant document dominates all 5 slots
• Diversity benefits: broader coverage, multiple perspectives, better answers
• Implementation: after scoring, iterate results and skip if document already has 2 chunks
• Exception: if ABCR scope is single document, no diversity constraint
299 How does the system handle very short documents? short document • Documents < 512 tokens: single chunk covering entire document
• No overlap needed for single-chunk documents
• Metadata still attached: document_id, page (page 1), chunk_index (0)
• Works fine with RAG — just one chunk to retrieve
• Edge case: empty document (0 tokens) → validation rejects at Stage 1
300 How would you implement semantic caching for RAG? semantic cache • Instead of exact query match: cache by embedding similarity
• Embed query → search cache embeddings → if similarity > 0.95, return cached result
• Benefits: "What is DNA?" and "Explain DNA" share cached results
• Storage: Redis or separate Qdrant collection for cached query embeddings
• Higher hit rate than exact cache (~30-40% vs 15-20%)
• Trade-off: embedding lookup adds ~50ms but saves ~300ms on cache hit

Section 6: Document Processing & OCR Pipeline (60 Questions)¶

Key Concepts: 7-Stage Pipeline · DocumentAgent · Validation · Text Extraction · OCR (Tesseract + EasyOCR) · Image Enhancement · Semantic Chunking · LaTeX Formula Handling · Progress Reporting · Notes Processing

# Question Keywords Answer
301 What is the DocumentAgent and how big is the codebase? DocumentAgent, overview • 617 lines of Python implementing 7-stage document processing pipeline
• LangGraph state machine with 7 nodes (one per stage)
• Handles: PDF, DOCX, PPTX, TXT, images (JPG/PNG)
• Async processing: runs in background after upload
• Reports progress via SSE to frontend (0% → 100%)
302 Describe Stage 1: Document Validation. validation, Stage 1 • Check file extension against whitelist: .pdf, .docx, .pptx, .txt, .jpg, .png
• Check file size: max 50MB
• MIME type verification: actual content matches extension
• Virus scan: optional ClamAV integration for malware detection
• If validation fails: status → "failed", error message stored, pipeline stops
303 What happens in Stage 2: Preprocessing? preprocessing, Stage 2 • PDF: extract page count, reading order, metadata
• DOCX: extract text, tables, headers, footers
• PPTX: extract slide text, speaker notes
• Text normalization: unicode normalization (NFKC), whitespace cleanup
• Language detection: identify document language for downstream processing
304 How does Stage 3: Text Extraction/OCR work? OCR, extraction, Stage 3 • Conditional: only triggered for scanned PDFs or image files
• Detection: if extracted text < 100 chars per page, assume scanned → OCR
• Hybrid OCR: Tesseract (primary) + EasyOCR (fallback for complex layouts)
• Image enhancement applied before OCR for better accuracy
• Output: full text from all pages combined
305 What is the image enhancement pipeline for OCR? image, enhancement • Step 1: Convert to grayscale (remove color noise)
• Step 2: Adaptive thresholding (binarize text vs background)
• Step 3: Noise removal (median filter, morphological operations)
• Step 4: Deskew (correct rotation using Hough transform)
• Step 5: Resize to 300 DPI (optimal for Tesseract)
• Improvement: ~20-30% accuracy boost for low-quality scans
306 Why use both Tesseract and EasyOCR? Tesseract, EasyOCR, hybrid • Tesseract: fast, accurate for printed English text, well-maintained
• EasyOCR: better for complex layouts, tables, multi-language, handwriting
• Strategy: Tesseract first → if confidence < 80%, retry with EasyOCR
• EasyOCR slower (~3x) but handles edge cases better
• Best of both: high accuracy without always paying EasyOCR's speed cost
307 How does LaTeX formula handling work? LaTeX, formula, math • Math expressions in PDFs often rendered as images
• OCR: recognizes math symbols but not LaTeX notation
• Enhancement: pix2tex or nougat model converts math images to LaTeX strings
• Output: $\int_0^1 f(x)dx$ stored in chunk text
• Frontend: MathJax renders LaTeX in tutor responses
308 Describe Stage 4: Semantic Chunking. chunking, Stage 4, semantic • Split text into 512-token chunks with 50-token overlap
• Chunk boundaries: prefer paragraph/section breaks
• If paragraph > 512 tokens: split on sentence boundaries
• Table handling: keep table as single chunk if fits, else split by rows with header
• Output: list of chunks with metadata (page, position, type)
309 How does Stage 5: Embedding Generation work? embedding, Stage 5 • Load all-mpnet-base-v2 model (singleton, already loaded at startup)
• Batch encode: model.encode(chunks, batch_size=32)
• Output: N × 768-dimensional numpy arrays
• Normalization: L2 normalized for cosine similarity computation
• Speed: ~200 chunks/minute on CPU, ~1000 chunks/minute on GPU
310 What happens in Stage 6: Qdrant Indexing? indexing, Stage 6, Qdrant • Create point objects: {id: uuid, vector: embedding, payload: metadata}
• Payload: {document_id, classroom_id, chunk_text, chunk_index, page_number, source_type}
• Upsert to collection: qdrant_client.upsert(collection, points, batch_size=100)
• Batch upsert for efficiency: chunks uploaded in groups of 100
• Verify: count points after upload matches expected chunk count
311 How does Stage 7: Completion & Notification work? completion, Stage 7 • Update document status in PostgreSQL: processing → completed
• Store: chunk_count, processed_at timestamp, total_pages
• Callback to core service: POST /api/documents/{id}/processing-complete
• SSE event: {type: "progress", value: 1.0, status: "completed"}
• If any stage failed: status → "failed" with error details
312 What is the DocumentState TypedDict? state, DocumentState • file_path: uploaded file location
• file_type: detected MIME type
• raw_text: extracted text from document
• chunks: list of text chunks after splitting
• embeddings: list of vectors after encoding
• progress: float 0.0-1.0 for progress bar
• error: error message if any stage fails
• metadata: page count, word count, language
313 How does conditional routing work in the document pipeline? conditional, routing • After Stage 2 (preprocessing): check if OCR needed
• Condition: state["needs_ocr"] set based on text extraction quality
• If True: route to Stage 3 (OCR) → then Stage 4
• If False: skip Stage 3, go directly to Stage 4 (chunking)
• Saves time: text-based PDFs skip OCR (~3-5 seconds saved per document)
314 How does progress reporting work? progress, reporting, SSE • Each stage updates state["progress"] incrementally
• Stage 1: 0.05, Stage 2: 0.15, Stage 3: 0.30, Stage 4: 0.50, Stage 5: 0.75, Stage 6: 0.90, Stage 7: 1.00
• Progress streamed via SSE to frontend: data: {"progress": 0.50, "stage": "chunking"}
• Frontend renders: progress bar with stage label
• Useful for large documents that take several minutes
315 Describe the NotesAgent and how it differs from DocumentAgent. NotesAgent, comparison • NotesAgent: processes student-created notes (shorter, informal text)
• 483 lines — simpler pipeline than DocumentAgent
• No OCR needed: notes are typed text, not scanned
• Chunking: smaller chunks (256 tokens) — notes are more concise
• Privacy: notes stored with user_id filter in Qdrant
• Trigger: on note save/update, not file upload
316 How does the system handle processing failures mid-pipeline? failure, recovery • Each stage wrapped in try/except with stage-specific error handling
• On failure: state updated with error, pipeline routes to completion (with error)
• Document status: "failed" with error_stage and error_message
• Retry: user can retry processing (re-triggers from Stage 1)
• Common failures: OCR on very poor quality scans, embedding service overloaded
317 What are the supporting data classes for the document pipeline? data classes, document • DocumentChunk(text, page, index, type, metadata)
• OCRResult(text, confidence, language, method) — method = tesseract or easyocr
• ProcessingProgress(stage, progress, message, estimated_remaining)
• ValidationResult(valid, errors, file_info)
• All using Python dataclasses for type safety
318 Scenario: A student uploads a 200-page scanned textbook. What happens? large document, scenario • Validation: 200 pages, ~20MB PDF — within limits ✓
• Preprocessing: text extraction yields little text → triggers OCR
• OCR: 200 pages × ~3s/page = ~10 minutes (bottleneck)
• Chunking: ~800 chunks (4 chunks per page average)
• Embedding: 800 chunks ÷ 200/min = ~4 minutes
• Indexing: 800 vectors uploaded to Qdrant in ~30 seconds
• Total: ~15-20 minutes, progress streamed throughout
319 How would you speed up the document processing pipeline? optimization, speed • OCR: GPU-accelerated EasyOCR for 10x speedup on scanned docs
• Embedding: GPU batch encoding for 5x speedup
• Parallel chunking: split pages across workers
• Async OCR: process pages concurrently instead of sequentially
• Skip OCR: ML classifier to detect scanned vs text pages (skip OCR for text pages)
• Pre-processing cache: if same file uploaded before (hash match), reuse results
320 How does the system handle password-protected PDFs? password, protected, PDF • Stage 1 validation detects password protection
• Currently: processing fails with error "Password-protected PDF not supported"
• Enhancement: prompt user for password → decrypt → proceed with pipeline
• Implementation: PyMuPDF supports password decryption
• Security: password not stored, used only during processing session
# Question Keywords Answer
321 How does the system handle multi-column PDFs? multi-column, layout • PyMuPDF extracts text in reading order (handles most layouts)
• Complex layouts: text blocks sorted by position (top-to-bottom, left-to-right)
• Two-column: text extracted column by column, not mixed
• Very complex: fallback to OCR which handles layout detection
• Edge case: academic papers with footnotes, sidebars — quality varies
322 What is the OCR confidence score and how is it used? OCR, confidence • Tesseract returns per-word confidence (0-100)
• Page-level confidence: average of all word confidences
• If page confidence < 80%: retry with EasyOCR
• If both < 60%: mark document with warning "Low quality OCR — results may be inaccurate"
• Low-confidence chunks tagged in Qdrant payload: ocr_quality: "low"
323 How does the system handle images within PDFs? images, PDF, extraction • PyMuPDF extracts embedded images from PDF pages
• Images containing text (screenshots, diagrams with labels): OCR applied
• Decorative images: skipped (detected by size/position heuristics)
• Image captions: extracted as text and associated with nearest text chunk
• Enhancement: use vision LLM to describe images for accessibility
324 Scenario: OCR produces garbled text for a complex diagram. How to handle? garbled, OCR, scenario • Detection: chunk with low word-to-character ratio or high special character rate
• Handling: mark chunk as type: "diagram", don't embed (low search value)
• Alternative: send image to vision LLM for description instead of OCR
• User warning: "Some pages contain diagrams that couldn't be fully processed"
• Future: integrate document layout analysis models
325 How does the Pipeline handle PPTX files specifically? PPTX, PowerPoint • python-pptx library for extraction
• Per-slide: title, body text, table text, speaker notes
• Slide number stored as "page" in chunk metadata
• Speaker notes: chunked separately with type: "notes" tag
• Tables in slides: extracted as text rows
• Images in slides: skipped (OCR not applied to embedded slide images currently)
326 How does the pipeline handle code blocks in documents? code, extraction • Code blocks in PDF/DOCX: extracted as plain text
• Indentation preserved when possible (critical for Python)
• Challenge: PDF code blocks may lose formatting
• Solution: detect code blocks by font (monospace) → preserve as-is
• Chunking: code blocks kept intact as single chunk when possible
327 What metrics are tracked for document processing? metrics, monitoring • Processing time per stage and total
• OCR accuracy (confidence scores)
• Chunk count and document coverage
• Failure rate by stage (which stages fail most?)
• Queue depth: how many documents waiting to process
• Storage: Qdrant collection size growth over time
328 How does the system handle concurrent document uploads? concurrent, uploads • Each upload gets its own async task (background processing)
• Limit: max 5 concurrent uploads per user
• Global limit: max 20 concurrent document processing tasks
• Queue: if limit reached, new uploads queued (FIFO)
• Progress: each upload tracked independently via SSE
329 How would you add a document preview feature? preview, feature • After Stage 2 (preprocessing): store first 500 words as preview text
• Preview API: GET /api/documents/{id}/preview returns preview text
• Frontend: show preview on hover/click in document list
• PDF thumbnail: extract first page as image using PyMuPDF
• No second processing needed: preview generated during initial pipeline
330 How does the system ensure chunk quality? chunk quality, validation • Post-chunking validation: reject chunks with < 20 characters (too short)
• Header/footer removal: common repeating text stripped before chunking
• Page number removal: regex strips "Page X of Y" patterns
• Encoding issues: detect and fix garbled unicode characters
• Quality flag: chunks with many special characters flagged as low quality
331 How would you add support for CSV/Excel documents? CSV, Excel, feature • Add xlsx and csv to validation whitelist
• Extraction: openpyxl (Excel) or csv module (CSV)
• Chunking: each row group (50 rows) as one chunk
• Column headers: prepended to each chunk for context
• Embedding: tabular data benefits from "Row N: col1=val1, col2=val2" format
• Challenge: very wide tables need special handling
332 How does the document processing pipeline handle timeouts? timeout, handling • Overall timeout: 30 minutes per document (covers very large scanned PDFs)
• Per-stage timeout: Stage 3 (OCR) has 15-minute limit
• If timeout: pipeline aborted, status → "failed", error = "Processing timed out"
• User can retry or upload smaller document
• Background: asyncio task cancelled on timeout
333 What is the pipeline error handling for each stage? error handling, stages • Stage 1 (validation) fail: immediate rejection, no processing
• Stage 2 (preprocessing) fail: try backup extraction library
• Stage 3 (OCR) fail: skip OCR, use whatever text was extracted
• Stage 4 (chunking) fail: try simpler chunking (fixed-size split)
• Stage 5 (embedding) fail: retry once, then fail document
• Stage 6 (indexing) fail: retry 3 times, then fail, clean up partial vectors
• Stage 7 (completion) fail: mark as "completed_with_warnings"
334 How would you implement document versioning? versioning, documents • Upload same filename: create new version, keep old version archived
• Version table: DocumentVersion(document_id, version, file_path, processed_at)
• Qdrant: old version vectors tagged with version: 1, new with version: 2
• Default search: latest version only
• Compare: generate diff between versions (text comparison)
335 Scenario: 50 students upload documents simultaneously. What happens? concurrent, load, scenario • Global limit: 20 concurrent processing tasks
• 30 uploads queued in FIFO order
• Each upload gets SSE progress stream
• Queued uploads show: "Your document is in queue, position: 12"
• Embed model: shared singleton, handles batch requests efficiently
• Qdrant: handles concurrent upserts well (built for it)
336 How does the system handle corrupted files? corrupted, files • Stage 1 validation: basic integrity check (file header matches format)
• Stage 2 preprocessing: library throws exception on corrupted file
• Error: "This file appears to be corrupted. Please re-upload."
• Graceful handling: no crash, error stored in database
• Corrupted file: preserved in uploads (for debugging) but marked as failed
337 How would you add audio/video document processing? audio, video, feature • Audio (MP3, WAV): Whisper transcription → text → chunking → embedding pipeline
• Video (MP4): extract audio track → Whisper → same pipeline
• Metadata: timestamps per chunk for time-based retrieval
• Query: "What was discussed at 15:30?" → find chunk with that timestamp
• Reuse: meeting transcription pipeline already handles this for LiveKit recordings
338 How are document processing results verified? verification, quality • Post-processing checks:
• Chunk count > 0 (document not empty)
• Average chunk length > 50 characters (meaningful content)
• Qdrant vector count matches chunk count
• Sample chunks readable (not garbled OCR)
• Automated: run checks in Stage 7 before marking complete
339 What is the disk space management for uploaded documents? disk, storage • Uploads stored in Docker volume: uploads_data
• Shared between core-service (receives upload) and ai-service (processes)
• No automatic cleanup: files persist until manually deleted
• Enhancement: delete processed files after X days (keep only Qdrant vectors)
• Production: move to S3 for scalable, durable storage
340 How would you implement document search within a single document? search, within document • Already supported via ABCR scoped routing + document_id filter
• Query: "search in data_structures.pdf for binary tree"
• ABCR: classifies as rag_scoped, sets document_filter = "doc_123"
• Qdrant: filters to only that document's chunks
• Returns: relevant chunks from that specific document only

Section 7: Research & Web Enrichment Agents (50 Questions)¶

Key Concepts: ResearchAgent · Web Enrichment Agent · WebIngestService · LangGraph Pipelines · Source Fetchers · Trust Scoring · Quality Ranking · Web Caching · Parallel Execution

# Question Keywords Answer
341 What is the ResearchAgent and when is it invoked? ResearchAgent, overview • 510-line agent for web-based research queries
• Invoked by Orchestrator when question needs external knowledge beyond uploaded docs
• Example: "What are the latest advances in quantum computing?"
• LangGraph pipeline: query analysis → web search → content extraction → synthesis → response
• Difference from Tutor: searches the web, not classroom documents
342 Describe the ResearchAgent's LangGraph pipeline. pipeline, research • State: ResearchState(query, search_results, web_sources, synthesized_answer, sources)
• Node 1: analyze_query — extract search keywords, determine search strategy
• Node 2: search_web — execute web searches via search API
• Node 3: fetch_content — retrieve and parse web pages
• Node 4: synthesize — LLM combines sources into coherent answer
• Node 5: format_response — add citations, trust indicators
343 How does query analysis work in the ResearchAgent? query analysis, search • LLM extracts: key search terms, query intent, required depth
• Generates 2-3 search queries from original question for broader coverage
• Example: "How does BERT work?" → ["BERT transformer architecture", "BERT pretraining MLM NSP", "BERT fine-tuning tutorial"]
• Search strategy: academic (arxiv), general web, or both
344 What is the Web Enrichment Agent and how does it differ from ResearchAgent? WebEnrichment, comparison • Purpose: proactively enriches knowledge base with web content (not on-demand Q&A)
• 456 lines — runs asynchronously, not triggered by user queries
• ResearchAgent: answers questions using web. WebEnrichment: builds web knowledge base
• Output: web content embedded in Qdrant for future RAG retrieval
• Trigger: teacher requests topic enrichment or scheduled crawling
345 Describe the Web Enrichment Agent's LangGraph pipeline. pipeline, web enrichment • Node 1: identify_topics — determine what to search based on curriculum
• Node 2: fetch_sources — parallel source fetchers (Google, Wikipedia, academic)
• Node 3: evaluate_quality — score each source for quality and relevance
• Node 4: embed_content — chunk and embed high-quality sources
• Node 5: index_content — store in Qdrant web_content collection
346 How do source fetchers work with parallel execution? parallel, fetching • Multiple source types searched simultaneously using asyncio.gather()
• Google Custom Search: general web results
• Wikipedia API: encyclopedic content
• Arxiv API: academic papers (optional)
• YouTube API: educational video transcripts (future)
• Each fetcher returns: WebSource(url, title, text, source_type, raw_html)
347 What is the WebSource data structure? WebSource, data structure • url: source URL
• title: page title
• text: extracted clean text (HTML stripped)
• source_type: "google", "wikipedia", "arxiv"
• trust_score: 0.0-1.0 calculated quality score
• fetched_at: timestamp for freshness tracking
• domain: extracted domain for authority scoring
348 How is trust score calculated for web sources? trust score, quality • Domain authority: known educational domains get base score (stanford.edu=0.9, random-blog.com=0.3)
• Content quality: text length, readability score, presence of citations
• Relevance: embedding similarity between source content and search query
• Freshness: content age (newer = slight boost for evolving topics)
• Formula: 0.3*authority + 0.3*quality + 0.3*relevance + 0.1*freshness
349 How does quality scoring & ranking work? ranking, quality, web • All fetched sources scored using trust score formula
• Sources below 0.3 threshold filtered out (unreliable)
• Remaining ranked by trust score (descending)
• Top 5 sources selected for response synthesis
• For web embedding: only sources > 0.6 trust score embedded in Qdrant
350 What caching strategy does the web research system use? caching, web, strategy • Redis cache: key = web:{hash(url)}, value = extracted text
• TTL: 1 hour for search results, 24 hours for page content
• Cache hit: skip HTTP fetch + HTML parsing (~500ms saved per page)
• Hit rate: ~30% for common educational topics
• Invalidation: no manual invalidation — TTL-based expiry only
351 What is the WebIngestService and why is it the largest file? WebIngestService, largest • web_ingest_service.py — 59,963 bytes, largest file in entire codebase
• Handles: HTTP fetching, HTML parsing, content extraction, trust scoring, embedding
• Large because: implements multiple fetcher backends, complex parsing logic, error handling for diverse web pages
• Could be split: separate fetchers, parsers, scorers into individual files
• Technical debt: candidate for refactoring
352 How does the ResearchAgent handle search API failures? failure, search, handling • Search API failure: retry once with backoff
• If still fails: try alternative search provider
• If all searches fail: respond with "I couldn't search the web right now"
• Cached results: check Redis for recent results on similar query
• Graceful degradation: never crash, always return user-facing message
353 How does the system prevent unsafe web content from reaching students? safety, web, content • Trust score filtering: low-authority domains excluded
• Content moderation: web text checked by ModerationAgent before presentation
• Domain blacklist: configurable list of excluded domains
• Adult content filter: enabled on search API queries
• Teacher oversight: web enrichment results reviewable in dashboard
354 Scenario: Teacher asks to enrich the curriculum with web resources on "neural networks". enrichment, scenario • WebEnrichmentAgent triggered with topic = "neural networks"
• Parallel search: Google (tutorials), Wikipedia (overview), arxiv (papers)
• ~15-20 results fetched, ~10 pass trust score threshold
• Top 5 sources chunked and embedded in web_content collection
• Tagged: classroom_id, topic: "neural_networks", trust_score
• Result: TutorAgent can now reference web content in neural network answers
355 How does the system handle rate-limited web APIs? rate limiting, web • Google Custom Search: 100 queries/day free tier, then paid
• Rate limiting: track daily count, reject requests over limit
• Mitigation: cache aggressively to reduce API calls
• Fallback: if Google exhausted, use DuckDuckGo (no rate limit, less quality)
• Alert: notify admin when approaching daily limit
356 What web resource services exist? web resources, services • GoogleSearchService: Google Custom Search API wrapper
• WikipediaService: Wikipedia REST API for encyclopedia content
• WebScraperService: generic HTTP fetch + BeautifulSoup parsing
• ArxivService: arxiv API for academic paper abstracts
• ContentExtractor: HTML → clean text conversion with boilerplate removal
357 How does the ResearchAgent synthesize information from multiple sources? synthesis, multi-source • Top 5 sources injected into LLM prompt as numbered references
• LLM prompted: "Synthesize information from sources. Cite [N] for each claim."
• Output: cohesive answer combining insights from all sources
• Contradictions handled: "Source [1] says X while source [3] argues Y"
• Temperature: 0.7 for ResearchAgent (more creative synthesis than tutoring)
358 How would you add a "fact-checking" capability to the ResearchAgent? fact-checking, feature • After synthesis: extract key claims from response
• Per claim: search for supporting/contradicting evidence
• Label each claim: "supported by N sources", "contradicted", "unverifiable"
• UI: color-coded claims (green=supported, yellow=unverifiable, red=contradicted)
• Trade-off: doubles processing time and API calls
359 What are the performance characteristics of the ResearchAgent? performance, research • Search API call: ~200ms
• Web page fetching (5 pages parallel): ~1-2s (network dependent)
• Content extraction per page: ~50ms
• Trust scoring: ~100ms
• LLM synthesis: ~2-3s
• Total: ~4-6s (longer than TutorAgent due to web latency)
• Cached queries: ~2-3s (skip fetch, go straight to synthesis)
360 How would you add citation link validation? citation, validation, links • After response generation: extract all URLs from citations
• Background: HEAD request to each URL to verify it's still live
• Dead links: mark as "(link may be unavailable)" or find archived version
• Archive: check Wayback Machine for cached copies
• Prevention: Trust score includes domain uptime history
# Question Keywords Answer
361 How does the web scraperhandle JavaScript-rendered pages? JavaScript, scraping • Default: HTTP GET with BeautifulSoup (static HTML only)
• Limitation: JavaScript-rendered content (SPAs) not captured
• Enhancement: Playwright/Selenium for dynamic content extraction
