362 How does the ResearchAgent handle academic queries vs general queries? academic, general, routing • Query analysis classifies: academic (needs arxiv/scholar) vs general (web search sufficient)
• Academic indicators: "paper", "study", "research by", citation references
• Academic path: arxiv API + Google Scholar → more authoritative sources
• General path: Google Custom Search + Wikipedia → broader coverage
• Both: trust scoring applies regardless
363 How would you implement source diversity in research results? diversity, results • Current: top-5 by trust score (may all be from similar domains)
• Enhancement: max 1 source per domain in final selection
• Select: highest trust per domain, then fill remaining slots
• Diversity benefit: multiple perspectives, not echo chamber
• Implementation: group results by domain → take best per group → merge
364 What is the WebEnrichmentAgent's caching strategy? caching, enrichment • Enriched content stored permanently in Qdrant (not ephemeral)
• Redis cache: enrichment task results cached for 24 hours
• Prevents: re-enriching same topic within 24 hours
• Teacher can force re-enrichment (bypasses cache)
• Freshness: web content has fetched_at timestamp for staleness detection
365 How does the web content filter work for educational relevance? filter, educational • LLM classification: "Is this content educationally relevant to [topic]?"
• Reject: promotional content, spam, off-topic pages
• Accept: tutorials, explanations, documentation, academic resources
• Edge cases: Stack Overflow answers (accept), forum complaints (reject)
• Manual override: teacher can whitelist/blacklist specific URLs
366 Scenario: A web search returns mostly paywalled content. How to handle? paywall, scenario • Detection: HTTP 403 or paywall detection patterns in HTML
• Skip paywalled content: don't include in results
• Metadata: note "X sources were behind paywalls and excluded"
• Alternatives: search for free alternatives (preprints, summaries)
• Arxiv: academic papers often have free preprint versions
367 How does boilerplate removal work in content extraction? boilerplate, extraction • BeautifulSoup: remove <nav>, <header>, <footer>, <sidebar> elements
• readability-lxml: extract main content area from HTML
• Text ratio: compare text density before/after extraction
• Common patterns: cookie notices, subscription popups, navigation menus removed
• Result: clean article text ~70-80% shorter than raw HTML text
368 How would you add real-time web monitoring for topic updates? monitoring, real-time • Scheduled task: daily check for new content on enriched topics
• RSS feeds: subscribe to topic-specific RSS for automatic updates
• Kafka: publish "new_content_available" event when updates detected
• WebEnrichmentAgent re-runs for topics with new content
• Teacher notification: "New resources available for [topic]"
369 What error handling exists in the WebIngestService? error handling, web • HTTP errors: retry 3 times with backoff for 5xx, skip 4xx immediately
• Parsing errors: if HTML parsing fails, try simpler text extraction
• Encoding issues: detect and convert non-UTF-8 content
• Timeout: 10s per page fetch, 60s total for all fetches
• Poison pages: pages that crash parser → blacklist URL, log error
370 How does the ResearchAgent handle conflicting web sources? conflicting, web sources • LLM synthesis prompt: "Sources may disagree. Present all perspectives fairly."
• Example: "Source [1] (2023) says X, but source [3] (2024) reports Y, suggesting the understanding has evolved."
• Prefer: more recent sources and higher-trust domains when contradictions exist
• Let student decide: present evidence, don't pick sides on evolving topics

Section 8: Curriculum & Learning Agents (50 Questions)¶

Key Concepts: CurriculumAgent · LearningAgent · Topic Dependency Graph · Topological Sort · Spaced Repetition · Syllabus Extraction · Topic Extraction · Critic-Learner-Performance Cycle · Kafka Integration

# Question Keywords Answer
371 What is the CurriculumAgent and what does it generate? CurriculumAgent, overview • 733-line agent — generates personalized learning paths from syllabi
• Input: syllabus document + student profile + available time
• Output: curriculum with daily goals, topic ordering, activities, resources
• Uses: topic dependency analysis, topological sort, spaced repetition
• LangGraph: 6-node pipeline from syllabus extraction to compiled curriculum
372 Describe the CurriculumAgent's 6-node LangGraph pipeline. pipeline, curriculum • Node 1: load_syllabus_topics — extract topics from uploaded syllabus
• Node 2: analyze_dependencies — LLM determines prerequisite relationships
• Node 3: assess_knowledge — gauge student's existing knowledge
• Node 4: build_learning_path — topological sort for valid ordering
• Node 5: generate_schedule — map topics to calendar days
• Node 6: compile_curriculum — combine all into structured Curriculum object
373 How does topic dependency analysis work? dependencies, DAG, topology • LLM analyzes topic list: "Which topics must be learned before which?"
• Creates DAG (Directed Acyclic Graph) of dependencies
• Example: "Variables" → "Functions" → "Classes" (must learn in order)
• Some topics independent: "File I/O" and "Error Handling" can be parallel
• Validation: check for cycles (A→B→C→A invalid) — reject and retry
374 How does topological sort determine learning order? topological sort, ordering • Input: DAG of topic dependencies
• Algorithm: Kahn's algorithm — start with topics having no prerequisites
• Output: valid ordering where every prerequisite comes before dependent topic
• Multiple valid orderings possible — choose based on difficulty progression
• Example: [Variables, Control Flow, Functions, OOP, Design Patterns]
375 What is the Curriculum data model? Curriculum, model • Curriculum: user_id, syllabus_id, total_days, daily_hours, start_date
• CurriculumTopic: topic title, description, difficulty (1-5), estimated_hours
• TopicDependency: topic_id → prerequisite_topic_id (DAG edges)
• DailyGoal: day_number, topics, activities, estimated_time
• Activity: type (read, practice, quiz), resource_link, duration
376 How does schedule generation work? schedule, generation • Input: ordered topics + total available days + daily study hours
• Bin packing: fit topics into days based on estimated_hours
• Constraints: max 3 new topics per day, review time allocated
• Spaced repetition: schedule review sessions for previously learned topics
• Output: day-by-day plan with specific activities and time allocations
377 What is the spaced repetition integration? spaced repetition, review • Source: spaced_repetition.py (20,244 bytes)
• Algorithm: SM-2 (SuperMemo 2) — adjusts review intervals based on recall quality
• Integration: CurriculumAgent inserts review sessions into daily schedule
• Data: SpacedRepetitionCard(topic_id, ease_factor, interval, next_review)
• Example: learned recursion day 1 → review day 2, day 4, day 8, day 16
378 How does syllabus extraction work? syllabus, extraction • Source: syllabus_extractor.py (33,138 bytes — second largest service)
• Input: uploaded PDF/DOCX syllabus document
• Process: OCR/text extraction → LLM identifies topics, subtopics, weightage
• Output: structured hierarchy {unit: [{topic: [{subtopic}]}]}
• Handles: diverse syllabus formats (tables, lists, paragraphs)
379 What is the LearningAgent and how is it a Type 5 agent? LearningAgent, Type 5 • 569-line self-improving agent — adapts based on student performance
• Type 5 (Russell & Norvig): has critic, learner, performance, and problem generator components
• Critic: analyzes assessment results to identify weak areas
• Learner: updates difficulty model based on critique
• Performance: generates appropriate-difficulty questions
• Problem generator: creates practice exercises for weak topics
380 Describe the Critic-Learner-Performance cycle. critic, learner, cycle • Trigger: student completes assessment → Kafka event
• Critic (analyze_performance): "Student scored 40% on recursion, 90% on arrays"
• Learner (update_learning): increase recursion difficulty weight, reduce array weight
• Threshold check: if topic score < 60%, trigger new questions
• Performance (generate_questions): create recursion-focused practice (not arrays)
• Deduplication: ensure new questions don't duplicate existing pool
381 How does the LearningAgent's LangGraph state machine work? LangGraph, learning • LearningState: topic_id, performance_data, memory, new_questions, difficulty_adjustments
• Node 1: load_topic_memory — load student's history for this topic
• Node 2: analyze_performance — critic function: score trends, weak subtopics
• Node 3: update_learning — adjust difficulty model parameters
• Node 4: check_threshold — decide if new questions needed
• Node 5: generate_questions — create questions at adjusted difficulty
• Node 6: deduplicate_questions — remove duplicates against existing pool
382 How does Kafka integration work for the LearningAgent? Kafka, trigger, event • Producer: AssessmentAgent publishes assessment.completed to Kafka
• Event payload: {user_id, topic_id, score, attempt_details, timestamp}
• Consumer: LearningAgent subscribes to assessment.completed topic
• Processing: consumer triggers LangGraph pipeline per event
• Async: student doesn't wait for LearningAgent — happens in background
• Idempotency: event_id checked to prevent duplicate processing
383 How does topic extraction work? topic extraction, Groq • Source: topic_extractor.py (36,594 bytes)
• Uses Groq (fast LLM) for rapid topic identification
• Input: text from syllabus or document
• Output: list of {topic, subtopics, difficulty, estimated_hours}
• Fast: Groq processes extraction in ~200ms for typical syllabus
• Structured output: JSON schema enforced for consistent parsing
384 What is the Curriculum Storage service? storage, curriculum • Source: curriculum_storage.py (22,284 bytes)
• PostgreSQL operations for curriculum CRUD
• Methods: save_curriculum(), get_active_curriculum(), update_progress()
• Progress tracking: marks topics as completed, updates daily goals
• Version control: curriculum changes create new version, keep history
385 How does knowledge assessment work? assessment, knowledge • Node 3: assess_knowledge in CurriculumAgent
• Student takes short diagnostic: 5 questions per major topic
• Results: classify each topic as known (>80%), partial (40-80%), unknown (<40%)
• Known topics: skipped in learning path (optional review)
• Partial topics: included with reduced time allocation
• Unknown topics: full coverage with activities
386 How would you implement adaptive curriculum adjustment? adaptive, adjustment • Track daily progress: topics completed vs scheduled
• Behind schedule: adjust remaining days (split content differently)
• Ahead of schedule: add enrichment activities or deeper topics
• LearningAgent feedback: if student struggles with topic, increase allocated time
• Re-run schedule generation weekly with updated parameters
387 Scenario: Student falls behind on their curriculum by 3 days. What happens? behind, scenario • Progress tracking detects: days 5-7 topics not marked complete
• Option 1: compress remaining schedule (more topics per day)
• Option 2: extend curriculum end date by 3 days
• Option 3: skip low-priority topics (lowest difficulty items)
• Student view: notification "You're 3 days behind. Would you like to adjust your plan?"
• CurriculumAgent re-generates schedule with updated constraints
388 How does the LearningAgent handle multiple students? multiple students, scaling • LearningAgent processes events per-student (Kafka message contains user_id)
• Each student has independent: topic memory, performance history, difficulty model
• No cross-student learning (privacy): student A's data doesn't affect student B
• Scaling: Kafka partitioned by user_id for natural parallelism
• Storage: per-student entries in PostgreSQL
389 How does difficulty adjustment work in the LearningAgent? difficulty, adjustment • Initial difficulty: topic difficulty from curriculum (1-5 scale)
• Adjustment factors: assessment score trend, time spent, attempts needed
• Score < 40% → increase difficulty weight (more practice needed)
• Score > 80% → decrease difficulty weight (student has mastered)
• Exponential moving average for smooth adjustment (no overreaction to one score)
• Result: question generation targets specific difficulty range
390 What is the multi-layer deduplication in the LearningAgent? deduplication, questions • Layer 1: exact text match against existing question pool
• Layer 2: semantic similarity (embedding comparison, threshold 0.9)
• Layer 3: structural similarity (same question type + same answer choices)
• Purpose: prevent repetitive questions that bore students
• Implementation: embed new question → search existing pool → reject if too similar
# Question Keywords Answer
391 How does the CurriculumAgent handle invalid dependency cycles? cycles, validation • After LLM generates dependencies: run cycle detection (DFS-based)
• If cycle found: remove the weakest dependency (lowest confidence) and retry
• Example cycle: "A requires B, B requires C, C requires A" — remove one edge
• Maximum 3 retry attempts — if cycles persist, use flat ordering (no dependencies)
• Log: cycle detection events for prompt improvement
392 How does activity generation work for each topic? activity, generation • Per topic: LLM generates 3-5 activities based on topic nature
• Activity types: read (textbook section), practice (exercises), quiz (self-test), watch (video link), discuss (forum prompt)
• Duration: estimated per activity based on topic complexity
• Resources: linked to uploaded documents or web enrichment content
• Personalization: activity mix adjusted based on learning style preference
393 How does the InterviewQuestionAgent differ from the LearningAgent? interview, learning, difference • InterviewQuestionAgent: 798 lines (largest agent) — focuses on interview prep
• LearningAgent: focuses on course mastery
• Interview: generates behavioral + technical interview questions at industry level
• Learning: generates academic questions at student's current level
• Interview: 8-node LangGraph pipeline vs Learning: 6-node
• Both use deduplication and Kafka triggers
394 What is the InterviewLearningState (22 fields)? InterviewState, fields • Standard fields: query, user_id, topic, performance_data
• Interview-specific: industry, role_level, company_type, question_type (behavioral/technical/system_design)
• Generation: difficulty_level, format (MCQ/open/coding), model_answers
• Quality: deduplication_hash, similar_question_ids, quality_score
• Metadata: generation_time, token_count, feedback_history
• 22 fields total — most complex state in the system
395 How does the spaced repetition SM-2 algorithm work? SM-2, algorithm • After each review: student rates recall quality (0-5)
• Quality ≥ 3: correct recall → increase interval
• Quality < 3: failed recall → reset interval to 1 day
• Ease factor: multiplier for interval growth (starts at 2.5)
• Updated per review: EF = EF + 0.1 - (5-q)*(0.08+(5-q)*0.02)
• Minimum EF: 1.3 (prevent intervals from growing too slowly)
396 How would you implement collaborative curriculum for a classroom? collaborative, classroom • Teacher creates master curriculum for entire class
• Each student gets personalized copy (adjusted by knowledge assessment)
• Shared milestones: all students complete Chapter 1 by Date X
• Individual pacing: some students move faster within milestones
• Teacher dashboard: see class progress distribution
397 How does the system handle conflicting prerequisite opinions from LLM? conflicting, prerequisites • Run dependency analysis 3 times with slightly different prompts
• Majority vote: dependency included if 2/3 runs agree
• Confidence score: percentage of runs that suggested relationship
• Low confidence dependencies: marked optional in the learning path
• Manual override: teacher can add/remove dependencies
398 What is the syllabus hierarchy extractor? hierarchy, syllabus • Extracts multi-level structure: Course → Unit → Chapter → Topic → Subtopic
• Uses: heading detection (font size, bold, numbering), indentation analysis
• PDF tables: rows become topics with columns as attributes (hours, difficulty)
• LLM fallback: if structure detection fails, LLM infers hierarchy from content
• Output: nested JSON structure used by CurriculumAgent
399 How does the LearningAgent generate questions at specific difficulty? difficulty, generation • Difficulty level injected into LLM prompt: "Generate questions at difficulty 3/5"
• Difficulty guidelines in prompt: Level 1 (recall facts), Level 3 (apply concepts), Level 5 (analyze/evaluate)
• Bloom's taxonomy alignment: remember → understand → apply → analyze → evaluate → create
• Validation: generated question assessed for actual difficulty (does it match target?)
• Adjustment: if validation says wrong difficulty, regenerate with adjusted prompt
400 Scenario: A new topic is added to the syllabus mid-semester. How does the curriculum adapt? mid-semester, scenario • Teacher updates syllabus → CurriculumAgent notified
• New topic extracted and dependency-analyzed against existing topics
• Insertion: placed in topological order based on dependencies
• Schedule: remaining days re-allocated to include new topic
• Student notification: "Your curriculum has been updated with [new topic]"
• Existing progress preserved — only future schedule changes
401 How does the LearningAgent prevent question pool staleness? staleness, question pool • Track: question usage count and student performance per question
• High-usage questions: flagged for potential retirement or variation
• Performance analytics: questions everyone gets right → too easy, remove
• Periodic: generate batch of new questions to refresh pool
• Kafka cron: weekly event triggers LearningAgent for all active topics
402 What is the design trade-off of the LearningAgent's self-improving approach? trade-off, self-improving • Pro: continuously adapts to student needs without manual intervention
• Pro: question difficulty naturally calibrates to student level
• Con: slow to adapt — needs multiple assessment data points
• Con: cold start — new students have no performance history
• Con: LLM-generated questions may have quality variance
• Mitigation: teacher-created questions supplement LLM-generated ones
403 How does the CurriculumAgent handle diverse syllabus formats? formats, syllabus, diversity • Challenge: syllabi are non-standardized (tables, prose, lists, PDF, DOCX)
• Step 1: document pipeline extracts raw text from any format
• Step 2: LLM parses text into structured JSON (regardless of original format)
• Prompt: "Extract all topics, subtopics, and their relationships from this syllabus text"
• Structured output: JSON schema enforced for consistency
• Fallback: if extraction fails, ask teacher to confirm/correct
404 How does the revision assessment agent work? revision, assessment, agent • Source: revision_assessment_agent.py (473 lines)
• Purpose: generate review tests based on spaced repetition schedule
• LangGraph: 6 nodes — load schedule → identify due topics → generate review → present → score → update repetition
• Daily flow: student opens app → due reviews triggered → mini-quiz → results fed back to SM-2
• Integration: RevisionAssessment updates SpacedRepetitionCard ease factors
405 How would you implement learning analytics for the CurriculumAgent? analytics, learning • Track per-student: topics completed, time spent, assessment scores, streaks
• Store in Cassandra: time-series data for longitudinal analysis
• Dashboard: line charts (progress over time), heat maps (topic mastery), bar charts (time distribution)
• Insights: "Student spends 2x expected time on recursion — may need more support"
• Teacher view: class-level aggregates, individual deep-dives
406 How does the LearningAgent handle the first assessment (cold start)? cold start, bootstrap • No historical data: default to medium difficulty questions
• First assessment covers broad topics at balanced difficulty (2-3-4 range)
• Results: immediately establish baseline performance per topic
• Aggressive adjustment: first few assessments cause larger difficulty shifts
• Stabilization: after 3-4 assessments, adjustments become smoother
407 What event flow happens via Kafka for the learning pipeline? Kafka, event flow, learning • Event 1: assessment.completed → LearningAgent processes performance
• Event 2: curriculum.topic_completed → update progress, trigger next activities
• Event 3: questions.generated → QuestionPoolAgent indexes new questions
• Event 4: review.due → scheduled trigger for spaced repetition reviews
• All events: at-least-once delivery with idempotency keys
408 How does the QuestionPoolAgent manage the question bank? QuestionPool, management • Source: question_pool_agent.py (241 lines)
• Operations: add questions, retire old questions, search by topic/difficulty
• Trigger conditions: pool size < threshold (50 per topic), new topic added, teacher request
• Deduplication: before adding, check for semantically similar existing questions
• Quality gate: questions with < 3.0 avg student rating retired
• Integration: feeds AssessmentAgent and RevisionAssessmentAgent
409 How does the difficulty calibration loop work end-to-end? calibration, loop, e2e • 1. Student takes assessment → score recorded
• 2. Kafka event → LearningAgent analyzes
• 3. Critic: "Score 40% on topic X (expected 60%)" → difficulty too high
• 4. Learner: reduce difficulty target for topic X from 4→3
• 5. Next assessment: LearningAgent generates level 3 questions for topic X
• 6. Student scores 75% → approaching target → minor adjustment
• 7. Converge: difficulty stabilizes when scores meet expectations
410 How would you add group study features to the CurriculumAgent? group study, feature • Shared curriculum goals: group of students assigned same learning path
• Peer activities: discuss topic X together, peer-review practice problems
• Leaderboard: gamification with points for completed topics
• Sync points: "All group members must complete Chapter 3 before moving on"
• Implementation: StudyGroup model, group progress tracking, shared Kafka events

Section 9: Assessment, Question Pool & Interview Agents (50 Questions)¶

Key Concepts: AssessmentAgent · LangGraph Pipeline · Question Generation · Safety Validation · QuestionPoolAgent · Deduplication · InterviewQuestionAgent · RevisionAssessmentAgent · Spaced Repetition · Trigger Conditions

# Question Keywords Answer
411 What is the AssessmentAgent and what does it do? AssessmentAgent, overview • LangGraph agent for generating course assessments (quizzes, tests, exams)
• Generates question sets based on topic, difficulty, format (MCQ, short answer, coding)
• Validates: each question has exactly one correct answer, options are plausible
• Safety: checks questions aren't biased, inappropriate, or misleading
• Stores generated assessments in PostgreSQL linked to classroom
412 Describe the AssessmentAgent's LangGraph pipeline. pipeline, assessment • Node 1: load_topic_context — fetch topic materials from RAG
• Node 2: generate_questions — LLM creates questions with answers
• Node 3: validate_questions — check correctness, format, duplicates
• Node 4: safety_check — moderation for harmful/biased content
• Node 5: compile_assessment — format into final assessment structure
• Conditional: if validation fails, regenerate specific questions
413 How does question generation safety work? safety, question generation • Post-generation: each question checked for bias, offensive content, misleading options
• MCQ validation: exactly 4 options, exactly 1 correct, distractors plausible but wrong
• Content check: no personal questions, no political opinion, no sensitive topics
• If flagged: question replaced with regenerated version
• Deterministic settings: temperature=0.1 for consistent, factual questions
414 What are the question format types supported? formats, question types • MCQ: 4 options with 1 correct answer, explanation for each option
• True/False: statement with correct answer and explanation
• Short Answer: question with model answer and grading rubric
• Fill-in-the-blank: sentence with missing term(s)
• Coding: problem statement with expected output and test cases
• Each format has its own generation prompt template
415 How does the AssessmentAgent ensure question uniqueness? uniqueness, deduplication • Step 1: hash-based exact match against existing question pool
• Step 2: semantic similarity check (embedding comparison, threshold 0.85)
• Step 3: structural check (same stem + same answer = duplicate)
• If duplicate found: regenerate with "different from: [duplicate text]" in prompt
• Maximum 3 regeneration attempts per question before moving on
416 How does the QuestionPoolAgent trigger question generation? trigger, conditions • Condition 1: pool size < 50 questions per topic → auto-generate batch
• Condition 2: new topic added to curriculum → initial pool creation
• Condition 3: teacher manually requests more questions
• Condition 4: LearningAgent identifies gap (no questions at difficulty level needed)
• Trigger: Kafka event questions.needed consumed by QuestionPoolAgent
417 What question generation metrics does the InterviewQuestionAgent track? metrics, interview • Questions generated per topic per day
• Deduplication rate (% rejected as duplicates)
• Student performance per question (discrimination index)
• Generate time: average LLM latency for question creation
• Quality: average student rating per generated question
• Coverage: % of curriculum topics with sufficient question pool
418 What is the 22-field InterviewLearningState? state, fields, interview • Core: query, user_id, session_id, topic_id, classroom_id
• Interview context: industry, role_level, company_type, question_category
• Generation params: difficulty, format, max_questions, model_answers
• Quality: dedup_hashes, similar_ids, quality_scores, feedback_history
• Pipeline state: context_chunks, generated_questions, validated_questions
• Metadata: start_time, token_count, retry_count, error
• Most complex state definition across all agents
419 How does the InterviewQuestionAgent's 8-node pipeline work? pipeline, interview, 8-node • Node 1: analyze_requirements — parse industry, role, difficulty requirements
• Node 2: load_topic_context — fetch relevant curriculum content via RAG
• Node 3: generate_behavioral — create behavioral interview questions
• Node 4: generate_technical — create technical questions with solutions
• Node 5: generate_system_design — create system design discussion questions
• Node 6: validate_quality — check relevance, difficulty, accuracy
• Node 7: deduplicate — multi-layer dedup against existing pool
• Node 8: compile_set — format into interview prep package
420 How does the RevisionAssessmentAgent integrate with spaced repetition? revision, spaced repetition • Daily trigger: check SpacedRepetitionCard.next_review for due cards
• Generate: mini-quiz (5-10 questions) on due topics
• Student takes quiz → scores recorded
• After quiz: SM-2 algorithm updates each card's interval and ease factor
• Good recall (quality ≥ 3): increase interval (review less frequently)
• Poor recall (quality < 3): reset interval to 1 day (review soon again)
421 How does the AssessmentAgent handle ambiguous questions? ambiguous, validation • Validation node: LLM reviews each question — "Could this be interpreted ambiguously?"
• If ambiguous: LLM rewrites to be more precise
• MCQ: ensure distractors are clearly wrong (no "all of the above" unless intended)
• Short answer: rubric includes acceptable alternative answers
• Coding: clear input/output specifications, edge cases documented
422 How does assessment difficulty selection work? difficulty, selection • Teacher sets: overall difficulty level for assessment (easy/medium/hard)
• Distribution: easy=20% Q at L1-2, medium=50% Q at L3, hard=30% Q at L4-5
• Adaptive: if generating for individual student, use LearningAgent difficulty data
• Bloom's alignment: easy=remember/understand, medium=apply, hard=analyze/evaluate
• Each question tagged with difficulty in metadata
423 How does automatic grading work? grading, automatic • MCQ/True-False: exact match comparison (automated, instant)
• Short answer: LLM evaluates against model answer + rubric
• Coding: run test cases against student output (if sandboxed execution available)
• Confidence score: grading certainty for short answers (human review if low)
• Edge case: partial credit for partially correct answers
424 Scenario: Generated MCQ has two correct answers. How to handle? validation, scenario • Validation Node catches: LLM verifies each option independently
• If two correct: regenerate with explicit instruction "ensure exactly ONE correct answer"
• Fallback: if regeneration still fails, flag for teacher review
• Prevention: prompt engineering with strict JSON schema for answer key
• Quality gate: questions with validation warnings go to teacher review queue
425 How does the assessment result pipeline work? results, pipeline • Student submits → Core service grades MCQ automatically
• Core publishes assessment.completed to Kafka with: user_id, topic_id, score, item_results
• LearningAgent consumes: analyzes per-topic performance
• RevisionAgent: schedules reviews for failed topics
• QuestionPoolAgent: tracks per-question discrimination (was question too easy/hard?)
• Student dashboard: shows score, explanations, weak areas
426 How would you add peer assessment capabilities? peer assessment, feature • Teacher enables: "Peer review for Assignment X"
• Submission: student uploads answer → randomly assigned 3 peers
• Peers: grade using rubric provided by assessmentAgent
• Scoring: average of peer scores, outliers removed
• Bias prevention: anonymous submissions, calibration exercises
• Data: peer assessments feed LearningAgent alongside teacher assessments
427 How does the question discrimination index work? discrimination, index, question quality • Track per-question: how many high-performers got it right vs low-performers
• Formula: D = (top_group_correct% - bottom_group_correct%)
• Good D (>0.3): question differentiates between strong and weak students
• Poor D (<0.1): question is too easy or too hard for everyone
• Negative D: question might be misleading (weak students get it right, strong don't)
• Action: retire questions with D < 0.1, review negative D questions
428 How does timed assessment work? timed, assessment • Assessment created with time_limit_minutes field
• Frontend: countdown timer starts when student opens assessment
• Auto-submit: when time expires, current answers submitted automatically
• Server-side: verify submission timestamp is within time window
• Grace period: 30 seconds for network latency
• Prevention: timer runs server-side too (no client-side cheating)
429 What is the assessment creation flow for teachers? creation, teacher, flow • Teacher selects: topic(s), difficulty, format(s), question count
• Request: POST /api/assessments/generate → Core → AI service
• AssessmentAgent: generates questions meeting specifications
• Preview: teacher reviews generated questions, can edit/remove/add
• Publish: assessment becomes available to students
• Manual: teacher can also create questions manually (bypass agent)
430 How does the system handle assessment retakes? retakes, assessment • Policy: teacher configures max retake count per assessment
• Each attempt: stored separately with attempt_number
• Question variation: if retaking, generate new questions (avoid memorization)
• Scoring: best score, latest score, or average (teacher configurable)
• Analytics: track improvement between attempts for LearningAgent data

Section 10: Core Service — Flask Architecture (70 Questions)¶

Key Concepts: Flask Application Factory · Blueprints · SQLAlchemy ORM · Flask-Migrate · JWT Authentication · token_required Decorator · Role-Based Access Control · Route Inventory · File Upload · Inter-Service Communication

# Question Keywords Answer
431 What is the Core Service and why is it built in Flask? Core Service, Flask • Primary backend for CRUD operations, auth, user management, classroom management
• Flask chosen for: mature ecosystem, SQLAlchemy integration, blueprint pattern
• Handles: authentication, enrollment, document management, assessment storage
• Synchronous request/response model — appropriate for database CRUD
• 178 files in core-service codebase
432 Describe the Application Factory pattern used. factory, pattern, Flask • create_app() function returns configured Flask application
• Benefits: multiple app instances (testing, production), deferred configuration
• Steps: create app → load config → init extensions → register blueprints → register error handlers
• Extensions initialized: SQLAlchemy, Migrate, JWT, CORS, Mail
• Returns: fully configured app ready for WSGI server
433 How are Flask Blueprints organized? Blueprints, organization • Each domain has its own blueprint: auth, classroom, curriculum, assessment, document, meeting, user
• Blueprint registration: app.register_blueprint(auth_bp, url_prefix="/api/auth")
• Benefits: modular code, separate route namespaces, independent testing
• Each blueprint in its own file: routes/auth.py, routes/classroom.py, etc.
• ~25 route files covering all API endpoints
434 What Flask extensions are used? extensions, Flask • Flask-SQLAlchemy: ORM for PostgreSQL database access
• Flask-Migrate: Alembic wrapper for database migrations
• Flask-JWT-Extended: JWT token management with refresh tokens
• Flask-CORS: Cross-Origin Resource Sharing for frontend access
• Flask-Mail: email sending for password reset, notifications
• Flask-Limiter: rate limiting on API endpoints
435 How does the JWT authentication flow work? JWT, auth, flow • Login: POST /api/auth/login with email + password
• Server: verify credentials → generate access token (24h) + refresh token (7d)
• Response: {access_token, refresh_token, user: {...}}
• Subsequent requests: Authorization: Bearer <access_token> header
• Token expired: POST /api/auth/refresh with refresh token → new access token
• Logout: add token to blacklist (Redis)
436 What is the token_required decorator? decorator, token, auth • Custom decorator wrapping Flask-JWT-Extended's @jwt_required()
• Extracts user from JWT, loads User model from database
• Injects current_user into route handler as parameter
• Returns 401 if: token missing, expired, blacklisted, user not found
• Usage: @token_required def get_profile(current_user):
437 How does Role-Based Access Control (RBAC) work? RBAC, roles, access • Three roles: student, teacher, admin
• Role stored in User model: role column
• @role_required("teacher") decorator: checks current_user.role == "teacher"
• Route protection: student can't access teacher-only endpoints
• Admin: full access to all endpoints + admin dashboard
• Granular: some endpoints allow both student and teacher (e.g., view classroom)
438 What routes does the auth blueprint expose? auth, routes • POST /api/auth/register — create new user account
• POST /api/auth/login — authenticate and get tokens
• POST /api/auth/refresh — refresh expired access token
• POST /api/auth/logout — blacklist current token
• POST /api/auth/forgot-password — send password reset email
• POST /api/auth/reset-password — set new password with reset token
• GET /api/auth/me — get current user profile
439 What routes does the classroom blueprint expose? classroom, routes • POST /api/classrooms — create classroom (teacher only)
• GET /api/classrooms — list user's classrooms
• GET /api/classrooms/:id — get classroom details
• POST /api/classrooms/:id/join — join with code (student)
• POST /api/classrooms/:id/leave — leave classroom
• GET /api/classrooms/:id/members — list enrolled students
• DELETE /api/classrooms/:id — delete classroom (teacher only)
440 What routes does the curriculum blueprint expose? curriculum, routes • POST /api/curriculum/generate — trigger curriculum generation (calls AI service)
• GET /api/curriculum/:id — get curriculum with daily goals
• PUT /api/curriculum/:id/progress — update topic completion status
• GET /api/curriculum/active — get user's active curriculum
• POST /api/curriculum/:id/reschedule — regenerate schedule with new dates
441 What routes does the assessment blueprint expose? assessment, routes • POST /api/assessments/generate — trigger assessment creation (calls AI service)
• GET /api/assessments/:id — get assessment with questions
• POST /api/assessments/:id/submit — submit student answers, auto-grade
• GET /api/assessments/:id/results — get graded results
• GET /api/assessments/history — list previous attempts
• POST /api/assessments/manual — teacher creates manual assessment
442 How does the document upload endpoint work? upload, document, endpoint • POST /api/documents/upload — multipart form data with file
• Validation: file type whitelist, size limit (50MB), MIME type check
• Storage: save to shared Docker volume (uploads_data)
• Database: create Document record (status = "uploaded")
• Trigger: call AI service /ai/documents/process to start pipeline
• Response: document ID + status for frontend polling
443 How does inter-service communication work (Core → AI)? inter-service, HTTP • Core service makes HTTP calls to AI service for AI features
• No authentication needed: both on same Docker network (trusted zone)
• Requests: httpx.post("http://ai-service:8000/ai/tutor/chat", json=payload)
• Timeout: 30 seconds for AI calls (LLM can be slow)
• Error handling: if AI service unavailable, return 503 to frontend
444 How does password hashing work? password, hashing, bcrypt • Library: bcrypt with work factor 12
• Registration: password_hash = bcrypt.hashpw(password.encode(), bcrypt.gensalt(12))
• Login: bcrypt.checkpw(password.encode(), stored_hash)
• Work factor 12: ~250ms per hash (slow enough to prevent brute force)
• Never stored: plaintext password never saved anywhere
445 How does the Flask error handling work? error handling, Flask • Custom error handlers registered for common HTTP errors
• @app.errorhandler(404): returns {"error": "Resource not found"} with 404
• @app.errorhandler(ValidationError): returns field-specific errors with 400
• @app.errorhandler(500): returns generic error, logs full traceback
• All errors return consistent JSON format: {error, message, status_code}
446 How does request validation work? validation, request • Marshmallow schemas define expected request format per endpoint
• @validate_request(LoginSchema) decorator validates before handler runs
• Field validation: required fields, type checking, length constraints
• Custom validators: email format, password strength, file type
• Errors: specific field-level error messages returned to frontend
447 Describe the SQLAlchemy session management. SQLAlchemy, session • Flask-SQLAlchemy manages sessions per-request automatically
• Session opened: when first database query executes in request
• Session committed: at end of successful request
• Session rolled back: on exception (automatic rollback)
• Scoped session: thread-local to prevent cross-request interference
• db.session.add(obj), db.session.commit(), db.session.rollback()
448 How does the file permission and access control work? file, permissions, access • Document model has classroom_id foreign key
• Access rule: user must be enrolled in classroom to access document
• Function: check_document_access(user, document_id) verifies enrollment
• Download endpoint: GET /api/documents/:id/download — checks access before serving
• Additional: teachers can access all classroom documents, students only their own and shared
449 How does the meeting integration work? meeting, Flask, integration • POST /api/meetings — create meeting with LiveKit room
• GET /api/meetings/:id/token — generate LiveKit access token for user
• POST /api/meetings/:id/end — end meeting, trigger recording processing
• GET /api/meetings/:id/recordings — list recordings for meeting
• GET /api/meetings/:id/transcript — get transcription (after processing)
• GET /api/meetings/:id/summary — get AI-generated summary
450 What is the enrollment and classroom management system? enrollment, management • Join by code: teacher shares classroom code → student enters code → Enrollment created
• Enrollment record: (user_id, classroom_id, role, enrolled_at)
• Roles within classroom: student (default), teaching_assistant (promoted by teacher)
• Leave: student can unenroll, removing access to classroom resources
• Capacity: no hard limit, but teacher can set max enrollment
# Question Keywords Answer
451 How does the Core Service handle database migrations? migrations, Alembic • Flask-Migrate wraps Alembic: flask db init, flask db migrate, flask db upgrade
• Auto-detection: compares Python models to current DB schema
• Migration files in migrations/versions/ — version controlled
• Production: flask db upgrade runs as pre-deployment step in CI/CD
• Down migrations exist but aren't guaranteed to work (forward-only in production)
452 How does pagination work across list endpoints? pagination, API • Query params: page (default 1), per_page (default 20, max 100)
• Implementation: Model.query.paginate(page=page, per_page=per_page)
• Response: {items: [...], total, page, per_page, pages}
• Cursor-based pagination NOT used (simpler offset-based for CRUD)
• Performance: fine for < 1M records with proper indexing
453 How does the Core Service handle file cleanup? file cleanup, storage • Currently: no automatic cleanup — files persist in Docker volume
• Enhancement: cleanup job to delete files after X days
• On classroom deletion: cascade delete documents → cleanup files
• Orphaned files: no tracking mechanism for disconnected files
• Production: S3 lifecycle policy for automatic old-file cleanup
454 Scenario: Teacher needs to bulk import students. How does the system handle it? bulk import, scenario • Enhancement: POST /api/classrooms/:id/import-students with CSV file
• CSV format: email, name (one per row)
• Process: create accounts for new emails, enroll existing users
• Response: {created: 15, enrolled: 12, errors: [{email, reason}]}
• Rate limit: max 500 students per import
• Notification: enrolled students receive email with classroom join code
455 How does the Core Service connect to the database? connection, database • Connection string from env var: DATABASE_URL=postgresql://user:pass@postgres:5432/ensurestudy
• SQLAlchemy engine config: pool_size=10, max_overflow=20, pool_timeout=30
• Connection pool reuses connections across requests
• Docker DNS: postgres resolves to PostgreSQL container IP
• Health check: query SELECT 1 on startup to verify connection
456 How does the Core Service log requests? logging, middleware • Flask before_request / after_request hooks
• Log: method, path, status code, duration_ms, user_id (if authenticated)
• Format: JSON structured logging for ELK/CloudWatch parsing
• Request ID: UUID assigned per request for correlation
• Sensitive data: password fields redacted from logs
• Log level: INFO for normal requests, ERROR for 5xx responses
457 How would you add webhook support to the Core Service? webhook, feature • Table: Webhook(url, events, classroom_id, secret, active)
• Events: assessment.submitted, document.uploaded, enrollment.created
• Trigger: after event, POST to webhook URL with event payload
• Security: HMAC signature using webhook secret for verification
• Retry: 3 attempts with exponential backoff for failed deliveries
458 How does the admin dashboard API work? admin, dashboard • Protected by: @role_required("admin") decorator
• GET /api/admin/stats — user count, classroom count, document count
• GET /api/admin/users — list all users with search/filter
• PUT /api/admin/users/:id — update user role, deactivate account
• GET /api/admin/moderation — view flagged content queue
• GET /api/admin/system — system health (DB connections, Redis status)
459 How does the Core Service handle data serialization? serialization, JSON • Model to_dict() methods: convert SQLAlchemy objects to JSON-serializable dicts
• Nested serialization: classroom.to_dict(include_members=True) for related data
• Date handling: ISO 8601 format for all timestamps
• Exclude fields: password_hash never included in serialization
• Response format: jsonify(data) with Content-Type application/json
460 How does the export/reporting feature work? export, reports • GET /api/assessments/:id/export — export results as CSV
• GET /api/classrooms/:id/report — classroom performance summary
• CSV generation: Python csv module or pandas DataFrame
• PDF reports: weasyprint for formatted documents
• Async: large exports processed in background, download link sent when ready
461 How does the Core Service handle configuration? config, Flask • Three config classes: DevelopmentConfig, TestingConfig, ProductionConfig
• Base class: shared settings (JWT expiry, pagination defaults)
• Env-specific: DEBUG, database URL, Redis URL, CORS origins
• Loading: app.config.from_object(config_class) based on FLASK_ENV
• Override: environment variables take precedence over config class
462 How does the health check endpoint work? health check, monitoring • GET /api/health — returns system health status
• Checks: PostgreSQL connection, Redis connection, disk space
• Response: {status: "healthy", db: "ok", redis: "ok", uptime_seconds: 3600}
• Docker: HEALTHCHECK CMD curl -f http://localhost:5000/api/health
• Used by: load balancer, Docker health monitoring, alerting system
463 How does the Core Service handle concurrent writes? concurrent, writes • SQLAlchemy: READ COMMITTED isolation level (default)
• Concurrent enrollment: unique constraint on (user_id, classroom_id) prevents duplicates
• Optimistic locking: not implemented (could add version column for curriculum updates)
• Race condition: two teachers update same classroom → last write wins
• Enhancement: add version column and check on update
464 How would you implement audit logging? audit, logging, compliance • Table: AuditLog(user_id, action, resource_type, resource_id, old_value, new_value, timestamp)
• Trigger: SQLAlchemy event listeners on after_insert, after_update, after_delete
• Track: who changed what, when, and what the old/new values were
• Query: GET /api/admin/audit?resource=user&action=update for admin review
• Retention: 90 days (configurable), older logs archived to cold storage
465 How does the user profile management work? profile, user management • GET /api/users/profile — get current user profile
• PUT /api/users/profile — update name, bio, avatar, preferences
• Avatar upload: stored in uploads volume, URL saved in profile
• Preferences: notification settings, language, learning style
• Privacy: students can't see other students' profiles (only teacher can)
466 How does the classroom code system work? classroom code, join • Teacher creates classroom → system generates unique 6-character alphanumeric code
• Code format: A1B2C3 (uppercase letters + digits, excluding confusing chars I/O/0/1)
• Student joins: POST /api/classrooms/join with {code: "A1B2C3"}
• Validation: check code exists, classroom not full, user not already enrolled
• Regenerate: teacher can generate new code (invalidates old one)
467 How does the notification system work in Flask? notification, system • Currently: basic email notifications via Flask-Mail
• Triggers: enrollment confirmation, assessment available, curriculum update
• Template: HTML email templates rendered with Jinja2
• SMTP: configured via env vars (MAIL_SERVER, MAIL_PORT, MAIL_USERNAME)
• Enhancement: push notifications via WebSocket or FCM for mobile
468 How does the search endpoint work? search, API • GET /api/search?q=topic&scope=classroom — cross-resource search
• Searches: documents (by title), topics (by name), assessments (by title)
• Implementation: PostgreSQL ILIKE for basic text search
• For semantic search: proxied to AI service Qdrant
• Response: grouped results by resource type
469 Scenario: The PostgreSQL connection pool is exhausted. What happens? connection pool, scenario • pool_timeout=30: new requests wait up to 30s for available connection
• If still exhausted: SQLAlchemy raises TimeoutError
• Error handler: returns 503 "Service temporarily unavailable"
• Monitoring: track active connections vs pool_size
• Fix: increase pool_size, optimize slow queries, add read replicas
470 How would you add GraphQL to the Core Service? GraphQL, Flask • Library: Graphene + Flask-Graphene
• Schema: define types mirroring SQLAlchemy models
• Queries: classroom, user, assessment with nested resolvers
• Mutations: createClassroom, submitAssessment
• Benefit: mobile clients can request exactly the data they need
• Route: Mount GraphQL at /api/graphql alongside REST
# Question Keywords Answer
471 How does the Core Service handle database transactions? transactions, database • Flask-SQLAlchemy: automatic transaction per request
• Explicit: with db.session.begin() for critical operations
• Nested transactions: db.session.begin_nested() for savepoints
• Example: create user + enrollment atomically — both succeed or both fail
• Rollback: automatic on exception, explicit db.session.rollback() on custom errors
472 How does rate limiting work in the Core Service? rate limiting, Flask • Flask-Limiter with Redis backend for distributed rate limiting
• Default: 100 requests/minute per IP for API endpoints
• Auth endpoints: stricter — 10 login attempts/minute (prevent brute force)
• Upload: 5 concurrent uploads per user
• Rate limit headers: X-RateLimit-Remaining, X-RateLimit-Reset in responses
473 How does the Core Service handle background tasks? background, tasks • Currently: limited background task support (primarily in AI service)
• Core service: synchronous request/response for most operations
• Document upload: async callback from AI service on completion
• Enhancement: Celery for email sending, report generation
• Alternative: Kafka producer for async event publishing
474 How does the data model handle soft deletes? soft delete, model • Some models have deleted_at timestamp column (nullable)
• Query: automatically filtered WHERE deleted_at IS NULL
• Restore: set deleted_at = NULL
• Benefits: data recovery, audit trail, referential integrity preserved
• Trade-off: queries slightly slower (extra filter), storage grows
• Not all models use soft delete — only critical ones (User, Classroom, Document)
475 How does the user registration flow work end-to-end? registration, e2e • Frontend: form with name, email, password, role, institution
• POST /api/auth/register: validate input → hash password → create User record
• Generate: access token + refresh token
• Optional: send verification email with confirmation link
• Response: {user, access_token, refresh_token}
• Frontend: redirect to dashboard, store tokens in localStorage
476 How does the Core Service handle CORS configuration? CORS, config • Flask-CORS: CORS(app, origins=["https://localhost:3000"], supports_credentials=True)
• Allowed methods: GET, POST, PUT, DELETE, OPTIONS
• Allowed headers: Content-Type, Authorization
• Credentials: supports_credentials=True for cookie/JWT transmission
• Production: restrict origins to production domain only
477 What database model inheritance strategies are used? inheritance, model, strategy • Concrete table inheritance: each model has its own complete table
• Mixins: TimestampMixin adds created_at, updated_at to all models
• SoftDeleteMixin: adds deleted_at column and filtered queries
• SerializationMixin: adds to_dict() method for JSON serialization
• No STI (Single Table Inheritance) — each model standalone
478 How does the Core Service handle file serving? file serving, static • Upload files: served via send_from_directory with access control
• Not directly accessible via URL — must go through auth middleware
• Download: GET /api/documents/:id/download checks permission then streams file
• Large files: send_file(path, as_attachment=True) for efficient streaming
• Production: CDN or S3 pre-signed URLs for better performance
479 How does the Core Service implement API versioning? versioning, API • Currently: no explicit versioning (v1 implied)
• Strategy: URL-based versioning recommended (/api/v1/, /api/v2/)
• Migration: keep v1 endpoints working while v2 develops
• Breaking changes: new version, deprecation notices for old
• Alternative: header-based versioning (Accept: application/v2+json)
480 How does the Core Service handle database seeding? seeding, test data • Seed script: populates database with test data for development
• Creates: admin user, sample teachers, sample students, classrooms
• Generates: sample enrollments, example documents, test assessments
• Idempotent: can run multiple times without duplicating data
• Triggered: flask seed-db CLI command or Makefile target
481 How does the email notification system work? email, notification • Flask-Mail: configured with SMTP credentials from environment
• Templates: Jinja2 HTML templates for consistent branding
• Transactional emails: password reset, email verification, enrollment confirmation
• Marketing emails: not implemented (out of scope)
• Production: use SES or SendGrid instead of direct SMTP
482 How does the Core Service handle input sanitization? sanitization, security • Marshmallow: type coercion and validation at schema level
• SQL injection: prevented by SQLAlchemy ORM (parameterized queries)
• XSS: not applicable (API returns JSON, not HTML)
• File upload: extension whitelist + MIME type verification
• User input: strip HTML tags from text fields
• Request size: max 50MB for uploads, 1MB for JSON body
483 Scenario: Need to add a notification preferences endpoint. Design it. notification, design, scenario • GET /api/users/notification-preferences — get current preferences
• PUT /api/users/notification-preferences — update preferences
• Schema: {email_assessments: bool, email_curriculum: bool, email_meetings: bool, frequency: "immediate" or "daily"}
• Model: NotificationPreference(user_id, channel, event_type, enabled)
• Apply: check preferences before sending any notification
484 How does the ORM relationship loading strategy work? lazy loading, eager, ORM • Default: lazy loading — related objects loaded only when accessed
• Eager loading: db.relationship('Enrollment', lazy='joined') for always-needed data
• Dynamic loading: lazy='dynamic' returns query object for filtering
• N+1 problem: prevented using db.session.query().options(joinedload(...)) for specific queries
• Strategy: lazy by default, eager for critical paths
485 How does the Core Service handle token blacklisting? token, blacklist, logout • Logout: add JWT jti (unique token ID) to Redis blacklist
• Key: blacklist:{jti}, expire: matches token TTL
• Check: @jwt.token_in_blocklist_loader callback checks Redis
• If blacklisted: 401 Unauthorized, user must log in again
• Refresh tokens: also blacklisted on logout for full session termination
486 How does the Core Service handle database connection failures? connection failure, resilience • Startup: retry database connection 5 times with 2s backoff
• In-request: SQLAlchemy raises OperationalError → returns 503
• Pool exhaustion: TimeoutError → 503 with retry header
• Reconnection: SQLAlchemy pool automatically tries new connections on next request
• Docker: depends_on + healthcheck ensures DB is ready before core starts
487 How would you add WebSocket support to the Core Service? WebSocket, real-time • Library: Flask-SocketIO or switch to ASGI (Quart or add standalone WS server)
• Use cases: real-time notifications, live classroom updates
• Authentication: verify JWT on WebSocket handshake
• Rooms: per-classroom rooms for broadcasting
• Alternative: keep Flask REST + add separate WS service (simpler)
488 How does the Core Service expose API documentation? API docs, Swagger • Currently: no auto-generated docs (recommended to add)
• Enhancement: Flask-RESTX or flasgger for Swagger/OpenAPI auto-generation
• Benefits: interactive API testing, client SDK generation
• Alternative: manually maintained Postman collection
• AI Service (FastAPI) gets this for free with docs_url="/docs"
489 What testing patterns exist for the Core Service? testing, patterns • Test client: app.test_client() for HTTP request testing
• Test database: SQLite in-memory for fast test execution
• Fixtures: pytest fixtures for user, classroom, enrollment creation
• Auth testing: helper to generate test JWT tokens
• Mocking: patch AI service calls with unittest.mock
• Coverage target: >80% line coverage
490 How does the Core Service handle timezone management? timezone, datetime • All timestamps: stored as UTC in PostgreSQL (TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE)
• API response: ISO 8601 UTC format (2024-01-15T10:30:00Z)
• Frontend: converts UTC to user's local timezone for display
• Never: store local time in database (prevents timezone bugs)
• SQLAlchemy: default=datetime.utcnow for auto-timestamps

Section 11: AI Service — FastAPI Architecture (70 Questions)¶

Key Concepts: FastAPI · AsyncIO · Router Inventory · SSE Streaming · Pydantic Models · Service Layer (89 files) · Model Preloading · Request Logging · Background Tasks · Inter-Service Communication

# Question Keywords Answer
491 What is the AI Service and why is FastAPI used? AI Service, FastAPI • Secondary backend for AI-powered features: agents, RAG, document processing
• FastAPI chosen for: native async/await, StreamingResponse for SSE, automatic OpenAPI docs
• Handles: all LLM interactions, embedding generation, vector search, agent execution
• Async by design: LLM API calls are I/O-bound → async/await prevents blocking
• 230+ files in ai-service codebase
492 Describe the FastAPI application setup. setup, FastAPI • main.py: creates FastAPI app instance with middleware registration
• Middleware: CORS, request logging, request ID injection
• Event handlers: @app.on_event("startup") preloads models, initializes agents
• Routers registered with app.include_router(router, prefix="/ai/...")
• Automatic docs at /docs (Swagger UI) and /redoc
493 How many routers exist and how are they organized? routers, organization • 25 routers across 7 categories:
• Agent routers: tutor, research, curriculum, assessment, learning, interview, notes (7)
• Document routers: upload, process, status, chunks (4)
• Search routers: semantic_search, vector_search, hybrid_search (3)
• Meeting routers: transcription, summary, recording (3)
• Auth routers: verify_token, user_context (2)
• Admin routers: health, metrics, cache (3)
• Utility routers: embedding, moderation, config (3)
494 How does the streaming SSE response pattern work? SSE, streaming, pattern • Endpoint returns StreamingResponse(event_generator(), media_type="text/event-stream")
• event_generator(): async generator yielding SSE-formatted strings
• Format: f"data: {json.dumps(event)}\n\n" per event
• Event types: content (tokens), sources, thinking, error, done
• Client: EventSource in browser processes events incrementally
• Keep-alive: empty comment ": keepalive\n\n" every 15 seconds
495 Describe the Pydantic model validation approach. Pydantic, validation • Request models: Pydantic BaseModel with field validators
• Automatic: FastAPI validates request body against Pydantic schema
• Example: class TutorRequest(BaseModel): query: str; session_id: UUID; classroom_id: str
• Validation errors: 422 with detailed field-level error messages
• Response models: response_model=TutorResponse for output schema documentation
496 How does the service layer architecture work? service layer, 89 files • 89 service files in app/services/: business logic separated from routes
• Categories: agent services (13), RAG services (8), embedding services (4), cache services (6), utility services (58)
• Pattern: Router → Service → Repository/External API
• Services are singletons: initialized at startup, shared across requests
• Dependency injection: FastAPI Depends() for service instances
497 How does model preloading work at startup? startup, preloading • @app.on_event("startup") runs async initialization
• Step 1: Load SentenceTransformer embedding model (~3 seconds, ~500MB)
• Step 2: Initialize LLM API clients (Gemini, Groq) with connection pools
• Step 3: Compile LangGraph state machines for all agents
• Step 4: Connect to Redis, Qdrant, verify database connections
• Total startup: ~10-15 seconds before accepting requests
498 How does the request logging middleware work? logging, middleware • Custom middleware: LoggingMiddleware registered in FastAPI
• Captures: method, path, status code, response time, request body size
• Request ID: X-Request-ID header injected if not present
• Format: JSON structured logging for parsing by log aggregators
• Performance: adds < 1ms overhead per request
499 How does FastAPI handle background tasks? background tasks, async • BackgroundTasks: FastAPI built-in for fire-and-forget tasks
• Usage: background_tasks.add_task(process_document, file_id)
• Document processing: kicked off after upload response sent to client
• Kafka consumer: runs as background asyncio task on startup
• Long-running: Kafka consumers for event-driven agent triggers
500 How does the dependency injection system work? DI, dependency injection • FastAPI Depends() for runtime dependency resolution
• Example: def get_qdrant(settings: Settings = Depends(get_settings)) → creates QdrantService
• Services: injected into route handlers as function parameters
• Benefits: testability (mock dependencies), separation of concerns
• Caching: @lru_cache for settings and service singletons
501 How does the AI Service handle concurrent requests? concurrency, async • FastAPI + uvicorn: async event loop handles concurrent I/O
• LLM API calls: async with httpx.AsyncClient() for non-blocking HTTP
• Database queries: async SQLAlchemy or async Redis client
• CPU-bound (embedding): runs in thread pool executor to avoid blocking event loop
• Rule: never use blocking I/O in async handlers
502 What endpoints does the tutor router expose? tutor, endpoints • POST /ai/tutor/chat — main tutoring endpoint (SSE streaming response)
• POST /ai/tutor/session/create — start new tutoring session
• GET /ai/tutor/session/{id}/history — get conversation history
• DELETE /ai/tutor/session/{id} — clear session
• POST /ai/tutor/feedback — submit response quality feedback
503 What endpoints does the document router expose? document, endpoints • POST /ai/documents/process — start document processing pipeline
• GET /ai/documents/{id}/status — get processing status and progress
• GET /ai/documents/{id}/chunks — get processed chunks for inspection
• DELETE /ai/documents/{id}/vectors — remove document vectors from Qdrant
• POST /ai/documents/reprocess — re-run processing on existing document
504 How does the search router work? search, router • POST /ai/search/semantic — vector similarity search in Qdrant
• POST /ai/search/keyword — BM25 keyword search
• POST /ai/search/hybrid — combined vector + keyword with RRF
• Request: {query, classroom_id, filters, top_k, score_threshold}
• Response: {results: [{chunk_text, score, document_id, page}], metadata}
505 How does the embedding router work? embedding, router • POST /ai/embedding/encode — encode text to vector
• POST /ai/embedding/batch — encode multiple texts
• GET /ai/embedding/model-info — get model name, dimensions, etc.
• Used internally and for debugging/testing
• Returns: 768-dimensional vector(s) as JSON array
506 How do Pydantic settings manage configuration? Pydantic, settings • class Settings(BaseSettings): loads config from environment
• Env prefix: AI_ → AI_LLM_MODEL=gemini-pro maps to settings.llm_model
• Validation: Pydantic validates types, applies defaults
• Nested: class RedisSettings(BaseModel) for grouped configuration
• Access: get_settings() function with @lru_cache() for singleton
507 How does the AI Service handle errors? error handling, FastAPI • Exception handlers: @app.exception_handler(HTTPException) returns JSON
• Custom exceptions: AgentError, EmbeddingError, QdrantError
• Structured: {"error": "Agent execution failed", "detail": "RAG timeout", "request_id": "..."}%
• SSE errors: stream {type: "error", message: "..."} event then close
• 500 errors: log full traceback, return sanitized message to client
508 How does the AI Service handle health checks? health, monitoring • GET /ai/health — basic health status
• GET /ai/health/detailed — check all dependencies (Qdrant, Redis, LLM APIs)
• Checks: embedding model loaded, Qdrant reachable, Redis connected, GPU available
• Docker: HEALTHCHECK interval 30s with 3-retry tolerance
• Returns: per-dependency status for debugging
509 How does request ID correlation work between services? request ID, tracing • Core service generates UUID X-Request-ID header
• Passed to AI service in inter-service calls
• AI service: extracts from header or generates new one
• All log entries include request_id field
• Debugging: search logs by request_id to trace full request path
• Future: OpenTelemetry for distributed tracing
510 How does the AI Service handle LLM streaming vs non-streaming? streaming, non-streaming • Streaming (tutor, research): StreamingResponse with async generator
• Non-streaming (assessment, classification): regular JSON response
• Decision: user-facing chat = streaming, background tasks = non-streaming
• Streaming benefits: lower perceived latency, progressive rendering
• Non-streaming benefits: simpler error handling, response caching
# Question Keywords Answer
511 How does the AI Service communicate back to Core Service? callback, inter-service • Document processing completion: POST to http://core-service:5000/api/documents/{id}/status
• Assessment results from agents: POST to /api/assessments/{id}/ai-results
• No auth needed: internal Docker network (trusted zone)
• Error handling: retry 3 times with backoff if core service unreachable
• Alternative: Kafka events for async communication
512 How does the caching layer work in the AI Service? caching, Redis, AI • Multiple cache services: ABCR, session, response, web content, embedding
• Pattern: check cache → if hit, return cached → if miss, compute → store in cache
• Implementation: @cache_result(ttl=300, prefix="abcr") decorator
• Invalidation: manual (document upload) or TTL-based (automatic)
• Memory efficient: only cache frequently accessed data
513 How does the AI Service handle API rate limits from LLM providers? rate limits, LLM • Gemini: track requests per minute, queue excess
• Groq: faster rate limits, used for quick classification tasks
• Strategy: per-key rate limiting with semaphore
• Fallback: if primary provider limited, switch to secondary
• Monitoring: log rate limit 429 responses for capacity planning
• Multiple keys: round-robin across API keys for higher throughput
514 How does the Kafka consumer integration work? Kafka, consumer, FastAPI • Background task: asyncio loop consuming from Kafka topics
• Started at app startup: asyncio.create_task(kafka_consumer.start())
• Topics: assessment.completed, document.uploaded, curriculum.updated
• Processing: deserialize event → route to appropriate agent
• Error handling: failed events logged, optionally re-queued
• Offset commit: after successful processing
515 How does the AI Service handle memory management? memory, management • Embedding model: ~500MB in memory (singleton, loaded once)
• LLM clients: lightweight (HTTP connection pools)
• Per-request state: TypedDict instances garbage collected after response
• Redis client: connection pool with max connections
• Monitoring: track process memory usage, alert if > 80% of container limit
516 Scenario: Need to add a new AI feature (quiz generation from images). Design the endpoint. design, scenario • New router: POST /ai/quiz/from-image
• Request: multipart form (image file + topic + difficulty + count)
• Pipeline: receive image → Gemini Vision extracts content → generate questions from content
• Response: JSON with generated questions
• Validation: image format check, size limit
• Reuse: existing question generation logic from AssessmentAgent
517 How does the admin metrics endpoint work? admin, metrics • GET /ai/admin/metrics — aggregate statistics
• Metrics: total requests, requests per agent, average latency, error rate, cache hit rate
• Token usage: per-model token consumption for cost tracking
• Time window: last hour, last day, last week
• Storage: metrics aggregated in Redis sorted sets
• Future: Prometheus metrics exporter for Grafana dashboards
518 How does the AI Service handle graceful shutdown? shutdown, graceful • @app.on_event("shutdown") handler triggered by SIGTERM
• Step 1: stop accepting new requests (let in-flight complete)
• Step 2: commit Kafka consumer offsets
• Step 3: close Redis and Qdrant connections
• Step 4: cleanup embedding model from memory
• Timeout: 30-second grace period before forced shutdown
519 How does the AI Service handle file downloads from Core Service? file, download, inter-service • Core Service stores uploaded files in shared Docker volume
• AI Service: reads files directly from mounted volume (no HTTP download needed)
• Path: /app/uploads/{classroom_id}/{document_id}/{filename}
• Alternative: if on different servers, Core Service provides pre-signed download URL
• Security: volume mount is read-only for AI Service
520 How does the moderation router work? moderation, router • POST /ai/moderation/check — check text for safety
• Request: {text, context, check_type}
• Response: {is_safe, category, severity, reason}
• Used by: TutorAgent internally, and Core Service for content moderation
• Fast: ~200ms using Groq model for quick classification
521 How does the AI Service handle model versioning? model, versioning • Embedding model version: stored in Qdrant collection metadata
• LLM model version: configured via environment variable
• On model change: startup check verifies compatibility with existing data
• Incompatible change: alerts admin, requires re-embedding
• Feature flags: AI_USE_NEW_MODEL=true for gradual rollout
522 How would you add WebSocket support to the AI Service? WebSocket, FastAPI • FastAPI native WebSocket support: @app.websocket("/ai/ws/tutor")
• Persistent connection: lower latency than SSE (no reconnection overhead)
• Bi-directional: server pushes tokens, client sends follow-up without new HTTP request
• Authentication: verify JWT on WebSocket handshake
• Trade-off: more complex connection management, but better real-time experience
523 How does the AI Service run in production? production, deployment • Server: uvicorn with gunicorn worker manager
• Workers: 4 uvicorn workers (async, so 4 workers handle 100+ concurrent requests)
• Command: gunicorn main:app -w 4 -k uvicorn.workers.UvicornWorker
• Docker: multi-stage build, slim Python image
• CPU/Memory: 2 vCPU, 4GB RAM minimum per instance
524 How does the AI Service manage external API keys? API keys, security • Stored in environment variables: GEMINI_API_KEY, GROQ_API_KEY, QDRANT_API_KEY
• Docker Compose: loaded from .env file
• Production: AWS Secrets Manager or HashiCorp Vault
• Never in code or config files committed to Git
• Rotation: update env var → restart container (no code change)
525 How does the AI Service handle timeouts? timeout, handling • Per-endpoint timeout: tutor chat (120s), document processing (1800s), search (30s)
• LLM API timeout: 60s per LLM call
• Implementation: asyncio.wait_for(agent.process(), timeout=120)
• Timeout response: SSE error event or HTTP 504 Gateway Timeout
• Retry: single retry for transient timeouts (LLM API slowness)
526 How does request queuing work under load? queuing, load, backpressure • FastAPI/uvicorn: async event loop naturally queues waiting coroutines
• LLM bottleneck: concurrent requests share LLM API connection pool
• Backpressure: if > N concurrent agent executions, new requests queued
• Semaphore: asyncio.Semaphore(50) limits concurrent LLM calls
• Client: receives 503 if queue too long (>60s wait)
527 How does the AI Service integrate with testing? testing, AI Service • Test client: TestClient(app) from FastAPI testutils
• Mock LLM: replace LLM client with mock returning fixed responses
• Mock Qdrant: in-memory vector store for testing retrieval
• Agent tests: verify LangGraph state transitions with mock dependencies
• Performance: load tests with locust against staging deployment
528 How would you add request prioritization? prioritization, queue • Priority levels: high (interactive tutor chat), medium (assessment generation), low (document processing)
• Implementation: priority queue with asyncio.PriorityQueue
• High priority: skip to front of LLM request queue
• Low priority: can be delayed up to 5 minutes during peak load
• Benefit: user-facing requests get faster response even under load
529 How does the cache router provide cache management? cache, management, admin • GET /ai/admin/cache/stats — cache hit rates, memory usage, key counts
• DELETE /ai/admin/cache/clear?prefix=abcr — clear specific cache type
• DELETE /ai/admin/cache/flush — clear all caches (requires admin auth)
• POST /ai/admin/cache/warm?topic=... — pre-populate cache for specific topic
• Monitoring: cache hit/miss ratio tracked per cache type
530 How does the AI Service handle CORS differently from Core Service? CORS, AI Service • FastAPI CORSMiddleware: similar to Flask-CORS but middleware-based
• Origins: same configuration as Core Service (both frontends communicate)
• SSE considerations: CORS must allow text/event-stream content type
• Credentials: allowed for JWT cookie transmission
• Pre-flight: cached for 3600 seconds to reduce OPTIONS requests

531 What frontend framework is used and why? Next.js, React • Next.js 14 with App Router — latest React framework with server-side rendering

536 How does Zustand state management work? Zustand, state • Lightweight alternative to Redux — simple stores with hooks
• Store: useAuthStore for user state, useChatStore for tutor conversation, useClassroomStore for active classroom

• Persistence: localStorage for auth tokens, memory for session data
• Benefits: minimal boilerplate, no providers/context needed, DevTools support
