Section 13: Proctoring & Soft Skills Systems (60 Questions)¶

Key Concepts: 8 Detectors · Lazy Loading · LightGBM Static Classifier · LSTM Temporal Predictor · Integrity Scoring · Flag Generation · Cheat Score · MediaPipe · Gaze/Posture/Gesture/Speech Analysis · HaGRID Dataset · Filler Words

# Question Keywords Answer
561 What is the proctoring system and what detectors does it use? proctoring, detectors • 8 AI-powered detectors monitoring exam integrity:
• Face detector: verifies student presence (face visible)
• Multi-face detector: flags if multiple people visible
• Gaze detector: tracks eye movement for looking away
• Object detector: identifies phones, books, secondary screens
• Tab switch detector: monitors browser focus changes
• Audio detector: flags unexpected voices/sounds
• Head pose detector: estimates 3D head orientation
• Lip movement detector: flags potential whisper/dictation
562 How does the detector lazy loading pattern work? lazy loading, performance • Not all 8 detectors loaded at once — would consume too much memory (~2GB total)
• Load on demand: detector initialized when first needed
• Caching: once loaded, stays in memory for session duration
• Unload: after session ends, release detector resources
• Benefits: assessment starts faster, lower baseline memory usage
• Pattern: if not self._detector_loaded: self._load_detector()
563 How does the LightGBM static classifier work? LightGBM, classifier, static • Input: single frame features (face present, head angle, gaze direction, objects detected)
• Output: anomaly probability (0.0-1.0) — how suspicious is THIS frame
• Training: labeled dataset of normal vs cheating behaviors
• Features: ~24 numerical features extracted from each frame
• Fast: ~1ms inference per frame (lightweight gradient boosted model)
• Purpose: quick per-frame anomaly scoring
564 How does the LSTM temporal predictor work? LSTM, temporal, predictor • Input: sequence of 30 frame features (1-second window at 30fps)
• Output: cheat probability considering temporal patterns
• Why LSTM: catches patterns LightGBM misses (e.g., repeated glancing = suspicious, single glance = normal)
• Architecture: 2-layer LSTM → Dense → Sigmoid
• Complements LightGBM: static + temporal for robust detection
565 How is the integrity scoring system designed? integrity, scoring • Start: each student begins assessment with 100% integrity score
• Deductions: per flag type — tab switch (-5), face missing (-10), multi-face (-15), phone detected (-20)
• Recovery: brief face absence (<3s) no deduction (bathroom break tolerance)
• Final score: minimum of 0%, displayed to teacher with flag details
• Threshold: score < 50% → assessment flagged for manual review
566 How does flag generation work? flags, generation, alerts • Each detector continuously reports: DetectionResult(detector_name, confidence, timestamp, evidence)
• Flag logic: if confidence > threshold AND duration > minimum → generate flag
• Flag types: WARNING (amber, minor), ALERT (red, significant), CRITICAL (exam paused)
• Evidence: screenshot/frame capture attached to flag for teacher review
• Flag aggregation: multiple warnings in short span escalate to alert
567 What is the cheat score and how is it calculated? cheat score, calculation • Composite score: weighted combination of all detector outputs
• Formula: cheat_score = 0.2*gaze + 0.25*face + 0.2*object + 0.15*audio + 0.1*tab + 0.1*head_pose
• Per-frame: LightGBM static score
• Per-window: LSTM temporal score
• Final: max(static, temporal) for each window
• Session score: average of window scores across exam
568 How does the face detection work? face, detection, MediaPipe • MediaPipe Face Detection: lightweight, real-time face localization
• Returns: face bounding box, confidence score, landmarks
• Checks: face present (yes/no), face count (single expected), face size (not too far)
• Face missing: flag after 5 seconds of no face detection
• Multiple faces: immediate flag (potential helper present)
569 How does gaze tracking work? gaze, tracking, eyes • MediaPipe Face Mesh: 468 facial landmarks including iris centers
• Gaze direction: estimated from iris position relative to eye corners
• Direction classification: center, left, right, up, down
• Threshold: sustained off-center gaze > 3 seconds → flag
• Calibration: brief calibration at exam start (look at screen corners)
570 How does posture analysis work in soft skills? posture, MediaPipe, soft skills • MediaPipe Pose: 33 body landmarks (shoulders, elbows, hips, etc.)
• Posture classification: sitting straight, slouching, leaning
• Metrics: shoulder alignment angle, head tilt, spine curvature estimate
• Scoring: 1-10 posture quality score per assessment window
• Feedback: "You tend to slouch when speaking. Try keeping your shoulders back."
571 How does gesture analysis work? gesture, HaGRID, hands • HaGRID dataset: hand gesture recognition training data
• MediaPipe Hands: 21 hand landmarks per hand
• Classified gestures: pointing, open palm, thumbs up, counting, etc.
• Soft skills use: assess hand gesture usage during presentations
• Feedback: "Good use of illustrative gestures" or "Try using more hand movements"
572 How does speech analysis work for filler words? speech, filler words • Audio analysis: speech-to-text (Whisper) → text analysis
• Filler detection: count "um", "uh", "like", "you know", "basically", "actually"
• Rate: fillers per minute (target: < 3/min for professional speech)
• Fluency score: based on filler rate, pause duration, speech pace
• Feedback: "You used 'um' 12 times. Try pausing silently instead."
573 How does the object detection work in proctoring? object, detection, YOLO • Model: YOLOv5 or similar lightweight object detector
• Target objects: mobile phone, book, second screen, earbuds, smart watch
• Confidence threshold: > 0.7 for phone detection to avoid false positives
• Frame rate: analyzed every 5th frame (6fps) to reduce compute
• False positive handling: teacher can dismiss incorrect detections
574 How does tab switch detection work? tab switch, detection • Browser API: document.visibilitychange event listener
• Detection: when page becomes hidden → timestamp recorded
• Duration tracking: how long tab was unfocused
• Threshold: unfocused > 5 seconds → flag generated
• Browser: also detects Alt+Tab, window minimize, screen switch
• Server-side: verified by comparing client timestamps with server
575 How does audio monitoring work? audio, monitoring • Browser: MediaRecorder API captures microphone audio
• Analysis: volume level, speech detection, multiple voice detection
• Whisper: real-time transcription to detect spoken content
• Flags: background speech (someone dictating answers), unexpected noise
• Privacy: audio analyzed in real-time, not permanently recorded
• Consent: student must grant microphone permission before exam
576 How is the proctoring data stored? MongoDB, proctoring, storage • MongoDB collection proctoring_frames: per-frame analysis results
• Structure: {session_id, timestamp, detectors: {face: {...}, gaze: {...}, ...}}
• Write rate: up to 30 documents/second per active session
• TTL index: automatically delete data after 90 days
• Session summary: aggregated results in proctoring_reports collection
577 How does the proctoring initialization flow work? initialization, setup • 1. Student opens proctored assessment → permission requests (camera, mic)
• 2. System check: verify camera/mic working, internet stable
• 3. Calibration: student looks at screen corners for gaze calibration
• 4. Environment scan: 360° room scan using camera (optional)
• 5. Detectors loaded lazily as assessment begins
• 6. Monitoring starts: frames analyzed continuously
578 Scenario: Student has poor lighting. How does proctoring handle it? lighting, scenario • Face detection confidence drops with poor lighting
• Threshold adjustment: confidence threshold lowered from 0.8 to 0.5
• Image enhancement: brightness/contrast adjustment before detection
• Warning: "Poor lighting detected. Please improve your lighting for accurate proctoring."
• Grace period: 60 seconds to fix lighting before flags start
• Fallback: if face consistently undetectable, flag for teacher review
579 How does head pose estimation work? head pose, estimation • MediaPipe Face Mesh: extract 3D face landmarks
• PnP (Perspective-n-Point): solve for head rotation angles (yaw, pitch, roll)
• Yaw: looking left/right (suspicious if > 30° for extended time)
• Pitch: looking up/down (checking notes below screen)
• Roll: head tilt (normal, not suspicious by itself)
• Combined with gaze: head turned + eyes looking away = stronger signal
580 How does the lip movement detector work? lip, movement, whisper • MediaPipe Face Mesh: extract lip landmarks (inner and outer contour)
• Mouth openness: distance between upper and lower lip landmarks
• Movement pattern: track openness over time for speaking detection
• Without audio: if lips moving but microphone hears nothing → potential muting/whisper
• With audio: cross-reference lip movement with detected speech → consistency check
# Question Keywords Answer
581 How does the soft skills assessment generate feedback? soft skills, feedback • Per skill area: gaze (eye contact), posture, gestures, speech fluency, filler words
• Scoring: 1-10 per area, composite score weighted average
• Feedback generation: LLM creates actionable feedback from raw scores
• Example: "Eye contact: 6/10. You maintained good eye contact 60% of the time. Try looking at the camera more during key points."
• Progress tracking: compare scores across multiple assessments
582 How does the system handle false positives in proctoring? false positive, handling • Teacher review dashboard: all flags with evidence (screenshot/frame)
• Dismiss button: teacher can dismiss false positive flags
• Learning: dismissed flags feed back to improve detector thresholds
• Student appeal: student can contest flags with explanation
• Tolerance: integrity score has buffer — single false positive doesn't fail student
583 How does the proctoring report work? report, proctoring • Generated after assessment completion
• Contents: integrity score, flag timeline, per-detector summary, evidence screenshots
• Timeline: minute-by-minute integrity graph
• Risk classification: low (>80%), medium (50-80%), high (<50%)
• Teacher action: review high-risk reports, decide on score validity
584 How does ML model training work for proctoring detectors? training, ML, proctoring • Source: ml/ directory with PyTorch training notebooks
• Data: synthetic + real labeled datasets
• LightGBM: trained on tabular frame features, fast training (<5 min)
• LSTM: trained on sequential frame data, longer training (~30 min)
• YOLO/MediaPipe: pre-trained, fine-tuned on education-specific data
585 How would you improve proctoring accuracy? improvement, accuracy • More training data: collect labeled examples from real exams
• Multi-modal fusion: combine video + audio + behavior for stronger signals
• Personalization: student-specific baselines (some people naturally look around more)
• Transformer model: replace LSTM with temporal transformer for better sequence modeling
• Active learning: uncertain detections flagged for human labeling
586 How does the speech fluency service work? speech fluency, service • Source: ml/inference_wrappers/speech_fluency_service.py
• Input: audio segment from assessment recording
• Process: Whisper transcription → text analysis → fluency metrics
• Metrics: words per minute, pause frequency, filler rate, vocabulary diversity
• Output: FluencyReport with per-metric scores and improvement suggestions
587 How does the system handle webcam permission denial? permission, webcam • If student denies camera: proctored assessment cannot start
• Clear message: "Camera access is required for proctored assessments"
• Retry: button to re-request permission
• Alternative: teacher can allow non-proctored mode for specific students
• Browser handling: different UX for Chrome, Firefox, Safari permission dialogs
588 How does the environment scan work? environment, scan • Before exam: student rotates camera 360° to show room
• Analysis: object detection on scan frames (looking for cheat materials)
• Recording: scan saved for teacher review if needed
• Duration: ~30 seconds required
• Optional: teacher can skip environment scan for lower-stakes assessments
589 Scenario: Student's internet disconnects mid-proctored exam. What happens? disconnect, scenario • Exam state: saved locally in browser (localStorage) as student answers
• Reconnection: auto-reconnect with SSE/WebSocket, resume proctoring
• Gap handling: disconnection period flagged as "monitoring gap"
• Timer: paused during disconnection if teacher allows (configurable)
• Data: pre-disconnection proctoring data already sent to server
• Post-reconnection: integrity score adjusted for unmonitored gap
590 How does the real-time proctoring data flow work? real-time, data flow • Frontend: captures frames (camera) → runs MediaPipe detection locally (browser)
• Results: detection results sent to AI service (not raw frames — bandwidth efficient)
• Payload: {timestamp, face_detected, gaze_direction, objects, head_pose, audio_level}
• AI Service: LightGBM + LSTM scoring → integrity score update
• Storage: each frame analysis in MongoDB
• SSE: integrity score streamed back to student UI
591 How does the ML training pipeline work? ML, training, pipeline • Source: ml/deep_learning_models.py (256 lines) — PyTorch model definitions
• Notebooks: 15 training notebooks for different models
• Data: synthetic data generation scripts for training
• Registry: trained model weights stored in ml/models/ directory
• Inference: wrappers load saved weights → serve predictions in AI service
592 How does synthetic data generation work? synthetic data, training • Purpose: generate labeled training data when real data is scarce
• Face data: augmented face images with various poses, lighting, expressions
• Cheat scenarios: simulated phone-in-hand, looking away, multiple people
• Quality: synthetic data bootstraps model, then fine-tuned on real data
• Generated: Python scripts in ml/data/ directory
593 How does model inference wrapping work? inference, wrapper • Base class: ModelWrapper with predict(input) interface
• Load: self.model = torch.load(weights_path, map_location='cpu')
• Preprocessing: input normalization, feature extraction
• Inference: model.eval(), torch.no_grad() for efficient prediction
• Postprocessing: threshold application, confidence scoring
• Thread-safe: model weights are read-only after loading
594 How does the system handle real-time vs batch proctoring analysis? real-time, batch • Real-time: per-frame LightGBM scoring + 1-second LSTM windows → immediate flags
• Batch: after exam ends, re-analyze full session with more expensive models
• Batch catches: patterns spanning minutes that real-time windows miss
• Report: batch analysis updates final integrity score
• Trade-off: real-time is approximate (fast), batch is thorough (slower)
595 How are ML model weights managed? model weights, management • Stored: ml/models/ directory with versioned filenames
• Docker: weights baked into AI service Docker image
• Size: total ~200MB for all proctoring models
• Updates: rebuild Docker image with new weights → redeploy
• Enhancement: model registry (MLflow) for version tracking and A/B testing
596 How does the gaze calibration process work? calibration, gaze • 4-point calibration: student looks at screen corners sequentially
• System records: iris position for each corner direction
• Calibration model: maps iris offset to gaze direction
• Per-student: calibration adjusts for eye shape, glasses, screen distance
• Duration: ~10 seconds, stored for session duration
• Skip: if calibration fails, use generic thresholds (less accurate)
597 How does the system handle students wearing glasses? glasses, accessibility • MediaPipe: handles glasses natively (face mesh works through glasses)
• Gaze tracking: slightly less accurate with glasses (lens refraction)
• Sunglasses: face detection may fail → request removal
• Reflective glasses: may confuse iris detection → lower confidence threshold
• Accessibility: no student penalized for wearing prescription glasses
598 What privacy considerations exist for proctoring? privacy, proctoring • Raw video: NOT recorded permanently (only detection results stored)
• Evidence frames: specific flagged frames saved for teacher review
• Data retention: proctoring data deleted after configurable period (default 90 days)
• Consent: explicit student consent required before proctoring activates
• GDPR: student can request deletion of all proctoring data
• Transparency: student can see their real-time integrity score
599 How would you add emotion detection for learning engagement? emotion, engagement • MediaPipe: face mesh provides expression features
• Emotions: happy, confused, frustrated, bored, focused, surprised
• Model: trained classifier on AffectNet or FER2013 dataset
• Use case: detect when student is confused → offer help
• Privacy concern: emotion tracking needs strong consent framework
• Not for proctoring: only for optional learning assistance
600 How does the soft skills system handle non-native speakers? non-native, speech, fairness • Filler word dictionary: adjustable per language
• Speaking pace: adjusted expectations for non-native speakers
• Accent handling: Whisper is robust across accents
• Fairness: scores compared against student's own baseline, not global standard
• Configurable: teacher can adjust scoring leniency for ESL students

# Question Keywords Answer
601 Describe the Docker development stack (13 services). Docker, development, services • Application: frontend (Node.js), core-service (Flask), ai-service (FastAPI)
• Databases: postgres, redis, qdrant, mongodb, cassandra
• Messaging: kafka, zookeeper
• Infrastructure: nginx (reverse proxy), livekit (video)
• Monitoring: kafka-ui (web dashboard for Kafka)
• All defined in docker-compose.yml (340 lines)
602 How does the production Docker stack differ? production, Docker • 6 services (vs 13 in dev): drops kafka-ui, lightweight zookeeper config
• Multi-stage Docker builds: smaller images (~200MB vs ~500MB)
• Health checks: automated restart on unhealthy containers
• Volumes: production-grade persistent storage
• Config: .env.prod with production secrets
• Source: docker-compose.prod.yml (208 lines)
603 What Docker volumes are defined? volumes, persistence • 10 named volumes: postgres_data, redis_data, qdrant_data, mongo_data, cassandra_data, kafka_data, livekit_data, nginx_certs, uploads_data, ml_models
• uploads_data: shared between core and ai service
• Named volumes: survive docker-compose down (preserved until explicit removal)
• Production: mapped to AWS EBS/EFS for durability
604 How does the Nginx reverse proxy configuration work? Nginx, proxy, routing • Routes: /api/* → core-service:5000, /ai/* → ai-service:8000
• Frontend: / → frontend:3000 (dev) or static files (prod)
• TLS: terminates HTTPS at nginx, internal traffic is HTTP
• WebSocket: upgraded connection for LiveKit and real-time features
• Headers: X-Request-ID, X-Real-IP forwarded to backends
605 What are the Kafka topics used in the system? Kafka, topics • assessment.completed — student finishes quiz → triggers LearningAgent
• document.uploaded — new document → triggers processing pipeline
• curriculum.updated — curriculum changes → triggers student notification
• questions.needed — question pool low → triggers QuestionPoolAgent
• review.due — spaced repetition reminder → triggers RevisionAgent
• analytics.event — student activity → stored in Cassandra
606 Describe the three event flow patterns. event flow, patterns • Pattern 1: Assessment → Learning Agent (Async AI Trigger)
Student submits → Kafka → LearningAgent analyzes → generates questions
• Pattern 2: Document Upload → RAG Pipeline (Async Processing)
Teacher uploads → Kafka → DocumentAgent processes → indexes in Qdrant
• Pattern 3: Student Activity → Analytics (Time-Series)
Student activity → Kafka → Spark streaming → Cassandra storage
607 How does the Kafka-Spark streaming pipeline work? Kafka, Spark, streaming • Source: kafka_spark_streaming.py
• Spark Structured Streaming reads from Kafka analytics.event topic
• Processing: window aggregation (5-minute windows), count events per student
• Output: aggregated metrics written to Cassandra daily_aggregates table
• Use case: real-time dashboard of student engagement metrics
608 How does Kafka topic creation work? Kafka, topic creation • Auto-creation: enabled in development (topics created on first produce)
• Production: topics pre-created with specific partition counts
• Partitions: assessment.completed = 6 partitions (by student_id hash)
• Replication: factor 1 in dev, factor 3 in production for durability
• Configuration: retention 7 days, log.cleanup.policy = delete
609 How does the Kafka consumer group work? consumer group, Kafka • Consumer group: ensurestudy-ai-consumers for AI service consumers
• Multiple consumers in group share partitions for parallelism
• Offset management: commit after successful processing
• Rebalancing: if consumer dies, partitions redistributed automatically
• Error handling: failed message → log error, commit offset (don't block queue)
610 How does the JWT security architecture work? JWT, security, auth • Access token: 24-hour expiry, contains user_id, role, classroom_ids
• Refresh token: 7-day expiry, used to get new access token
• Secret: JWT_SECRET_KEY from environment variable (256-bit random)
• Algorithm: HS256 (HMAC-SHA256) for token signing
• Blacklist: Redis-backed for logout and token revocation
• Verification: signature check + expiry check + blacklist check
611 How does RBAC (Role-Based Access Control) enforce permissions? RBAC, permissions • Three roles: student, teacher, admin
• Decorator: @role_required("teacher") blocks students from endpoint
• Classroom-level: teacher can only manage their own classrooms
• Data isolation: students see only their data, teachers see classroom data, admin sees all
• Implementation: role checked from JWT token, no database query needed
612 How is CORS configured across services? CORS, cross-origin • Core Service: Flask-CORS with specific origin whitelist
• AI Service: FastAPI CORSMiddleware with same origins
• Development: allows https://localhost:3000
• Production: only production domain allowed
• Credentials: enabled for JWT cookie transmission
• Pre-flight: OPTIONS responses cached for 1 hour
613 How does TLS/HTTPS work in development? TLS, HTTPS, mkcert • mkcert: generates locally-trusted development certificates
• Nginx terminates TLS: browsers see valid HTTPS
• Required because: WebRTC (LiveKit) needs HTTPS for camera/mic access
• Certificate files: mounted as Docker volume in nginx container
• run-local.sh script: auto-generates certs if missing
614 How would you set up monitoring and alerting? monitoring, alerting • Prometheus: scrape /metrics endpoints from each service
• Grafana: dashboards for latency, error rate, throughput, resource usage
• Alerts: PagerDuty/Slack for P95 latency > 5s, error rate > 1%, database connection failures
• Log aggregation: ELK stack or CloudWatch for centralized logging
• Distributed tracing: OpenTelemetry for cross-service request tracing
615 How does content moderation work at the system level? moderation, system • Layer 1: ModerationAgent checks user queries before processing
• Layer 2: file upload scanning for malware/inappropriate content
• Layer 3: generated response post-check for bias/inaccuracy
• ModerationLog: tracks all moderation decisions in PostgreSQL
• Admin dashboard: review queue for flagged content
• Severity: low (warn), medium (filter), high (block + notify)
616 What is the recommended AWS production architecture? AWS, production, architecture • Compute: ECS Fargate for containerized services (serverless containers)
• Database: RDS PostgreSQL (managed, automated backups)
• Cache: ElastiCache Redis with read replicas
• Storage: S3 for file uploads, CloudFront CDN for frontend
• Messaging: Amazon MSK (Managed Kafka)
• Vector DB: Qdrant on EC2 with EBS volumes (no managed offering)
• Networking: ALB for routing + TLS termination, VPC for isolation
617 How do the Makefile targets work? Makefile, DevOps, targets • make dev — starts development stack with logs
• make prod — builds and starts production stack
• make migrate — runs database migrations
• make test — runs all test suites
• make logs — tails all service logs
• make clean — removes containers, volumes, images
• make seed — populates database with test data
• 92 lines covering 12+ targets
618 How does the launch script handle LAN access? LAN, mkcert, access • run-lan.sh: detects local IP, generates mkcert cert for that IP
• Nginx config: updated with IP-based server_name
• Environment: NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL=https://192.168.1.100
• Use case: test mobile devices on same network
• Security: mkcert certs trusted only on machines with mkcert CA installed
619 How does Docker health checking work? health check, Docker • Each service defines HEALTHCHECK in docker-compose
• Core service: curl -f http://localhost:5000/api/health
• AI service: curl -f http://localhost:8000/ai/health
• PostgreSQL: pg_isready -U postgres
• Interval: 30 seconds, timeout: 10s, retries: 3
• Unhealthy: Docker restarts container after 3 failed checks
620 How does secret management work? secrets, management • Development: .env file in project root (gitignored)
• .env.example: template with dummy values for new developers
• Production: AWS Secrets Manager or SSM Parameter Store
• Docker: env_file directive in docker-compose
• Sensitive values: API keys, DB passwords, JWT secret
• Rotation: update env variable → restart affected service
# Question Keywords Answer
621 How does the network isolation strategy work? network, isolation • Single Docker bridge network: ensurestudy-net
• Only nginx publishes ports (443, 80) to host
• Databases: not exposed externally (no published ports)
• Service discovery: Docker DNS (container names as hostnames)
• Production: VPC with public/private subnets — databases in private subnet
622 How does the CI/CD pipeline work? CI/CD, pipeline • Recommended approach using GitHub Actions
• Workflow per service: build → test → Docker build → push to registry → deploy
• Database migrations: run as pre-deployment task in CI
• Frontend: build → upload to S3 → CloudFront invalidation
• Environment promotion: dev → staging → production with manual approval
623 How does log aggregation work across 13 services? logging, aggregation • Each service: JSON structured logging to stdout

• make logs: docker-compose logs -f --tail=100 for development
