624 How would you implement blue-green deployment? blue-green, deployment • Two identical environments: blue (current), green (new version)
• Deploy to green → run smoke tests → switch ALB to green
• Rollback: switch ALB back to blue if issues detected
• Database: both environments point to same database
• Migration: run schema changes before switching (backward compatible changes only)
625 How does the system handle SSL certificate renewal? SSL, certificate, renewal • Development: mkcert certs valid for 10 years (no renewal needed)
• Production: ACM (AWS Certificate Manager) auto-renews
• Let's Encrypt: certbot auto-renewal every 60 days
• Nginx: certificate files mounted via Docker volume
• No downtime: renewal happens before expiry, nginx reloaded
626 How does database backup strategy work? backup, disaster recovery • PostgreSQL: pg_dump daily to S3, WAL archiving for PITR
• Redis: RDB snapshots every 5 minutes + AOF for durability
• Qdrant: snapshot API to S3 weekly
• MongoDB: mongodump daily to S3
• Cassandra: nodetool snapshot + replication
• Testing: quarterly restore drills to verify backup integrity
627 How does the system handle database version upgrades? database upgrade, maintenance • PostgreSQL: pg_upgrade for major versions, or dump/restore
• Test: run upgrade in staging first with production data clone
• Downtime: scheduled maintenance window for major upgrades
• Minor patches: Docker image update, no downtime (rolling restart)
• Rollback plan: keep old data volume until new version verified
628 How does horizontal scaling work? scaling, horizontal • AI Service: run N instances behind nginx load balancer (round-robin)
• Core Service: run N instances with session-less design (JWT, no sticky sessions)
• Redis: supports multiple read replicas
• PostgreSQL: read replicas for reporting queries
• Qdrant: built-in sharding and replication
• Kafka: add partitions and consumers for throughput
629 How does the system handle resource limits? resource limits, Docker • Docker: deploy.resources.limits per service
• Core service: 1 CPU, 1GB RAM
• AI service: 2 CPU, 4GB RAM (embedding model needs memory)
• PostgreSQL: 2 CPU, 2GB RAM
• Qdrant: 1 CPU, 2GB RAM
• Total development: ~10 CPU, ~15GB RAM recommended
630 How would you implement canary deployment? canary, deployment • Deploy new version to 1 of N instances
• Route 10% of traffic to canary instance
• Monitor: error rate, latency, user feedback
• If healthy after 30 min: roll out to remaining instances
• If issues: roll back canary, investigate
• Implementation: nginx upstream weight-based routing
631 How does Spark Streaming integrate with the analytics pipeline? Spark, analytics • Source: Kafka analytics.event topic
• Processing: 5-minute micro-batch windows
• Aggregation: events per student, per topic, per activity type
• Output sink: Cassandra daily_aggregates table
• Metrics: active students, popular topics, peak usage hours
• Dashboard: Grafana reads from Cassandra for real-time analytics
632 How does Kafka ensure at-least-once delivery? Kafka, delivery, guarantee • Producer: acks=all ensures message written to all replicas
• Consumer: manual offset commit after processing
• If consumer dies before commit: message redelivered to another consumer
• Idempotency: consumer checks event_id to prevent duplicate processing
• Trade-off: at-least-once is simpler than exactly-once, acceptable for analytics
633 How would you add a message reply / dead letter queue? DLQ, dead letter • Failed messages: move to errors.{original_topic} dead letter topic
• Retry: separate consumer reads DLQ, retries with backoff
• Max retries: 3 attempts, then log permanently as unprocessable
• Monitoring: alert if DLQ grows beyond threshold
• Debug: DLQ messages include original event + error details
634 How does the service dependency chain work in Docker? dependency, Docker, startup • depends_on + health checks ensure ordered startup
• Order: databases → messaging → backends → proxy → frontend
• postgres starts → healthcheck passes → core-service starts → healthcheck → nginx starts
• If dependency fails: dependent service retries connection
• Total startup: ~30-60 seconds for full stack
635 How does the system handle cross-service API contract changes? contract, API, versioning • Challenge: core-service calls ai-service HTTP endpoints
• Contract: documented in shared API schema (Pydantic models)
• Breaking change: add new version endpoint, keep old working
• Testing: integration tests verify cross-service contracts
• Communication: API changes discussed before implementation
636 How would you implement feature flags? feature flags, rollout • Simple: environment variables FEATURE_NEW_AGENT=true/false
• Advanced: dedicated service (LaunchDarkly, Unleash) with user targeting
• Use cases: gradual agent rollout, A/B testing prompt versions
• Implementation: check flag before executing feature code
• Benefit: deploy code to production without activating feature
637 How does the system handle timezone differences across services? timezone, services • All services: use UTC internally for all timestamps
• Database: TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE column type
• API: ISO 8601 UTC format in all responses
• Frontend: converts UTC to user's local timezone for display
• Kafka events: timestamps in UTC milliseconds since epoch
• No timezone configuration needed per service — UTC everywhere
638 How does the system handle service version mismatch? version, mismatch • Risk: core-service v2 calls ai-service v1 endpoint that changed
• Prevention: API versioning with backward compatibility
• Detection: startup health check verifies connected service versions
• Mitigation: deploy ai-service first (new endpoints available), then core-service
• Rollback: if mismatch detected, revert to previous Docker image tag
639 How would you add distributed tracing? tracing, OpenTelemetry • OpenTelemetry SDK: add to both Flask and FastAPI services
• Trace ID: generated at nginx, propagated via traceparent header
• Span: each service creates spans for operations (DB query, LLM call, etc.)
• Backend: Jaeger or Zipkin for trace visualization
• Benefit: see full request path: frontend → nginx → core → ai → qdrant → LLM
640 How does the system handle graceful degradation? degradation, graceful • Qdrant down: tutor responds from general knowledge (no RAG)
• Redis down: skip caching, every request goes to LLM (slower)
• Kafka down: events published to dead letter / retry later
• MongoDB down: proctoring metadata not stored (exam continues)
• LLM API down: try fallback model, then cached response, then error message
• Principle: never prevent student from taking assessment due to AI failure

Section 15: LLM Strategy & Integration (40 Questions)¶

Key Concepts: Dual LLM Strategy · Gemini Pro · Groq Llama · Temperature Tuning · Token Management · Prompt Engineering · Structured Output · Streaming vs Batch · Cost Optimization · Model Selection

# Question Keywords Answer
641 Explain the dual-LLM strategy. dual LLM, Gemini, Groq • Gemini 1.5 Pro: main generation model — high quality, multi-modal, larger context window
• Groq (Llama 3): fast classification model — moderation, ABCR, topic extraction
• Rationale: use expensive/powerful model only where quality matters (generation), use cheap/fast model for supporting tasks
• Cost split: ~70% Gemini (generation), ~30% Groq (classification)
• Latency: Groq 50-200ms, Gemini 1-3s — critical for pipeline performance
642 Why Gemini specifically for generation? Gemini, generation, choice • Long context window (1M tokens) — can fit large RAG context
• Native multi-modal support — future image understanding
• Strong instruction following — reliable structured output
• Competitive pricing for quality level
• Google Cloud integration — potential for Vertex AI scaling
643 Why Groq specifically for classification? Groq, classification, speed • Fastest inference speed: ~100 tokens/sec
• Llama 3 on Groq: good enough for yes/no classification tasks
• Latency: 50-200ms per call (vs 1-3s for Gemini)
• Cost: ~$0.001 per classification (very cheap)
• Sweet spot: tasks where speed matters more than nuance
644 How is temperature configured per use case? temperature, tuning • Tutor generation: 0.3 (factual, accurate, not creative)
• Research synthesis: 0.7 (some creativity for combining sources)
• Question generation: 0.1 (deterministic, reproducible)
• ABCR classification: 0.0 (fully deterministic route selection)
• Moderation: 0.0 (safety decisions must be consistent)
• Follow-up suggestions: 0.8 (creative variety in suggestions)
645 How does token budget management work? token, budget, management • Total prompt budget: ~8000 tokens (Gemini generation prompt)
• System prompt: ~500 tokens (fixed)
• RAG context: ~2000 tokens (5 chunks × ~400 tokens each)
• Conversation history: ~4000 tokens (last N messages, truncated if needed)
• User query: ~200 tokens
• Instructions: ~300 tokens
• Budget tracking: tiktoken estimates, truncate largest section if over
646 How does the system prompt engineering work? prompt engineering, system • Tutor prompt: persona ("You are an educational AI tutor"), behavior rules, output format
• Versions: prompts versioned, A/B tested for quality
• Structure: role → context → task → constraints → format → examples
• Variables: classroom name, topic, student level injected dynamically
• Length: 300-500 tokens per system prompt (concise but comprehensive)
647 How does structured output work with LLMs? structured output, JSON • Gemini: native JSON mode with schema enforcement
• Groq: function calling / JSON mode
• Pattern: specify output schema in prompt + parse response
• Example: "Return JSON: {route: string, confidence: number, reasoning: string}"
• Validation: Pydantic model validates LLM output, retry if malformed
• Fallback: regex extraction if JSON parsing fails
648 What is the LLM fallback cascade? fallback, cascade, LLM • Level 1: Primary model (Gemini Pro) — normal operation
• Level 2: Fallback model (Groq Llama) — if Gemini unavailable/slow
• Level 3: Cached response — if all LLMs fail, serve similar cached answer
• Level 4: Error message — "AI service temporarily unavailable"
• Decision: automatic based on response time > 30s or API error
649 How does the prompt template system work? prompt template, system • Templates stored as Python f-strings or Jinja2 templates
• Variables: {query}, {context}, {history}, {instructions}
• Per-agent templates: each agent has specialized prompt templates
• Versioned: template changes are code changes (tracked in Git)
• Testing: regression tests verify prompt changes don't degrade quality
650 How does cost optimization work for LLM usage? cost, optimization, LLM • ABCR: prevents unnecessary RAG calls (saves embedding + retrieval + generation)
• Caching: ABCR cache, response cache, session cache reduce duplicate LLM calls
• Model selection: Groq for cheap tasks, Gemini only where needed
• Token budget: enforce limits to prevent runaway costs
• Monitoring: daily cost tracking per model, per agent
• Monthly budget: $50 dev, $500 production estimated
651 How does context window management work for long conversations? context window, long • Max history: 20 messages (but may exceed token budget)
• Strategy 1: drop oldest messages first (simple, current implementation)
• Strategy 2: keep first + last N messages (preserves initial context)
• Strategy 3: summarize old messages into paragraph (expensive but preserves info)
• Strategy 4: RAG over conversation history (search relevant past messages)
• Current: Strategy 1 with token counting via tiktoken
652 How does embedding generation differ from LLM generation? embedding vs LLM • Embedding: local model (all-mpnet-base-v2), no API call, ~50ms, cheap
• LLM: remote API (Gemini/Groq), network latency, 200ms-3s, per-token cost
• Embedding: deterministic (same input → same vector always)
• LLM: stochastic (same input → different tokens based on temperature, seed)
• Both: critical infrastructure, different scaling concerns
653 How would you add prompt versioning and A/B testing? A/B testing, prompt • Prompt registry: store multiple prompt versions per agent
• A/B split: randomly assign users to prompt version A or B (50/50)
• Metrics: track quality ratings, response time, citation accuracy per version
• Statistical significance: run for 1 week minimum, chi-squared test
• Winner: promote to 100%, archive losing version
• Implementation: feature flag per prompt version
654 How does the system handle LLM hallucination? hallucination, prevention • RAG grounding: provide factual documents so LLM has source material
• Low temperature: 0.3 reduces creative/inaccurate outputs
• Citation requirement: LLM must cite sources (verifiable claims)
• Post-processing: extract citations and verify against source chunks
• Disclaimer: "Based on course materials" framing
• No guarantee: can't fully prevent, but significantly reduced
655 How does retry logic work for LLM API calls? retry, LLM, API • Retry count: 2 retries (3 total attempts)
• Backoff: 1s, 3s exponential backoff between retries
• Retryable errors: 429 (rate limited), 500 (server error), timeout
• Non-retryable: 400 (bad request), 401 (auth error)
• On final failure: fallback to secondary model or cached response
• Circuit breaker: if >5 failures in 1 min, switch to fallback model
656 How does the system handle multi-language LLM responses? multilingual, LLM • Gemini: natively handles ~100 languages
• Strategy: respond in same language as query
• System prompt: "Respond in the same language as the student's question"
• RAG: documents may be in different language than query (cross-lingual retrieval challenge)
• Enhancement: translate document chunks to match query language before prompting
657 How would you fine-tune an LLM for this platform? fine-tuning, LLM • Data: high-rated tutor responses paired with queries
• Format: instruction-tuning dataset (query → response pairs)
• Platform: Google Vertex AI for Gemini fine-tuning, or open-source model
• Benefits: better domain-specific responses, lower hallucination
• Cost: one-time training cost + reduced per-token cost
• Trade-off: fine-tuning locks you into a model version
658 How does the LLM client abstraction work? client, abstraction • LLMClient base class: generate(prompt, config), generate_stream(prompt, config)
• GeminiClient(LLMClient): wraps Google AI Python SDK
• GroqClient(LLMClient): wraps Groq Python SDK
• Swappable: change LLM provider by implementing new client
• Config: LLMConfig(model, temperature, max_tokens, top_p, stop_sequences)
• Singleton: each client instance reuses HTTP connection pool
659 What token counting strategy is used? token counting, tiktoken • Library: tiktoken for GPT-compatible token counting
• Approximation: tiktoken counts may differ from Gemini's actual tokenizer
• Usage: pre-check prompt size before sending to LLM
• Alert: if prompt > 90% of model's context window, truncate history
• Billing: estimated token count for cost tracking (not exact, close enough)
660 How would you implement LLM output guardrails? guardrails, safety, output • Content filter: regex check for obviously harmful patterns in output
• Length guard: reject responses < 50 chars (too short) or > 5000 chars (too long)
• Format guard: verify expected structure (JSON parseable, markdown valid)
• Topic guard: verify response stays on-topic (embedding similarity with query)
• PII guard: regex scan for personal information leakage
• All guards: post-processing before response reaches student

Section 16: LiveKit & Meeting System (40 Questions)¶

Key Concepts: LiveKit Integration · Room Management · Token Generation · Recording Pipeline · Whisper Transcription · AI Meeting Summary · Chat Integration · Screen Sharing · WebRTC · Participant Management

# Question Keywords Answer
661 What is LiveKit and why is it used? LiveKit, overview • Open-source WebRTC infrastructure for real-time audio/video
• Handles: SFU (Selective Forwarding Unit), room management, recording
• Why LiveKit: self-hosted (data privacy), scalable, better than building WebRTC from scratch
• Docker: runs as a container in the development stack
• Features used: video rooms, recording, data channels for chat
662 How does room creation work? room, creation, LiveKit • Teacher creates meeting: POST /api/meetings → Core Service
• Core creates Meeting record in PostgreSQL with unique room_name
• LiveKit: room auto-created when first participant connects
• Room config: max participants, recording enabled, metadata
• Response: meeting ID + room info for frontend
663 How does LiveKit token generation work? token, LiveKit, JWT • POST /api/meetings/{id}/token — Core Service generates LiveKit JWT
• Token contains: room_name, participant_id, permissions (publish, subscribe)
• Signed with: LiveKit API secret (shared between Core and LiveKit server)
• Permissions: teacher can publish + record, student can publish + subscribe
• Expiry: token valid for meeting duration (max 8 hours)
664 How does the recording pipeline work? recording, pipeline • LiveKit: records room as composite (all participants in one video) or individual tracks
• Storage: recording saved to Docker volume (development) or S3 (production)
• Processing trigger: meeting ends → webhook → Core Service notifies AI Service
• AI processing: audio extraction → Whisper transcription → chunking → embedding
• Result: searchable, embedded meeting content in Qdrant
665 How does Whisper transcription work? Whisper, transcription • Model: OpenAI Whisper (medium or large-v2) for audio-to-text
• Input: extracted audio from meeting recording (WAV/MP3)
• Output: timestamped transcript {text, start_time, end_time, speaker_label}
• Speaker diarization: pyannote.audio for identifying different speakers
• Accuracy: ~90%+ for clear audio, lower for noisy environments
666 How does the AI meeting summary work? summary, meeting, AI • Input: full transcript from Whisper transcription
• Process: send transcript to Gemini with summary prompt
• Prompt: "Summarize this lecture transcript. Include: key topics, important points, action items"
• Output: structured summary with sections, highlights, key terms
• Storage: MeetingSummary record in PostgreSQL linked to meeting
667 How does in-meeting chat work? chat, meeting, data channel • LiveKit data channels: client ↔ SFU ↔ client text messaging
• Real-time: messages distributed to all participants via LiveKit
• Persistence: chat messages stored in PostgreSQL after meeting
• AI integration: teacher can ask AI questions during meeting via chat
• Chat history: available in meeting recording playback
668 How does screen sharing work? screen sharing, WebRTC • Browser: navigator.mediaDevices.getDisplayMedia() for screen capture
• LiveKit: screen share published as additional video track
• Priority: screen share displayed prominently, speaker video smaller
• Permission: any participant can share (teacher can restrict)
• Recording: screen share captured in meeting recording
669 How does participant management work? participant, management • Join: user connects to LiveKit room with generated token
• Track: LiveKit emits events for participant join/leave
• Mute: teacher can mute student's microphone
• Remove: teacher can remove participant from room
• Limit: configurable max participants per room (default 50)
• Role display: teacher shown with crown icon, students with standard avatar
670 How does the meeting scheduling system work? scheduling, meetings • POST /api/meetings/schedule — create future meeting with start/end time
• Notification: enrolled students receive email/notification before meeting
• Calendar: meeting appears in curriculum calendar view
• Auto-start: room created when teacher opens meeting link
• Recurring: weekly meetings with recurrence pattern (enhancement)
671 How does meeting attendance tracking work? attendance, tracking • LiveKit: participant join/leave events with timestamps
• Core Service: stores MeetingAttendance(meeting_id, user_id, joined_at, left_at, duration)
• Report: teacher can view attendance report per meeting
• Analytics: percentage attendance tracked in learning analytics
• Export: attendance as CSV for teacher records
672 How does the meeting transcript search work? transcript, search • Transcripts chunked and embedded → stored in meetings Qdrant collection
• Query: "What did the professor say about recursion?"
• Search: Qdrant semantic search within meeting chunks
• Result: relevant transcript segments with timestamps
• Player: clicking result jumps to that point in recording playback
673 How does WebRTC connectivity work behind firewalls? WebRTC, TURN, firewall • STUN: discovery of public IP for peer-to-peer connection
• TURN: relay server for connections that can't establish P2P
• LiveKit: built-in TURN support via ice_server configuration
• Corporate firewalls: may block UDP → LiveKit falls back to TCP/TLS
• Configuration: ICE servers defined in LiveKit server config
674 How would you add live transcription during meetings? live transcription, feature • Real-time: capture audio chunks → stream to Whisper → display subtitles
• Latency: 2-3 second delay for transcription
• Display: captions overlay at bottom of video feed
• Language: auto-detect language or teacher selects
• Accessibility: essential for hearing-impaired students
675 How does the meeting recording playback UI work? playback, recording, UI • Video player: HTML5 <video> with seek, speed control, full-screen
• Transcript sidebar: synchronized text showing current speaker's words
• Click-to-seek: click transcript segment → video jumps to timestamp
• Summary tab: AI-generated summary alongside video
• Download: option to download video file and transcript
676 How does room quality monitoring work? quality, monitoring, room • LiveKit: provides connection quality metrics
• Metrics: bitrate, packet loss, jitter, round-trip time per participant
• Adaptive: quality degrades gracefully (lower resolution) on poor connections
• Alert: if packet loss > 5%, warning to affected participant
• Teacher dashboard: real-time quality overview for all participants
677 How does the meeting end flow work? end, meeting, flow • Teacher clicks "End Meeting" → POST /api/meetings/{id}/end
• LiveKit: all participants disconnected, room closed
• Recording: finalized and saved to storage
• Core Service: Meeting record updated with end_time, duration, attendance
• Trigger: if recording enabled, start AI processing pipeline
• Notification: "Meeting recording and summary will be available shortly"
678 How would you add breakout rooms? breakout, feature, meeting • LiveKit: create sub-rooms dynamically during meeting
• Teacher creates N breakout rooms → students assigned or choose
• Each breakout: separate LiveKit room with limited participants
• Return: teacher can recall all students to main room
• Recording: each breakout recorded separately
• Use case: group discussions, pair programming exercises
679 How does the system handle large meetings (100+ participants)? large meeting, scaling • LiveKit SFU: designed to handle hundreds of participants
• Layout: only active speakers shown (not 100 video tiles)
• Audio: only top-N active speakers forwarded (selective forwarding)
• Bandwidth: each participant receives ~1Mbps (adaptive quality)
• Server: CPU scales with number of PUBLISHED tracks, not subscribers
• Enhancement: horizontal scaling with LiveKit cloud or multi-server
680 How does meeting integration with the tutor agent work? meeting, tutor, integration • During meeting: student can ask AI questions via chat panel
• Chat bot: routes questions through Orchestrator to TutorAgent
• Context: meeting topic (from meeting metadata) scopes the answer
• After meeting: transcript embedded and available for RAG
• Use case: student asks "Can you explain what the professor just said about X?"

Section 17: Cross-Cutting Scenarios & System Integration (60 Questions)¶

Key Concepts: End-to-End Flows · System-Wide Scenarios · Design Decisions · Trade-Off Analysis · Performance Optimization · Scalability · Migration Strategies · Disaster Recovery · Testing Strategies

# Question Keywords Answer
681 Walk through the complete user journey from sign-up to taking an AI-tutored lesson. e2e, user journey • 1. Register: POST /api/auth/register → user account created
• 2. Join classroom: enter code → enrollment record created
• 3. View dashboard: classroom page loads documents, curriculum
• 4. Open tutor: click "AI Tutor" → new session created
• 5. Ask question: "Explain binary search" → moderate → ABCR → RAG → generate → stream
• 6. Receive answer: streaming tokens build response with citations
• 7. Follow-up: click suggestion → new query through same session
• 8. Session data: stored for LearningAgent analysis
682 Walk through the complete document lifecycle. lifecycle, document, e2e • 1. Teacher uploads PDF → file stored, Document record created
• 2. AI service: 7-stage pipeline (validate → extract → OCR → chunk → embed → index → complete)
• 3. Ready: document searchable via RAG within seconds of completion
• 4. Student asks question → TutorAgent retrieves relevant chunks from this document
• 5. Teacher deletes document → vectors removed from Qdrant, record soft-deleted
• Total time: upload to searchable = 1-15 minutes depending on size/OCR
683 What happens when a teacher creates an assessment — full pipeline? assessment, pipeline, e2e • 1. Teacher selects: topic, difficulty, question count, format
• 2. Core Service: POST to AI Service /ai/assessment/generate
• 3. AssessmentAgent: load topic context → generate questions → validate → safety check
• 4. Generated questions returned to Core Service, stored in PostgreSQL
• 5. Teacher reviews: edit/approve generated questions
• 6. Publish: assessment available to students
• 7. Student takes: answers submitted → auto-graded
• 8. Kafka: assessment.completed → LearningAgent analyzes → adjusts difficulty
684 How does the system handle a classroom with 500 students simultaneously asking the TutorAgent? scale, concurrent, 500 • Load: 500 concurrent SSE connections to AI Service
• FastAPI: async handles concurrent I/O efficiently
• LLM bottleneck: 500 LLM requests saturate API quotas
• Semaphore: limits concurrent LLM calls to 50, queues rest
• ABCR cache: similar questions share classification (saves ~25% of calls)
• RAG cache: same classroom → similar chunks cached
• Response time: first 50 users get 3-4s, queued users get 10-30s
• Scale-out: add more AI service instances + increase API key quota
685 How would you migrate from monolith to microservices? migration, microservices • Currently: 2 services (core + ai) — semi-monolithic
• Step 1: extract document processing into separate microservice
• Step 2: extract meeting/recording service
• Step 3: separate agent execution service from RAG service
• Communication: REST for sync, Kafka for async (already in place)
• Database: each service gets own database (database per service pattern)
• Timeline: gradual migration, one service at a time
686 How does graceful degradation work when multiple services fail? degradation, multi-failure • Redis + Qdrant down: tutor can only answer from general knowledge (no cache, no RAG)
• Kafka down: no async events → but synchronous tutoring still works
• PostgreSQL down: nothing works (core dependency) → 503 for everything
• Priority: keep PostgreSQL + Core + AI alive for minimum viable service
• Fallback UI: "Some features are temporarily unavailable" with available features listed
687 Scenario: Need to add real-time collaboration (Google Docs-style). How? collaboration, real-time, design • Technology: CRDT (Conflict-free Replicated Data Types) or OT (Operational Transform)
• Library: Yjs or Automerge for collaborative text editing
• Transport: WebSocket for real-time changes, LiveKit data channels for in-meeting
• Backend: save document state periodically to PostgreSQL
• Existing integration: collaborative notes enhance TutorAgent context
• Scale: Yjs handles 50+ concurrent editors
688 How do you debug a slow tutor response (>10 seconds)? debugging, slow, performance • Step 1: check request ID in logs → find timing per pipeline node
• Step 2: Node 1 (moderate) slow? → Groq API latency issue
• Step 3: Node 2 (ABCR) slow? → cache miss + LLM classification
• Step 4: Node 3 (RAG) slow? → Qdrant search or embedding generation bottleneck
• Step 5: Node 4 (generate) slow? → Gemini API response time (most likely culprit)
• Fix: reduce context size, check API quotas, add caching
689 How would you implement a notification service? notification, service • Separate microservice consuming Kafka events
• Channels: email (SMTP/SES), push (FCM), in-app (WebSocket)
• Events: assessment available, curriculum update, meeting reminder, AI response ready
• Preferences: per-user notification settings (which channels, which events)
• Rate limiting: max 5 notifications per hour per user
• Templates: per-event-type notification templates
690 What are the biggest single points of failure? SPOF, reliability • PostgreSQL: complete system failure if down (most critical)
• Redis: degraded performance but system functions (no caching)
• Qdrant: no RAG — tutor gives generic answers
• Gemini API: no generation — fallback to Groq (lower quality)
• Nginx: no external access — all traffic blocked
• Mitigation: PostgreSQL replicas, Redis cluster, Qdrant replicas, multi-LLM fallback
691 How would you add multi-tenancy (multiple institutions)? multi-tenancy, SaaS • Schema-based: each institution gets own PostgreSQL schema
• Qdrant: separate collection per institution (or payload filter by tenant_id)
• Authentication: tenant identified by domain or subdomain
• Data isolation: guaranteed at query level (mandatory tenant filter)
• Billing: per-institution usage tracking (API calls, storage, LLM tokens)
• Customization: per-tenant branding, features, LLM provider preferences
692 How does the data consistency model work across services? consistency, distributed • Pattern: eventual consistency (not strong consistency)
• Document upload: Core creates record (immediate) → AI processes (eventually)
• Student sees: "Document processing..." until AI service completes
• Assessment: sync within Core (grading), async to LearningAgent (analysis)
• Trade-off: faster user response at cost of slightly stale data
• Consistency window: typically < 1 minute for most operations
693 How would you implement a plugin system for custom agents? plugin, extensible, agents • Agent interface: BaseAgent class with standard process(), get_info(), cleanup() methods
• Plugin loading: dynamic import from plugins/ directory
• Registration: plugin agent registered with Orchestrator at startup
• Configuration: JSON config file per plugin defining capabilities
• Marketplace: teachers install plugins from catalog
• Isolation: each plugin runs in sandboxed environment
694 Compare the codebase sizes and what they indicate. codebase, comparison, analysis • AI Service: 230+ files, 89 services — most complex, most business logic
• Core Service: 178 files — substantial CRUD, auth, data management
• Frontend: 200+ files — full-featured React application
• ML: 15 notebooks, 256-line model definition — ML training infrastructure
• Total: ~600+ files across the system
• Largest files: web_ingest_service.py (60K), syllabus_extractor.py (33K) → refactoring candidates
695 What are the key architectural trade-offs? trade-offs, architecture • Monolith vs Microservice: chose 2 services (simpler deployment vs full isolation)
• SQL vs NoSQL: chose polyglot (best tool per use case vs operational complexity)
• Sync vs Async: chose both (sync for user-facing, async for background processing)
• Self-hosted vs Cloud: chose self-hosted first (data privacy vs operational burden)
• RAG vs Fine-tuning: chose RAG (dynamic content vs static model)
• Each trade-off has rationale documented in architecture section
696 How would you handle a complete database corruption scenario? disaster, recovery, scenario • Detection: health check fails, queries return errors
• Step 1: alerting triggers, on-call engineer notified
• Step 2: switch to read replica (if available) for continued reads
• Step 3: restore from latest backup (pg_dump daily + WAL for PITR)
• Step 4: verify data integrity after restoration
• Step 5: replay Kafka events from retention period for missed updates
• RTO: ~30 minutes, RPO: ~1 hour (depending on backup frequency)
697 How would you add internationalization (i18n) to the platform? i18n, internationalization • Frontend: next-intl or react-i18next for UI translation
• Backend: error messages and email templates in multiple languages
• LLM: respond in user's language (detected from query or preference)
• RAG: multilingual embedding model for cross-language retrieval
• Database: user language preference stored in profile
• Priority: English first, then top 5 languages by user demand
698 How would you implement SSO (Single Sign-On)? SSO, authentication • Protocol: SAML 2.0 or OpenID Connect (OIDC)
• Integration: university's identity provider (Google Workspace, Azure AD, Okta)
• Flow: redirect to university login → receive assertion → create/update local user
• Benefits: students don't need separate password, IT manages accounts
• Implementation: flask-saml2 or python-social-auth
• Existing: supplement current email/password login (not replace)
699 What load testing strategy would you use? load testing, strategy • Tool: Locust (Python-based load testing framework)
• Scenario 1: 100 concurrent tutor chat requests → measure response time
• Scenario 2: 50 simultaneous document uploads → measure processing queue
• Scenario 3: classroom with 500 students → measure system behavior
• Metrics: P50, P95, P99 latency, error rate, throughput
• Target: P95 tutor response < 5 seconds, P95 search < 500ms
700 How does data privacy compliance work? GDPR, privacy, compliance • Data minimization: only collect necessary user data
• Right to erasure: admin can delete all user data (cascade across services)
• Data export: user can download their data in JSON format
• Consent: explicit consent for proctoring (camera/mic access)
• Encryption: at rest (AES-256 for databases) + in transit (TLS 1.3)
• Retention: configurable data retention policies per data type
# Question Keywords Answer
701 How would you implement API analytics and usage dashboards? analytics, API, dashboard • Track per-endpoint: request count, average latency, error rate
• Track per-user: API usage, feature engagement, session duration
• Storage: time-series data in Cassandra (already available)
• Dashboard: Grafana or custom admin page with charts
• Alerts: anomaly detection on usage patterns (sudden spike/drop)
702 How does the system handle schema migrations with zero downtime? zero downtime, migration • Phase 1: add new column (nullable) — backward compatible
• Phase 2: deploy code that writes to both old and new columns
• Phase 3: backfill old rows with new column values
• Phase 4: deploy code that reads only from new column
• Phase 5: drop old column (cleanup)
• Never: rename columns, change types destructively, drop columns in same deploy
703 How would you implement a DR (Disaster Recovery) site? DR, disaster recovery • Active-passive: primary in us-east-1, standby in us-west-2
• Database: PostgreSQL streaming replication to DR site
• Qdrant: periodic snapshot restoration to DR site
• Redis: separate instance at DR site, cold start acceptable
• DNS: Route53 health-based failover to DR site
• RTO: < 15 minutes, RPO: < 5 minutes
704 How would you add real-time collaborative whiteboard? whiteboard, collaboration • Library: tldraw or Excalidraw for whiteboard rendering
• Sync: Yjs CRDT for real-time collaboration
• Transport: LiveKit data channels during meetings
• Storage: whiteboard state saved to MongoDB (flexible schema)
• AI integration: OCR whiteboard content → answer questions about drawn diagrams
705 How would you implement usage-based billing? billing, SaaS • Track: LLM tokens used, documents processed, meeting minutes, storage
• Meters: per-institution usage counters in PostgreSQL
• Tiers: free (limited), standard (moderate), premium (unlimited)
• Billing: Stripe integration for subscription + usage-based add-ons
• Dashboard: institution admin sees usage and projected costs
706 Scenario: LLM provider changes pricing significantly. How to respond? pricing, migration, scenario • Short-term: optimize prompts to reduce token usage
• Medium-term: evaluate alternative providers (Claude, Mistral, local models)
• Long-term: abstract LLM client → swap provider with config change
• Already prepared: LLMClient abstraction means provider change is code-minimal
• Risk: model quality varies — extensive testing before switching
707 How would you add gamification to the platform? gamification, engagement • Points: earned for completing topics, assessments, study streaks
• Badges: achievements for milestones (50 questions, 7-day streak, top score)
• Leaderboard: classroom-level ranking (opt-in for privacy)
• XP system: experience points with levels
• Storage: gamification tables in PostgreSQL
• UI: progress bars, badge gallery, streak counter in dashboard
708 How would the system handle 10x growth (10K → 100K users)? 10x growth, scaling • Database: PostgreSQL read replicas, connection pooling (PgBouncer)
• AI Service: horizontal scaling (10 instances behind load balancer)
• Qdrant: sharding across multiple nodes
• Redis: cluster mode with multiple shards
• Kafka: increase partitions for higher throughput
• CDN: CloudFront for static assets
• LLM: increase API key quotas, add redundant providers
• Estimated cost: $500/mo → $3000/mo for 10x scale
709 How would you add offline mode for students without reliable internet? offline, PWA • PWA: service worker caches course materials
• Pre-download: student downloads topic materials for offline study
• Local storage: IndexedDB stores curriculum progress, notes, quiz attempts
• Sync: when online, sync local changes to server
• Limitation: AI tutor requires internet (LLM API dependency)
• Workaround: pre-generate FAQ answers for common questions, cache offline
710 What are the top 5 things you would improve about this architecture? improvement, critique • 1. Split WebIngestService (60K lines) into smaller, focused services
• 2. Add proper distributed tracing (OpenTelemetry) for debugging
• 3. Implement circuit breaker pattern for external API calls
• 4. Add comprehensive integration test suite (currently unit-focused)
• 5. Move from in-process embedding to dedicated embedding microservice (GPU-optimized)
• Bonus: add proper API versioning from the start

Section 18: Data Models, ORM & Schema Design (50 Questions)¶

Key Concepts: SQLAlchemy Models · Relationships · Polymorphic Queries · Index Strategy · Qdrant Payload Schema · Redis Data Structures · MongoDB Document Schema · Cassandra Table Design · Migration Strategy

# Question Keywords Answer
711 What are the core SQLAlchemy models in the system? models, SQLAlchemy, core • User: id, email, password_hash, name, role, institution, created_at
• Classroom: id, name, code, teacher_id (FK → User), description, max_students
• Enrollment: id, user_id (FK), classroom_id (FK), role, enrolled_at
• Document: id, title, file_path, classroom_id (FK), status, processed_at
• Assessment: id, title, classroom_id, topic, difficulty, time_limit, created_by
712 How do the table relationships work? relationships, foreign keys • User → Classroom: one-to-many (teacher creates classrooms)
• User ↔ Classroom: many-to-many via Enrollment table
• Classroom → Document: one-to-many (classroom has documents)
• Assessment → Question: one-to-many (assessment contains questions)
• User → AssessmentAttempt: one-to-many (student takes assessments)
• All relationships: defined with db.relationship() and lazy loading
713 What database indexes are defined and why? indexes, performance • users.email: unique index for login lookup
• classrooms.code: unique index for join-by-code
• enrollments.(user_id, classroom_id): unique composite for no duplicate enrollment
• documents.classroom_id: non-unique for classroom document listing
• assessments.classroom_id: non-unique for classroom assessment listing
• assessment_attempts.(user_id, assessment_id): composite for history queries
714 How does the Curriculum model cascade deletion work? cascade, deletion • Classroom deleted → Documents CASCADE deleted → Qdrant vectors cleaned separately
• User deleted → Enrollments CASCADE deleted → classroom stays (teacher still exists)
• Curriculum deleted → CurriculumTopics CASCADE deleted → progress records deleted
• Soft delete used for: User, Classroom, Document (recoverable)
• Hard delete used for: Enrollment, Progress, Session data (no recovery needed)
715 What is the Qdrant payload schema? Qdrant, payload, schema • document_id: string (links to PostgreSQL document record)
• classroom_id: string (mandatory filter for data isolation)
• chunk_text: string (original text for display and keyword search)
• chunk_index: integer (position within document)
• page_number: integer (source page for citation)
• source_type: string ("document", "notes", "meeting", "web")
• created_at: integer (timestamp for recency sorting)
716 How do Redis data structures map to features? Redis, data structures • Strings: JWT blacklist entries, ABCR classification cache
• Lists: conversation history per session (RPUSH, LRANGE, LTRIM)
• Hashes: topic anchor state per session (HSET, HGETALL)
• Sorted Sets: rate limiting counters (ZADD with timestamps)
• Sets: active session tracking per user
• Each structure chosen for its access pattern
717 What is the MongoDB document schema for proctoring? MongoDB, proctoring, schema • Collection proctoring_frames document: {session_id, user_id, timestamp, detectors: {face: {detected, confidence, bbox}, gaze: {direction, angle}, objects: [{type, confidence}]}, cheat_score: 0.0-1.0}
• Collection proctoring_reports: {session_id, user_id, integrity_score, flags: [{type, timestamp, evidence_url}], summary}
• TTL index on timestamp for automatic cleanup
718 How does the Cassandra table design work for analytics? Cassandra, analytics, design • daily_aggregates: partition key = (classroom_id, date), clustering = (user_id)
• Columns: events_count, topics_studied, time_spent, assessments_taken
• Read pattern: "Get all student activity for classroom X on date Y"
• Write pattern: Spark streaming writes aggregated per-user metrics
• Compaction: SizeTiered (write-heavy workload)
• No secondary indexes — all queries by partition key
719 What is the SpacedRepetitionCard data model? SpacedRepetition, model • SpacedRepetitionCard(user_id, topic_id, ease_factor, interval_days, repetitions, next_review_date, last_review_date, quality_history)
• ease_factor: starts at 2.5, minimum 1.3
• interval_days: days until next review (grows exponentially)
• repetitions: count of successful reviews (resets on failure)
• quality_history: list of 0-5 quality scores from past reviews
720 How does the Meeting model work with recordings? Meeting, model, recordings • Meeting: id, classroom_id, room_name, start_time, end_time, recording_path
• MeetingAttendance: meeting_id, user_id, joined_at, left_at, duration_seconds
• MeetingSummary: meeting_id, summary_text, key_topics, generated_at
• MeetingTranscript: meeting_id, transcript_json (timestamped segments)
• Relationship: Meeting → Attendance (one-to-many), Meeting → Summary (one-to-one)
721 How does the Question model support multiple formats? Question, format, model • Question: id, assessment_id, text, type (enum MCQ/TF/SHORT/CODING), difficulty
• QuestionOption: id, question_id, text, is_correct, explanation (for MCQ)
• CodingQuestion: question_id, starter_code, test_cases, expected_output
• Type column: discriminator for format-specific handling
• Polymorphic: different rendering and grading logic per type
722 How does the Notification model support multiple channels? Notification, model, channels • Notification: id, user_id, event_type, title, message, read, created_at
• NotificationPreference: user_id, event_type, email_enabled, push_enabled, in_app_enabled
• Delivery: check preference before sending via each channel
• Mark read: PUT /api/notifications/{id}/read
• Bulk: mark all as read, delete old notifications
723 How does the migration version control work? migration, version control • Alembic stores migration history in alembic_version table
• Each migration: unique hash ID + timestamp in filename
• Forward: upgrade() applies schema changes
• Backward: downgrade() reverses changes (best effort)
• Branch: multiple developers can create migrations that auto-merge
• Conflict: manually resolve if two migrations modify same table
724 How would you add audit fields to all models? audit, mixin, fields • Mixin: class AuditMixin: created_at, updated_at, created_by, updated_by
• created_at: set on insert (default=datetime.utcnow)
• updated_at: set on every update (onupdate=datetime.utcnow)
• created_by, updated_by: user_id from JWT context
• SQLAlchemy event listeners: automatically populate audit fields
725 Scenario: Need to add a "favorites" feature. Design the data model. favorites, design, scenario • Favorite: id, user_id (FK), resource_type (enum: "document", "assessment", "topic"), resource_id, created_at
• Generic: polymorphic association (one table for all favoritable types)
• Index: (user_id, resource_type) for "get all my favorite documents"
• Unique constraint: (user_id, resource_type, resource_id) prevents duplicates
• Query: Favorite.query.filter_by(user_id=current_user.id, resource_type="document")
726 How does the Curriculum-Topic-Activity relationship work? curriculum, relationship • Curriculum → CurriculumTopic (one-to-many, ordered by order column)
• CurriculumTopic → TopicActivity (one-to-many, activities for this topic)
• CurriculumTopic → TopicDependency (self-referential many-to-many, prerequisite relationships)
• Curriculum → DailyGoal (one-to-many, mapped to calendar)
• DailyGoal → CurriculumTopic (many-to-many, topics scheduled per day)
727 How does the system handle schema changes across multiple databases? cross-database, schema • PostgreSQL: Alembic migrations (version controlled, scripted)
• Qdrant: collection recreation (new collection → re-embed → swap)
• MongoDB: schemaless — no formal migration needed (handle in code)
• Redis: key format changes handled in code (backward compatible)
• Cassandra: CQL ALTER TABLE for additive changes
• Coordination: deploy order matters — databases before application code
728 What is the full list of Qdrant collections? Qdrant, collections, list • ensurestudy_documents: course materials (largest collection)
• ensurestudy_notes: student personal notes
• ensurestudy_meetings: meeting transcripts
• ensurestudy_web: web enrichment content
• ensurestudy_questions: (potential) embedded question pool for semantic dedup
• All: 768 dimensions, cosine distance, HNSW index
729 How does the user-classroom-enrollment relationship enforce constraints? constraints, enrollment • Unique constraint: one enrollment per (user_id, classroom_id)
• Foreign key: enrollment.user_id references users.id ON DELETE CASCADE
• Foreign key: enrollment.classroom_id references classrooms.id ON DELETE CASCADE
• Check constraint: role IN ('student', 'teaching_assistant')
• Business rule: teacher is not enrolled — ownership via classroom.teacher_id
730 How would you add multi-language content support? i18n, content, model • ContentTranslation(resource_type, resource_id, language_code, field_name, translated_text)
• Example: translate assessment question text into multiple languages
• Query: join with translations table, filter by user's language preference
• Fallback: if translation missing, show original language
• Management: admin interface for managing translations

Section 19: Edge Cases, Error Handling & Resilience (50 Questions)¶

Key Concepts: Race Conditions · Timeout Handling · Data Corruption · Graceful Degradation · Error Propagation · Retry Strategies · Circuit Breaker · Idempotency · Dead Letters · Input Validation Edge Cases

# Question Keywords Answer
731 What happens if a student submits an assessment twice? double submit, idempotent • Idempotency: submission endpoint checks for existing attempt with same assessment_id + user_id + attempt_number
• If duplicate: return existing result instead of re-grading
• Frontend: disable submit button after first click
• Race condition: unique constraint on (user_id, assessment_id, attempt_number) prevents DB duplicates
• Database transaction: ensure atomic insert of attempt
732 How does the system handle a malformed JWT token? JWT, malformed, error • flask-jwt-extended: catches DecodeError, returns 401 with "Invalid token format"
• Cases: missing header, corrupted base64, invalid signature, wrong algorithm
• Security: never reveal WHY the token is invalid (prevent attack information leak)
• Logging: log full decode error for debugging, but not in response
• Mitigation: token generated only by server, client stores without modification
733 What happens if Qdrant runs out of disk space? Qdrant, disk, error • Qdrant: refuses new upserts with error "insufficient storage"
• Impact: document processing fails at Stage 6 (indexing)
• Detection: health check monitors disk usage percentage
• Alert: notification when disk > 80% full
• Fix: increase volume size, or delete unused collections/old data
• Prevention: monitor collection sizes, set growth alerts
734 How does the system handle concurrent curriculum updates? concurrent, curriculum, update • Scenario: teacher and system both update curriculum simultaneously
• Current: last-write-wins (no optimistic locking)
• Risk: one update overwrites the other
• Fix: add version column, check version on update, reject stale updates
• Response: 409 Conflict with "Curriculum was modified. Please refresh."
• Enhancement: merge non-conflicting changes automatically
735 What happens if the embedding model produces NaN values? NaN, embedding, error • Cause: corrupted input text, encoding issues, model bug
• Detection: check for NaN after model.encode(): np.isnan(embedding).any()
• Handling: skip that chunk, log warning, continue with remaining chunks
• Impact: one bad chunk doesn't fail entire document processing
• Prevention: input sanitization before embedding (remove null bytes, normalize unicode)
736 How does the system handle SSE connection drops? SSE, reconnect, connection • Browser EventSource: auto-reconnects on connection loss
• Server: maintains no state per SSE connection (stateless streaming)
• Reconnection: client sends Last-Event-ID header to resume
• Current: no resume support — response starts over on reconnect
• Impact: interrupted tutor response must be regenerated
• Enhancement: buffer events server-side for resume support
737 Scenario: A circular dependency forms in the curriculum topic graph. circular, dependency, scenario • Detection: DFS-based cycle detection during topological sort
• Handling: remove weakest edge (lowest LLM confidence dependency)
• Retry: re-run topological sort without the removed edge
• Max retries: 3 — if still cyclic, flatten to simple linear ordering
• Prevention: better prompting in dependency analysis to avoid cycles
• Alert: log cycle detection for prompt improvement
738 What happens if Redis crashes mid-session? Redis, crash, session • Impact: conversation history lost, ABCR cache cleared, session state gone
• Tutor: starts fresh session (no memory of previous conversation turns)
• User experience: message "Session interrupted. Starting fresh conversation."
• Mitigation: Redis AOF persistence (recover recent state on restart)
• Architecture: session data is ephemeral by design — not critical data
739 How does the system handle invalid file uploads? invalid, upload, validation • Wrong extension: stage 1 rejects immediately (400 Bad Request)
• Extension spoofing: MIME type verification catches .exe renamed to .pdf
• Oversized: rejected before fully uploading (Content-Length check)
• Corrupted PDF: parsing fails gracefully at stage 2 → status "failed" with clear error
• Virus: ClamAV scan (optional) catches malware before processing
740 What happens if Kafka message processing fails for LearningAgent? Kafka, failure, processing • Message processing raises exception → caught by consumer error handler
• Error logged: includes message content, exception details, stack trace
• Offset: committed (don't block queue for one bad message)
• DLQ (recommended): move failed message to errors.assessment.completed topic
• Alert: if failure rate > 5%, trigger admin notification
• Impact: student doesn't get adaptive questions until message reprocessed
741 How does the system handle a student trying to access another classroom's data? access control, unauthorized • MCP context: extracted from JWT → contains enrolled classroom_ids
• API validation: check classroom_id in current_user.enrolled_classrooms
• Qdrant filter: automatically scoped by classroom_id (can't bypass in search)
• If unauthorized: 403 Forbidden — not 404 (clear error vs information hiding debate)
• Security: defense in depth — checked at route level AND query level
742 What happens if the LLM generates harmful content despite moderation? bypass, harmful, LLM • Layer 1: moderation check on input (catches most cases)
• Layer 2: output post-check (scan generated response for harmful patterns)
• If caught post-generation: response filtered, replaced with safe message
• If missed: user can report response → admin review queue
• Monitoring: random sampling of responses for content quality review
743 How does the system handle database connection pool exhaustion? pool exhaustion, database • SQLAlchemy: QueuePool with pool_size=10, max_overflow=20
• Max connections: 30 (10 pool + 20 overflow)
• Exhaustion: new requests get TimeoutError after 30 seconds
• Response: 503 "Service temporarily unavailable, please retry"
• Root cause: slow queries holding connections too long
• Fix: optimize queries, increase pool_size, add connection timeout
744 Scenario: Power outage during document processing. What state recovery? recovery, state, outage • Document stuck in "processing" state (never completed)
• On restart: health check identifies stuck documents (status=processing, age>30min)
• Recovery: reset status to "uploaded" → re-trigger processing pipeline
• Partial vectors: cleanup any incomplete Qdrant entries before reprocessing
• Data integrity: original file preserved — reprocessing is safe
745 How does the system handle memory leaks in long-running services? memory leak, monitoring • Detection: container memory usage steadily increases over hours/days
• Monitoring: Docker stats or Prometheus process_resident_memory_bytes
• Alert: if memory > 80% of container limit
• Mitigation: container restart (Docker health check + restart policy)
• Investigation: memory profiling with tracemalloc or memray
• Common cause: unbounded caches, unclosed connections, circular references
746 What happens if ABCR and TAL disagree about conversation state? conflict, ABCR, TAL • ABCR says "general_chat" but TAL has active topic anchor
• Resolution: TAL wins for context (it tracks conversation better)
• ABCR may reclassify on next query with TAL's anchor as input
• Design: ABCR runs first, TAL runs in same node → can override ABCR
• Rare in practice: they usually agree because both analyze same input
747 How does the system handle exceeding LLM rate limits? rate limit, handling • Detection: 429 response from LLM API
• Immediate: retry after Retry-After header value (usually 1-60 seconds)
• Backoff: exponential backoff if repeated 429s
• Fallback: switch to secondary LLM provider if primary exhausted
• Prevention: client-side rate limiting (don't send more than quota allows)
• Monitoring: track 429 rate, alert if > 10% of requests
748 What happens during Docker network partition (service can't reach another)? network, partition, Docker • Symptom: ConnectionRefusedError or timeout in inter-service calls
• Core → AI service timeout: return 503 to frontend "AI service unavailable"
• AI → Qdrant timeout: tutor responds without RAG (general knowledge only)
• AI → Redis timeout: skip caching, process without cache
• Docker DNS failure: container name doesn't resolve → restart Docker networking
749 How does the system handle timezone edge cases in assessments? timezone, assessment, edge • Problem: assessment deadline is "midnight" — but whose midnight?
• Solution: all deadlines stored as UTC datetime in database
• Frontend: converts UTC to student's local time for display
• Submission: compared against UTC deadline (server-side, no client clock trust)
• DST: not an issue because UTC doesn't have DST transitions
750 How does the system handle extremely long user input? long input, validation • Chat: max 2000 characters per message (frontend + backend validation)
• If exceeded: "Message too long. Please limit to 2000 characters."
• Assessment: short answer max 5000 characters
• Document upload: max 50MB file size
• Search query: max 500 characters
• Why: prevent abuse, control LLM costs, ensure reasonable response times
# Question Keywords Answer
751 How does the system handle emoji and special characters in queries? emoji, special chars • Unicode: full UTF-8 support throughout the stack
• Embedding: all-mpnet-base-v2 handles unicode including emoji
• Database: PostgreSQL UTF-8 encoding (handles all unicode)
• Search: Qdrant payload search requires text normalization
• Edge case: emoji-only query ("😊") → ABCR classifies as general_chat
• No filtering: let users express naturally, system handles gracefully
752 What happens if two teachers modify the same document simultaneously? concurrent, document, conflict • Upload: each upload creates new Document record (no conflict)
• Edit metadata: last-write-wins (no optimistic locking on document metadata)
• Delete: if both delete, second delete is no-op (already deleted)
• Qdrant: re-processing overwrites previous vectors (idempotent)
• Enhancement: add document locking or versioning for shared editing
753 How does the system handle API versioning backwards compatibility? API, backward, compatibility • Additive changes: new fields added with defaults (old clients unaffected)
• Deprecated fields: kept for 2 versions with deprecation warning header
• Breaking changes: new version endpoint (/api/v2/...) alongside old
• Client: version negotiation via Accept header or URL path
• Documentation: changelog per version with migration guide
754 What happens if Cassandra is unavailable? Cassandra, unavailable • Impact: analytics data not stored (time-series writes fail)
• Kafka consumer: events for Cassandra pile up (processed when available)
• Student experience: unaffected (analytics is background, not user-facing)
• Teacher dashboard: analytics charts show "Data temporarily unavailable"
• Recovery: Kafka retention (7 days) allows replay of missed events
755 How does the system handle file path traversal attacks? security, path traversal • Upload: files saved with UUID filenames (not user-provided names)
• Download: file served by ID lookup from database, not by path
• Validation: path components sanitized (os.path.basename(), reject ..)
• Docker: upload volume mounted with restricted permissions
• Never: construct file paths from user input directly
756 Scenario: Student's browser crashes during proctored exam. Recovery? crash, recovery, exam • Local state: answers saved in localStorage periodically (every 30 seconds)
• Server state: server-side timer continues (no extra time given by default)
• Reconnection: student refreshes page → assessment resumes from localStorage
• Proctoring: brief gap in monitoring logged as "browser_crash"
• Evidence: crash event timestamped for teacher review
• Time: elapsed time not paused (teacher can grant extension manually)
757 How does the system handle database deadlocks? deadlock, database • Detection: PostgreSQL detects deadlocks and kills victim transaction
• SQLAlchemy: raises OperationalError with deadlock error code
• Handling: automatic retry (up to 3 times) with random backoff
• Prevention: consistent transaction ordering (always User before Enrollment)
• Monitoring: track deadlock frequency — optimize if > 1/hour
758 What happens if embedding dimensions don't match between model and Qdrant? dimension, mismatch • Model change: new model produces different dimension vectors
• Qdrant: rejects vectors with wrong dimensions (400 Bad Request)
• Detection: startup check compares model dimensions with collection dimensions
• Fix: either revert model or recreate collection with new dimensions
• Migration: create new collection → re-embed → swap → delete old
• Prevention: model version stored in collection metadata
759 How does the system handle sudden traffic spikes? traffic spike, scaling • Auto-scaling: if using cloud, scale AI service instances based on CPU/request count
• Rate limiting: per-user limits prevent one user from consuming all resources
• Backpressure: semaphore limits concurrent LLM calls
• Queue: excess requests queued, served within timeout or rejected
• Cached responses: reduce backend load for common queries
• Alert: traffic monitoring alerts on-call engineer for manual intervention
760 How does the system prevent SQL injection? SQL injection, prevention • Primary: SQLAlchemy ORM generates parameterized queries (never raw SQL with user input)
• Secondary: Marshmallow validation sanitizes input types
• Tertiary: role-based access limits data scope
• Raw SQL: if ever needed, always use text() with bound parameters
• Testing: security audit includes SQL injection test cases
• No user input ever concatenated into SQL strings

Section 20: Design Patterns & Best Practices (50 Questions)¶

Key Concepts: Singleton · Factory · Strategy · Observer · Decorator · State Machine · Repository Pattern · Dependency Injection · SOLID Principles · Clean Architecture · Performance Patterns · Caching Patterns

# Question Keywords Answer
761 How is the Singleton pattern used in the system? Singleton, pattern • Embedding model: single instance loaded at startup, shared across requests
• LLM clients: one GeminiClient, one GroqClient shared globally
• Agent instances: Orchestrator, TutorAgent, etc. — one per process
• Implementation: module-level instantiation or @lru_cache on factory function
• Purpose: avoid re-loading expensive resources (model weights, connections)
762 How is the Factory pattern used? Factory, pattern • Application Factory: create_app() in Flask for configurable app instances
• Agent Factory: Orchestrator selects and instantiates appropriate agent per query
• LLM Factory: create_llm_client(provider) returns Gemini or Groq client
• Benefits: loose coupling, easy testing with mock factories
• Configuration-driven: factory behavior changes based on environment
763 How is the Strategy pattern used? Strategy, pattern • Chunking Strategy: different chunking approaches per document type
• Scoring Strategy: different scoring formulas for different search types
• LLM Selection Strategy: choose Gemini vs Groq based on task requirements
• Grading Strategy: different grading logic per question format (MCQ vs coding)
• Implementation: strategy interface, concrete implementations per variant
764 How is the Observer pattern used via Kafka? Observer, Kafka, pattern • Producer = Subject: publishes events (assessment completed, document uploaded)
• Consumer = Observer: subscribes to events, reacts accordingly
• Loose coupling: producer doesn't know who consumes events
• Multiple observers: single assessment event triggers LearningAgent + RevisionAgent + Analytics
• Decoupled: adding new observer requires zero changes to producer
765 How is the Decorator pattern used? Decorator, pattern, Python • @token_required: wraps route handler with JWT authentication
• @role_required("teacher"): adds role-based access control
• @cache_result(ttl=300): wraps function with Redis caching
• @validate_request(Schema): adds input validation before handler
• @retry(max_retries=3): adds retry logic to external API calls
• Python decorators: clean, composable, reusable middleware
766 How is the State Machine pattern used with LangGraph? State Machine, LangGraph • Each agent: LangGraph StateGraph defines nodes and edges
• State: TypedDict passed between nodes, modified immutably
• Conditional edges: if state["is_safe"] → continue, else → reject
• Pattern: explicit states, explicit transitions, no implicit control flow
• Benefits: debuggable (inspect state at each node), testable (mock state transitions)
767 How is the Repository pattern implemented? Repository, pattern, data • Implicit: SQLAlchemy models act as repositories (User.query.filter_by(...))
• Service layer: wraps ORM queries with business logic
• Example: ClassroomService.get_by_code(code) encapsulates query + validation
• Benefits: data access logic centralized, swappable storage backend
• Not strictly followed: some routes query ORM directly (pragmatic choice)
768 How is Dependency Injection used in FastAPI? DI, FastAPI, injection • Depends(): runtime dependency resolution for route handlers
• Example: def get_tutor_agent(settings = Depends(get_settings)) -> TutorAgent
• Testing: override dependencies with mocks using app.dependency_overrides
• Chain: dependencies can depend on other dependencies (resolution tree)
• Scope: request-scoped or singleton based on @lru_cache usage
769 How does the system follow SOLID principles? SOLID, principles • S (Single Responsibility): each agent handles one domain (Tutor tutors, Research researches)
• O (Open/Closed): new agents added without modifying Orchestrator core logic
• L (Liskov Substitution): all agents implement BaseAgent interface
• I (Interface Segregation): agents expose only relevant methods
• D (Dependency Inversion): services depend on abstractions (LLMClient), not concrete (GeminiClient)
770 What caching patterns are used throughout the system? caching, patterns • Cache-aside: check cache → miss → compute → store (ABCR, RAG results)
• Write-through: update cache on write (session state)
• TTL-based expiry: all caches have time-to-live for freshness
• Cache invalidation: clear classroom cache on document upload
• Semantic cache: embed query → find similar cached query → return cached result
• No cache stampede protection currently (potential improvement)
771 What is the middleware chain in the system? middleware, chain, request • Nginx: TLS termination → reverse proxy → request logging
• Flask: CORS → request ID → authentication → rate limiting → route handler → response serialization
• FastAPI: CORS → logging → request ID → route → response → error handling
• Order matters: authentication before route handler, logging wraps everything
• Each layer adds specific cross-cutting concern
772 How is the Template Method pattern used? Template Method, pattern • BaseAgent.process(): defines skeleton algorithm (moderate → route → retrieve → generate)
• Concrete agents: override specific steps (TutorAgent overrides generate() for educational tone)
• LangGraph compilation: common graph structure, agent-specific nodes
• Benefit: shared pipeline logic with customizable steps
• Example: all agents share moderation → the rest varies
773 How is the Pipeline pattern used in document processing? Pipeline, pattern, document • 7-stage pipeline: each stage transforms DocumentState
• LangGraph: explicitly defined pipeline as graph of nodes
• Each stage: independent, testable, can be skipped (conditional edges)
• Benefits: clear data flow, easy to add/remove/reorder stages
• Pattern: similar to Unix pipes — output of one stage is input of next
774 How does the system implement the Circuit Breaker pattern? Circuit Breaker, pattern • Currently: not formally implemented
• Recommended for: LLM API calls, Qdrant queries, external web fetches
• States: CLOSED (normal), OPEN (failing, reject requests), HALF-OPEN (testing recovery)
• Threshold: if 5 failures in 1 minute → open circuit for 30 seconds
• Benefits: fail fast instead of waiting for timeouts, allow service recovery
• Library: tenacity or custom implementation with Redis counters
775 What error propagation pattern is used? error, propagation, pattern • Agent errors: caught per-node, stored in state, propagated to response
• Fatal errors: raise exception → caught by FastAPI error handler → 500 response
• Non-fatal errors: logged, handled gracefully (e.g., RAG timeout → answer without RAG)
• User-facing: specific, helpful error messages (not stack traces)
• Internal: full error details logged for debugging
776 How does the system implement the Adapter pattern? Adapter, pattern • LLM Adapter: GeminiClient and GroqClient adapt different APIs to common LLMClient interface
• Database Adapter: SQLAlchemy abstracts PostgreSQL-specific SQL
• Search Adapter: Qdrant-specific API wrapped in SearchService interface
• Benefits: swap implementations without changing calling code
• Example: switch from Qdrant to Milvus by implementing new adapter
777 How is the Builder pattern used? Builder, pattern • Prompt Builder: step-by-step construction of complex LLM prompts
PromptBuilder().system(persona).context(chunks).history(messages).query(question).build()
• Curriculum Builder: Node 6 compiles curriculum from topics + schedule + dependencies
• Assessment Builder: combines questions + config + metadata into Assessment object
• Benefits: readable, testable, extensible construction of complex objects
778 How does the system implement the Saga pattern for distributed transactions? Saga, distributed • Example: document upload needs Core (DB) + AI (process) + Qdrant (index)
• Pattern: each step has a compensating action for rollback
• Success: Core creates record → AI processes → Qdrant indexes → Core updates status
• Failure at AI: Core marks document as "failed" (compensating action)
• Failure at Qdrant: AI retries indexing, if fails → Core marks as "partially_processed"
• Not formally Saga: manual compensation logic per operation
779 How does the event-driven architecture pattern benefit the system? event-driven, architecture • Decoupling: assessment service doesn't know about learning service
• Extensibility: add new consumers without changing producers
• Scalability: consumers scale independently
• Resilience: if consumer is down, events wait in Kafka
• Trade-off: eventual consistency, harder debugging (async events harder to trace)
• Current: selective event-driven (critical paths synchronous, background async)
780 How does the system implement the Proxy pattern? Proxy, pattern • Nginx: reverse proxy — single entry point routing to multiple services
• Cache proxy: Redis sits between application and expensive operations
• Auth proxy: @token_required decorator acts as protection proxy
• SSE proxy: Nginx must be configured to pass SSE connections through
• Benefits: access control, caching, load balancing at proxy layer

Section 21: Testing & Quality Assurance (50 Questions)¶

Key Concepts: Unit Testing · Integration Testing · E2E Testing · Mock Strategies · Test Fixtures · Coverage · Load Testing · Security Testing · CI/CD Testing · Agent Testing

# Question Keywords Answer
781 What testing framework is used? pytest, framework • Python: pytest for both Flask and FastAPI services
• Frontend: Jest + React Testing Library
• E2E: Playwright or Cypress for browser-based testing
• Load: Locust for performance and stress testing
• Coverage: pytest-cov for line coverage reporting
782 How do you test a LangGraph agent? agent, testing, LangGraph • Unit test each node independently with mock state
• Mock LLM: return fixed responses for deterministic tests
• State fixture: create known TutorState → pass through single node → assert output state
• Integration: run full graph with mocked external dependencies
• Edge testing: verify conditional edges trigger correctly
783 How do you mock the LLM for testing? mock, LLM, testing • unittest.mock.patch("app.services.llm_client.generate") returns fixed string
• Fixture: mock_llm_response = "Binary search divides the array in half..."
• Structured output mock: return valid JSON matching expected schema
• Streaming mock: async generator yielding fixed token chunks
• Benefits: deterministic tests, no API costs, fast execution
784 How do you test RAG retrieval quality? RAG, testing, quality • Test dataset: known documents + known queries + expected results
• Metrics: Precision@5, Recall@5, MRR (Mean Reciprocal Rank)
• Process: embed test docs → search with test queries → compare to ground truth
• Threshold: Precision@5 > 0.6, MRR > 0.7 for passing
• Regression: run on CI to catch quality degradation from code changes
785 How do you test Flask routes? Flask, route, testing • app.test_client() makes HTTP requests without running server
• Auth helper: generate test JWT for authenticated endpoints
• Database: SQLite in-memory for fast, isolated tests
• Fixtures: create test user, classroom, enrollment per test
• Assert: status code, response JSON structure, database state changes
786 How do you test FastAPI endpoints? FastAPI, testing • TestClient(app) from httpx for synchronous test calls
• Async tests: @pytest.mark.asyncio with httpx.AsyncClient
• Dependency override: app.dependency_overrides[get_db] = get_test_db
• SSE testing: collect streamed events, verify sequence and content
• Mock services: replace real services with deterministic mocks
787 How do you test database migrations? migration, testing • Test: run flask db upgrade on empty database → should succeed
• Test: run upgrade then downgrade → should return to previous state
• Test: apply migration on database with existing data → data preserved
• CI: migrations run against test database on every PR
• Edge: test migration with large datasets for performance
788 How do you test the document processing pipeline? document, pipeline, testing • Unit: test each stage independently (validation, chunking, embedding)
• Integration: process a known test PDF → verify chunks in Qdrant
• Edge cases: empty document, very large document, corrupted file, password-protected
• Mock: mock embedding model with fixed vectors for deterministic results
• Assertion: correct number of chunks, correct metadata, correct status
789 How do you test Kafka consumers? Kafka, consumer, testing • Mock Kafka: use confluent_kafka.MockConsumer or in-memory queue
• Publish test event → verify consumer processed correctly
• Assert: database state changes, downstream effects triggered
• Error testing: publish malformed event → verify graceful error handling
• Integration: run with real Kafka in Docker for CI tests
790 How do you test the proctoring ML models? proctoring, ML, testing • Known images: test with labeled images (face present, no face, multiple faces)
• Accuracy: > 90% on test set for face detection
• False positive rate: < 5% for object detection
• Temporal: test LSTM with known sequences → expected classifications
• Regression: model outputs compared against baseline on CI
791 How do you test authentication flows? auth, testing • Registration: valid data → 201, duplicate email → 409, weak password → 400
• Login: correct creds → 200 + tokens, wrong password → 401, unknown email → 401
• Token refresh: valid refresh → new access token, expired refresh → 401
• Logout: blacklisted token → 401 on subsequent requests
• RBAC: student accessing teacher endpoint → 403
792 How do you test SSE streaming endpoints? SSE, testing, streaming • TestClient: consume streamed response line by line
• Parse: split by data: prefix, JSON parse each event
• Verify: first events are content tokens, then sources event, then done event
• Error: simulate LLM failure → verify error event in stream
• Timeout: verify stream completes within expected time
793 What is the test coverage target? coverage, target • Line coverage: > 80% across both services
• Branch coverage: > 70% for critical paths
• Agent nodes: 100% coverage (each node has dedicated tests)
• Routes: 100% coverage (every endpoint has at least one test)
• Critical paths: authentication, authorization, data isolation
794 How do you test cross-service integration? integration, cross-service • Docker Compose: spin up full stack for integration tests
• Test: Core uploads document → AI processes → verify searchable via tutor
• Test: Core creates assessment → AI generates questions → verify returned
• Timeout: integration tests allow 30s for async processing
