795 How do you test data isolation (MCP)? isolation, MCP, testing • Test: user A in classroom 1, user B in classroom 2
• Verify: user A's tutor query → only classroom 1 documents returned
• Verify: user A cannot access classroom 2's documents via API
• Verify: Qdrant filter enforces classroom_id
• Critical: these are security tests — must pass 100%
796 How do you load test the tutor endpoint? load, testing, Locust • Locust: 100 concurrent users sending tutor queries
• Mock LLM: use fast mock to isolate infrastructure from LLM
• Measure: P50, P95, P99 response times, error rate, throughput
• Target: P95 < 5 seconds with real LLM, P95 < 500ms with mock
• Ramp: gradually increase from 10 to 500 users, observe breaking point
797 How do you test the frontend components? frontend, components, testing • React Testing Library: render component → interact → assert DOM changes
• Chat component: simulate typing → send → verify message appears
• Assessment: select MCQ option → submit → verify result display
• Mock: mock API responses with MSW (Mock Service Worker)
• Accessibility: axe-core tests for WCAG compliance
798 How do you test the curriculum generation pipeline? curriculum, testing • Input: known syllabus JSON → expected topic ordering
• Verify: topological sort produces valid ordering (no prerequisites after dependents)
• Verify: schedule fits within total days, no topic overcommitment
• Edge: single topic syllabus, 100-topic syllabus, circular dependencies
• Mock: LLM returns predetermined dependency graph
799 How do you implement contract testing? contract, testing • Consumer-driven contracts: AI service defines expected request/response schemas
• Core service tests: verify requests match AI service's expected format
• AI service tests: verify responses match Core service's expected format
• Tool: Pact or manual schema validation with Pydantic
• CI: contract tests run on every PR that changes API interfaces
800 How do you test error handling and resilience? error, resilience, testing • Chaos testing: randomly kill services, verify system degrades gracefully
• Timeout: inject artificial delays, verify timeout handling works
• Error injection: mock LLM to return errors → verify fallback behavior
• Network: simulate network partition → verify error messages
• Recovery: kill service → restart → verify resumes correctly

Section 22: Performance Optimization & Scalability (50 Questions)¶

Key Concepts: Latency Budgets · Caching Hierarchy · Connection Pooling · Async I/O · Batch Processing · Query Optimization · Horizontal Scaling · Bottleneck Analysis · Resource Management · Monitoring

# Question Keywords Answer
801 What is the end-to-end latency budget for a tutor response? latency, budget • Total target: 3-5 seconds for first token (streaming)
• Node 1 (moderate): ~200ms (Groq classification)
• Node 2 (ABCR+TAL): ~500ms (300ms ABCR + 200ms TAL)
• Node 3 (RAG): ~300ms (50ms embed + 20ms Qdrant + 230ms post-process)
• Node 4 (generate): ~2-3s for first token from Gemini
• Overhead: ~100ms (framework, serialization, network)
• Optimization focus: Nodes 2 and 4 are the biggest contributors
802 What is the caching hierarchy and hit rates? caching, hierarchy • L1: In-process cache (Python dict) — <1ms, ~5% hit rate
• L2: Redis cache — ~1ms, ~15-25% hit rate
• L3: Qdrant (pre-computed vectors) — ~20ms, effectively 100% for indexed docs
• Combined: caching saves ~30-40% of total compute per request
• Total cache storage: ~500MB Redis for 10K concurrent sessions
803 How does connection pooling work across databases? connection pooling, databases • PostgreSQL: SQLAlchemy QueuePool(size=10, max_overflow=20, timeout=30)
• Redis: redis.ConnectionPool(max_connections=50)
• Qdrant: QdrantClient(timeout=30) with internal HTTP pool
• Benefits: reuse connections, avoid connection setup overhead (~50ms per new connection)
• Monitoring: track pool utilization — alert if > 80% sustained
804 How does async I/O improve throughput? async, throughput, I/O • Sync: thread blocked during LLM API call (3 seconds idle)
• Async: event loop handles other requests during LLM I/O wait
• Result: single process handles 100+ concurrent requests (vs ~4 with threads)
• FastAPI: native async/await throughout the request lifecycle
• CPU-bound exception: embedding generation runs in thread pool to avoid blocking
805 How does batch processing optimize embedding generation? batch, embedding, optimize • Single: model.encode("text") — 50ms per text
• Batch: model.encode(["text1", "text2", ...], batch_size=32) — 200ms for 32 texts
• Speedup: ~8x throughput (GPU parallelism or CPU SIMD)
• Document ingestion: chunks batched for embedding (significant time saving)
• Online queries: single query — batch doesn't help (only one text to embed)
806 What database query optimizations are applied? query, optimization • Indexes: on all foreign keys and commonly filtered columns
• Eager loading: joinedload() for endpoints that need related data
• Pagination: offset-based with limit to prevent full table scans
• Select specific columns: db.session.query(User.id, User.name) instead of full model
• N+1 prevention: identified via SQLAlchemy echo mode in testing
807 How would you reduce Gemini API latency? Gemini, latency, reduce • Shorter prompts: reduce context size (fewer history messages, fewer RAG chunks)
• Streaming: first token arrives in ~1s even if full response takes 5s
• Prompt caching: Gemini supports cached context prefix (save repeated system prompt processing)
• Model selection: Gemini Flash for faster inference (trade quality for speed)
• Parallel: make independent API calls concurrently
808 How does memory management work for the embedding model? memory, embedding, model • Model size: ~420MB in memory (all-mpnet-base-v2)
• Loaded once: at startup, kept in memory for lifetime
• Shared: singleton across all requests (read-only inference)
• Garbage collection: intermediate tensors freed after encode() completes
• Peak: ~600MB during batch encoding (input tensors + output vectors)
• Container limit: 4GB total, model uses ~15%
809 How would you optimize Qdrant search performance? Qdrant, search, optimize • HNSW parameters: increase ef at search time for better recall (trade latency)
• Payload indexes: create keyword indexes on filtered fields (classroom_id)
• Quantization: scalar quantization reduces memory 4x, loses minimal accuracy
• Sharding: distribute large collections across multiple machines
• Caching: Qdrant has internal caching — ensure enough memory for cache
810 What are the bottlenecks at different scales? bottleneck, scale • 10 users: no bottleneck — everything fast
• 100 users: LLM API rate limits may hit → add API key rotation
• 1K users: Qdrant search still fast, LLM becomes primary bottleneck
• 10K users: need horizontal scaling of AI service, connection pool limits
• 100K users: need managed services (RDS, ElastiCache), CDN, multi-region
811 How does Redis pipeline optimization work? Redis, pipeline, batch • Individual commands: 5 Redis calls × ~1ms each = ~5ms
• Pipeline: batch 5 commands in single round trip = ~1ms total
• Usage: session state load (LRANGE + HGETALL + GET = 3 commands pipelined)
• Benefit: reduces network round trips for multi-key operations
• Implementation: pipe = redis.pipeline(); pipe.get(); pipe.hgetall(); pipe.execute()
812 How would you implement request deduplication? deduplication, request • Problem: user clicks "Send" button multiple times → duplicate queries
• Frontend: debounce button, disable after first click
• Backend: idempotency key in request header → Redis check
• Key: dedup:{user_id}:{hash(query)} with 5-second TTL
• If exists: return "processing" or cached response
• Saves: LLM cost and compute for exact duplicate queries
813 How does database partitioning help at scale? partitioning, database • Table partitioning: split large tables by key (e.g., by date for analytics)
• Assessment attempts: partition by month → queries only scan relevant partition
• Proctoring data: partition by classroom_id for locality
• Benefits: faster queries, easier data archival (drop old partitions)
• PostgreSQL: native declarative partitioning support
814 How would you optimize the document processing pipeline? optimize, document, pipeline • GPU: use GPU for OCR (EasyOCR) and embedding → 10x speedup
• Parallel pages: OCR multiple pages concurrently using asyncio.gather
• Skip OCR: ML classifier detects text vs scanned pages, skip OCR for text
• Chunk in parallel: split document into sections, chunk each independently
• Pre-warm: keep embedding model loaded and warm (avoid cold start)
815 What monitoring metrics indicate performance problems? monitoring, metrics, alert • P95 latency > 5s for tutor: LLM bottleneck or cache miss spike
• Connection pool > 80% utilized: approaching exhaustion
• Redis memory > 70%: cache eviction about to start
• Error rate > 1%: something is failing systematically
• CPU > 80% sustained: need to scale out or optimize
• Kafka consumer lag > 1000: consumers can't keep up with events
816 How does frontend performance optimization work? frontend, performance • Code splitting: dynamic imports for heavy components (meeting, proctoring)
• Image optimization: Next.js <Image> with automatic WebP conversion
• Bundle analysis: identify and reduce large dependencies
• Caching: api responses cached with react-query (staleWhileRevalidate)
• Lazy rendering: virtualized lists for large data sets
817 How would you implement read replicas for performance? read replica, scaling • PostgreSQL: streaming replication to 1-2 read replicas
• Write path: primary database (all INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE)
• Read path: replicas for list endpoints, reports, dashboard queries
• Implementation: separate connection string for read-only queries
• Replication lag: typically < 1 second, acceptable for most reads
818 How does API response time monitoring work? response time, monitoring • Middleware: measure time between request start and response complete
• Histogram: P50, P75, P90, P95, P99 percentiles
• Per-endpoint: different targets (tutor: 5s, search: 500ms, CRUD: 200ms)
• Logging: slow queries (>1s) logged with query details
• Dashboard: real-time latency graphs per endpoint
819 How would you optimize cold start time? cold start, startup • Current startup: ~10-15 seconds (model loading dominates)
• Optimization 1: lazy load embedding model (first request slower, startup faster)
• Optimization 2: model pre-compiled and cached on disk (faster subsequent loads)
• Optimization 3: slim Docker image (fewer layers to pull)
• Optimization 4: health check delayed until model fully loaded
820 How does garbage collection impact Python service performance? GC, Python, performance • Python GC: reference counting + generational collector
• Impact: periodic GC pauses (usually < 10ms, but can spike)
• Large objects: NumPy arrays (embeddings) handled efficiently by refcounting
• Memory fragmentation: long-running process may fragment heap
• Mitigation: periodic container restart (Docker health check + restart policy)
# Question Keywords Answer
821 How does connection keep-alive improve performance? keep-alive, HTTP • HTTP keep-alive: reuse TCP connection across multiple requests
• Without: TCP handshake + TLS handshake per request (~100ms overhead)
• With: single connection handles multiple requests (~0ms overhead per request)
• Nginx: keepalive_timeout 65; keeps connections open
• httpx: connection pool reuses connections for inter-service calls
822 How would you implement response compression? compression, gzip • Nginx: gzip on; gzip_types application/json text/event-stream;
• Benefit: ~60-70% reduction in response size for JSON
• Trade-off: CPU cost for compression, negligible for modern servers
• SSE: compression may interfere with streaming — test carefully
• Frontend: browsers automatically decompress gzip responses
823 How does Qdrant quantization reduce memory usage? quantization, Qdrant, memory • Scalar quantization: reduces float32 (4 bytes) to int8 (1 byte) per dimension
• Memory: 768 dims × 4 bytes = 3KB per vector → 768 × 1 byte = 768 bytes
• Saving: 75% memory reduction
• Accuracy: ~1-2% recall loss (acceptable for most use cases)
• When to use: when collection grows beyond available RAM
824 How does request queuing prevent system overload? queuing, backpressure • Semaphore: asyncio.Semaphore(50) limits concurrent LLM calls
• Queue: requests beyond 50 wait in semaphore queue
• Timeout: if waiting > 30 seconds, return 503 to client
• Benefits: prevents LLM API rate limit breaches, controls memory usage
• Alternative: token bucket algorithm for smoother rate limiting
825 How would you optimize Redis memory usage? Redis, memory, optimize • Key expiry: all keys have TTL (no unbounded growth)
• Data structure selection: hashes for small objects (Redis-optimized memory)
• Compression: compress large values before storing
• Eviction policy: allkeys-lru evicts least recently used when full
• Monitoring: redis-cli info memory tracks usage vs max
826 What is the optimal number of Qdrant search results to retrieve? Qdrant, top-k, optimal • Initial retrieval: top_k=10 (cast wide net)
• After re-ranking: select top 5 for LLM context
• Why not more: diminishing returns, more tokens in prompt = slower + costlier
• Why not fewer: might miss relevant chunks
• Diversity: max 2 per document in top 5 → ensures breadth
• Tested: 5 chunks gives best quality-to-cost ratio
827 How does lazy loading improve service startup? lazy loading, startup • Current: load everything at startup (~15 seconds)
• Optimization: load embedding model only when first embedding request arrives
• Agent compilation: compile LangGraph on first agent invocation
• Trade-off: first request is slower, but service starts accepting health checks faster
• Docker: passes health check sooner, rolling deploys complete faster
828 How does database connection timeout affect performance? timeout, database, connection • pool_timeout=30: request waits up to 30s for free connection
• Too short (5s): requests fail unnecessarily during brief spikes
• Too long (120s): requests hang, user sees loading spinner
• Optimal: 30s balances responsiveness with spike handling
• Monitor: track P99 connection wait time — should be < 5s normally
829 How would you implement edge caching for API responses? edge cache, CDN • CloudFront: cache GET responses at edge locations
• Cache-Control headers: public, max-age=300 for cacheable endpoints
• Cacheable: document list, assessment list, curriculum view (student-specific with Vary)
• Not cacheable: tutor chat (real-time), auth (security-sensitive)
• Invalidation: CloudFront invalidation API when data changes
830 How does streaming reduce perceived latency? streaming, perceived, latency • Without streaming: user waits 5 seconds → sees complete response (feels slow)
• With streaming: user sees first token in 1s → response builds progressively (feels fast)
• Actual total time: roughly the same (maybe slightly longer for streaming overhead)
• UX impact: dramatic — users perceive streaming as 3x faster
• Implementation: StreamingResponse + EventSource pattern

Section 23: Behavioral & Strategic Interview Questions (70 Questions)¶

Key Concepts: Design Decisions · Trade-Off Analysis · Why Questions · What-If Scenarios · Leadership · Communication · Problem Solving · Architecture Critique · Future Vision · Lessons Learned

# Question Keywords Answer
831 Why did you choose a polyglot persistence approach? why, polyglot, decision • Each database optimized for its use case:
• PostgreSQL: ACID transactions for user/classroom data (relational integrity critical)
• Qdrant: purpose-built vector search for embeddings (100x faster than PostgreSQL for similarity)
• Redis: sub-millisecond caching and session store (in-memory speed)
• MongoDB: flexible schema for evolving proctoring data (schema changes without migration)
• Cassandra: high-write throughput for time-series analytics (append-only workload)
• Trade-off: operational complexity of 5 databases vs. performance benefits
832 Why dual backends (Flask + FastAPI) instead of one framework? why, dual backend • Flask: mature ecosystem for traditional CRUD, better SQLAlchemy integration, more Flask extensions
• FastAPI: native async for I/O-heavy AI operations (LLM calls, streaming)
• Could we use one? Yes, but Flask struggles with async streaming, FastAPI has less mature ORM ecosystem
• Decision: use each where it excels, accept inter-service HTTP overhead
• If starting over: might use FastAPI for everything (async ecosystem has matured)
833 What was the hardest technical challenge you faced? challenge, behavioral • ABCR classification accuracy: getting the routing right so queries go to correct path
• Problem: misclassification meant either wasting compute (RAG for simple chat) or poor answers (no RAG for document questions)
• Solution: iterative prompt engineering with test dataset of 200 queries
• Result: 90% accuracy, with fallback logic for edge cases
• Lesson: LLM classification needs rigorous testing, not just intuition
834 What would you do differently if starting over? retrospective, improvement • 1. Use FastAPI for everything (remove Flask dependency)
• 2. Add distributed tracing from day 1 (debugging across services is hard)
• 3. Implement proper API versioning before first deployment
• 4. Split WebIngestService earlier (60K lines is too large)
• 5. Add more integration tests earlier (caught bugs later than ideal)
• 6. Use TypeScript types more strictly on frontend
835 How did you decide on the agent architecture (supervisor vs. autonomous)? decision, agent, architecture • Considered: autonomous agents (each decides when to delegate), flat routing, supervisor
• Chose supervisor: Orchestrator as single entry point with clear routing
• Reasons: predictable behavior, easier debugging, centralized moderation
• Autonomous agents risk: infinite loops, unpredictable routes, hard to monitor
• LangGraph supervisor: explicit state machine with defined transitions
836 How do you handle disagreements about technical decisions? behavioral, disagreement • Present data: set up comparison with metrics (latency, accuracy, cost)
• Prototype: build small PoC for each approach (1-2 days each)
• Example: ABCR vs simple keyword routing — built both, tested on 200 queries
• ABCR won: 90% accuracy vs 65% for keyword routing
• Lesson: let data decide, not opinions
837 How do you prioritize features when time is limited? prioritization, behavioral • Framework: Impact vs Effort matrix (high impact, low effort first)
• Must-have: authentication, tutor chat, document upload (core value)
• Nice-to-have: gamification, mobile app, collaborative editing
• Cut: features that don't serve core learning objective
• Example: prioritized RAG quality over meeting integration (RAG is core, meetings are supplementary)
838 How would you explain this system to a non-technical stakeholder? communication, explain • "EnsureStudy is like having a personal tutor for every student."
• "Teachers upload course materials, and AI reads and understands them."
• "When a student asks a question, AI searches through those materials and gives an answer with references."
• "The system tracks what each student struggles with and adapts."
• "Think of it as combining Google search + ChatGPT + a textbook, but specifically for each classroom."

• Step 2: Build minimal working example (proof of concept)
• Step 3: Identify patterns and best practices from community
• Step 4: Integrate into project incrementally (one feature at a time)

840 How do you handle production incidents? incident, production • Detection: monitoring alerts trigger on-call notification
• Assessment: determine severity (P1=complete outage, P2=degraded, P3=minor)
• Communication: status page update, stakeholder notification
• Resolution: fix forward (quick patch) or rollback (if recent deployment)
• Post-mortem: blameless review, document root cause, action items
• Example: LLM API outage → fallback model activated → 10-minute resolution
841 Why LangGraph instead of other agent frameworks? why, LangGraph, comparison • Compared: LangChain LCEL, AutoGen, CrewAI, custom state machines
• LangGraph advantages: explicit state, debuggable graphs, conditional routing, persistence
• LangChain LCEL: too implicit, hard to debug complex flows
• AutoGen/CrewAI: too autonomous, less control over behavior
• Custom: would work but LangGraph provides battle-tested primitives
• Key: state machine paradigm matches our pipeline-style agents
842 How do you ensure code quality in a fast-moving project? code quality, process • Code review: all PRs reviewed by at least one team member

• Testing: minimum 80% coverage, CI blocks merge on test failure

844 Why did you choose Qdrant over other vector databases? why, Qdrant, comparison • Compared: Pinecone, Weaviate, Milvus, ChromaDB
• Qdrant advantages: self-hosted (data privacy), excellent filtering, Rust performance
• Pinecone: managed only (privacy concern for educational data)
• Weaviate: good but heavier, more complex setup
• ChromaDB: too simple for production (no auth, limited filtering)
• Milvus: powerful but highest operational complexity
• Key factors: self-hosted, filtering capability, ease of use
845 How do you measure success of the AI tutoring feature? metrics, success • Engagement: daily active students using tutor, messages per session
• Quality: user rating average (target > 4.0/5.0), citation accuracy
• Learning: student test scores before/after using tutor (A/B study)
• Efficiency: reduced teacher workload (fewer repeated questions)
• Retention: students who use tutor more have lower dropout rate
• Cost: LLM cost per student per month (target < $2)
846 What was the most impactful optimization you made? optimization, impact • ABCR routing: saved ~40% of unnecessary RAG calls
• Before: every query triggered full RAG pipeline (embed + search + retrieve = 300ms)
• After: "hello", "thanks", "I understand" skip RAG entirely
• Impact: reduced average latency by ~0.8 seconds across all queries
• Cost reduction: ~30% fewer embedding compute cycles
• Lesson: routing intelligence before expensive operations pays off

850 What's the most important lesson from building this project? lesson, reflection • "Systems design is about trade-offs, not perfection."
• Every decision has pros and cons — the key is understanding the trade-off
• Polyglot databases: better performance but harder to operate
• Dual backends: better tech fit but more deployment complexity
• RAG vs fine-tuning: dynamic content but higher latency
• Document decisions so future you understands the reasoning

• Option C: separate Kubernetes namespaces with dedicated services (complete isolation)
• Recommendation: Option A for cost-efficiency, Option C for maximum security

• Data cleanup: script to find documents with duplicate vector counts → delete + reprocess
• Impact: search results were duplicated (same chunk appearing twice)

# Question Keywords Answer
911 Why does the TutorAgent use streaming while the AssessmentAgent doesn't? streaming, design, why • Tutor: user-facing chat — streaming gives progressive response (feels interactive)
• Assessment: background generation — teacher doesn't watch live (results delivered later)
• Streaming complexity: error handling harder (can't return error after partial response)
• Assessment needs: full response for validation before saving to database
• Trade-off: UX benefit vs implementation complexity
912 Why is conversation history stored in Redis instead of PostgreSQL? Redis vs Postgres, history • Access pattern: read full history on every query (frequent, fast reads needed)
• Redis: O(1) LRANGE for list retrieval, sub-millisecond
• PostgreSQL: would need ORDER BY + LIMIT per query (heavier, faster if indexed)
• Ephemeral: session history is temporary (cleared after session ends)
• Trade-off: speed over durability (history is reproducible, not critical data)
913 Why use Kafka for async events instead of a simple task queue (like Celery)? Kafka vs Celery, why • Kafka: event log (messages preserved, multiple consumers, replay)
• Celery: task queue (one consumer per task, fire-and-forget)
• Need: multiple consumers for same event (assessment → learning + revision + analytics)
• Need: replay capability for debugging and recovery
• Trade-off: Kafka is more complex but future-proof for event-driven architecture
914 Why not use a single LLM for everything instead of dual-LLM? single LLM, cost, why • Single Gemini: ~$0.01 per query for moderation + classification + generation<br>• Dual: ~$0.001 (Groq) + $0.007 (Gemini) = ~$0.008 per query
• Savings: ~20% cost reduction with dual-LLM approach
• Latency: ABCR takes 200ms (Groq) vs 1s (Gemini) — faster routing decisions
• Trade-off: marginal cost saving and latency improvement vs operational complexity
915 Why self-host Qdrant instead of using Pinecone? self-host vs managed, why • Data privacy: educational data stays on our infrastructure
• Cost: self-hosted is free (vs Pinecone pricing per vector)
• Control: full configuration control (index parameters, quantization)
• Compliance: FERPA/GDPR easier when data doesn't leave our servers
• Trade-off: operational burden (backups, upgrades) vs managed convenience
916 Scenario: What if you need to support 50 simultaneous classrooms each with different documents? 50 classrooms, scale • Qdrant: single collection, classroom_id filter handles isolation
• Performance: payload index on classroom_id → O(1) filter
• Memory: 50 classrooms × 1000 docs × 100 chunks = 5M vectors × 768 dims = ~15GB
• Resource: Qdrant handles 5M vectors easily on 32GB machine
• No per-classroom collection needed — filter approach scales
917 How would you implement A/B testing for different RAG strategies? A/B test, RAG • Variants: A (semantic only), B (hybrid search), C (hybrid + reranking)
• Split: random user assignment to variant (stored in user profile)
• Metrics: citation accuracy, user rating, response quality score
• Duration: 2-4 weeks for statistical significance
• Implementation: feature flag checks for search strategy in RAG node
918 Scenario: Teacher uploads a 1000-page textbook. How does the system handle it? large document, 1000 pages • Stage 2 (extraction): takes 2-5 minutes (page-by-page text extraction)
• Stage 3 (OCR): skipped for text-based PDF, applied only to scanned pages
• Stage 4 (chunking): produces ~2000 chunks (500 tokens each with overlap)
• Stage 5 (embedding): ~60 seconds for 2000 chunks (batch=32, ~65 batches)
• Stage 6 (indexing): Qdrant upsert of 2000 vectors in ~2 seconds
• Total: ~5-10 minutes end-to-end
• Student experience: large document provides rich RAG context
919 How would you implement automated assessment grading for coding questions? coding, grading, auto • Sandbox: run student code in isolated Docker container (security)
• Test cases: pre-defined inputs → expected outputs
• Execution: timeout after 10 seconds, memory limit 256MB
• Grading: number of test cases passed / total test cases
• Partial credit: based on percentage of tests passed
• Feedback: show which test cases failed with expected vs actual output
920 How does the system ensure fair assessment when AI generates questions? fairness, assessment, AI • Difficulty calibration: bloom's taxonomy level in prompt
• Validation: teacher reviews and approves all AI-generated questions
• Bias check: questions reviewed for cultural/gender/racial bias
• Coverage: ensures questions cover all topics proportionally
• Alternative: question pool with random selection (different students get different questions but same difficulty)
• Statistical: post-assessment analysis of question discrimination index

Section 26: Advanced Architecture & Future Vision (50 Questions)¶

Key Concepts: Microservices Evolution · GraphQL · gRPC · Event Sourcing · CQRS · Serverless · Edge AI · Federated Learning · Multi-Modal · Knowledge Graph

# Question Keywords Answer
921 How would you evolve to a full microservices architecture? microservices, evolution • Phase 1: extract DocumentService (upload, process, status) from Core
• Phase 2: extract AssessmentService (CRUD, grading, results) from Core
• Phase 3: extract MeetingService (rooms, recordings, transcripts) from Core
• Phase 4: break AI Service into AgentService + EmbeddingService + SearchService
• Communication: REST for sync, Kafka for async (already in place)
• API Gateway: Kong or Ambassador for unified entry point
• Timeline: 3-6 months for full migration
922 Would you add GraphQL? Why or why not? GraphQL, API design • Pros: flexible queries (frontend asks for exactly what it needs), single endpoint
• Cons: caching harder (no URL-based caching), N+1 query risk, complexity
• Where beneficial: dashboard pages fetching multiple related entities
• Current REST: works well for well-defined endpoints
• Recommendation: add GraphQL layer as BFF (Backend For Frontend) for complex dashboard queries only
923 Would you use gRPC between services? gRPC, inter-service • Pros: faster than HTTP REST (binary protocol), strong typing (protobuf), streaming support
• Cons: no browser support (need proxy), less human-readable for debugging
• Where beneficial: Core → AI service hot path (high-frequency calls)
• Current HTTP: simpler, adequate performance for current scale
• Recommendation: migrate to gRPC when inter-service latency becomes bottleneck
924 How would you implement Event Sourcing? event sourcing, CQRS • Store all state changes as immutable events (not current state)
• Events: StudentAnswered, DocumentUploaded, TopicCompleted, MessageSent
• Benefits: complete audit trail, time travel debugging, event replay
• CQRS: separate read (materialized views) and write (event store) models
• Where useful: learning analytics (replay student journey) and audit compliance
• Trade-off: significantly more complex, not needed for initial version
925 How would you add serverless components? serverless, Lambda • Document processing: AWS Lambda triggered by S3 upload event
• Benefits: auto-scaling, per-invocation pricing, no server management
• Candidate functions: thumbnail generation, OCR, email notifications
• NOT suitable: LangGraph agents (need persistent state), embedding model (cold start too slow)
• Hybrid: serverless for stateless, event-driven tasks + containers for stateful agents
926 How would you implement edge AI for proctoring? edge AI, browser ML • TensorFlow.js: run face/object detection models in browser
• Benefits: no frame upload needed (privacy), reduced server load
• Models: MobileNet SSD for objects, BlazeFace for face detection
• Client sends: detection results only (not raw video frames)
• Server: validates and aggregates results (can't trust client fully)
• Trade-off: variable client hardware affects detection quality
927 How would you implement federated learning? federated learning, privacy • Concept: train models across institutions without sharing raw data
• Each institution: trains local model on their proctoring data
• Aggregation: server combines model weights (not data) from all institutions
• Privacy: student data never leaves institution's infrastructure
• Use case: improve proctoring accuracy with diverse data without privacy violation
• Complexity: very high, research-level implementation
928 How would you add multi-modal input (images, diagrams)? multi-modal, image input • Gemini Vision: natively supports image + text input
• Use case: student photographs textbook page → asks question about it
• Pipeline: image upload → Gemini Vision description → text-based RAG on description
• Diagram understanding: "Explain this circuit diagram" with image
• Assessment: image-based questions ("What does this graph show?")
• Implementation: multipart form upload → pass image bytes to Gemini
929 How would you build a knowledge graph from course materials? knowledge graph, NLP • Extraction: LLM extracts entities (concepts, topics) and relationships from documents
• Storage: Neo4j graph database for concept relationships
• Queries: "What are the prerequisites for recursion?" → graph traversal
• Visualization: interactive concept map for students
• Integration: replace LLM-based dependency analysis with graph queries
• Benefits: more accurate prerequisite chains, visual learning aids
930 How would you implement adaptive difficulty in real-time tutoring? adaptive, difficulty, real-time • Track: student's correct/incorrect answers during session
• Adjust: if consistently correct → increase complexity of follow-up questions
• Prompt: "The student has answered 5/5 correctly. Increase difficulty for the next explanation."
• Level bands: novice (definitions), intermediate (application), advanced (analysis/synthesis)
• Smooth: gradual transition, never jump from easy to hard immediately
# Question Keywords Answer

• Method 2: n-gram matching — detect verbatim copying
• Method 3: cross-student comparison — detect submission similarity between students
• Integration: Qdrant search with high similarity threshold (>0.95 = suspicion)
• Report: highlighted matching sections with source references
934 How would you implement predictive analytics for student dropout? predictive, dropout, analytics • Features: login frequency, assignment completion rate, tutor session frequency, assessment scores

939 How would you implement a flashcard system from course materials? flashcard, auto-generate • Source: RAG chunks with key definitions and concepts

940 How would you implement learning path branching? learning path, branching • Linear path: A → B → C → D (current approach)

• Visualization: interactive graph showing multiple paths through curriculum

941 How would you build an API marketplace for third-party integrations? marketplace, API, integration • Public API: documented REST endpoints with API key authentication

• Rate limiting: per-API-key limits (1000 req/hour for free, 10K for paid)

942 How would you implement student progress portfolios? portfolio, progress • Auto-generated: compile student's assessment scores, tutor interactions, completed topics

943 How would you handle exam content security? exam security, content protection • Encryption: assessment questions encrypted at rest in database
• Decryption: only when student starts assessment (just-in-time delivery)
• No cache: assessment content excluded from all caches
• Randomization: question order and option order randomized per student
• Anti-screenshot: CSS user-select disabled, right-click disabled (basic deterrent)
• Watermark: invisible watermark in question text for leak tracking
944 How would you implement a teaching assistant AI agent? TA agent, teaching • Purpose: helps teachers, not students

945 How would you add real-time collaboration on assessments? collaboration, real-time, assessment • Group assessments: multiple students collaborate on answers

946 How would you implement a content calendar for teachers? calendar, content, planning • Interface: drag-and-drop calendar for scheduling topics and assessments

947 What would a v3 architecture look like at enterprise scale? enterprise, v3, architecture • API Gateway: Kong with authentication, rate limiting, routing
• Service mesh: Istio for inter-service communication, observability
• Databases: managed services (RDS, ElastiCache, Atlas, Astra)
• Compute: Kubernetes with auto-scaling pods
• AI: dedicated GPU nodes for embedding, separate LLM gateway
• CDN: CloudFront for global static asset delivery
• Multi-region: active-active in us-east-1 and eu-west-1
948 How would you implement cost allocation per institution? cost, allocation, billing • LLM costs: per-token tracking by institution_id (custom header in LLM calls)
• Storage: per-institution Qdrant vector count × cost-per-vector
• Compute: per-institution request count × average compute cost
• Database: per-institution row count (proportional allocation)
• Dashboard: institution admin sees monthly cost breakdown
• Alerts: notification when institution approaches budget limits
949 How would you implement AI-powered course creation from scratch? course creation, AI • Input: teacher provides subject name, level, learning objectives
• Process: Gemini generates comprehensive syllabus → topics → learning activities
• Enhancement: web search enrichment (current research, videos, articles)
• Output: full curriculum with daily schedule, assessments, study materials
• Teacher review: approve/modify AI-generated course before publishing
• Time saving: 40-hour course creation → 4 hours with AI assistance
950 What is your final architecture recommendation for a startup vs enterprise? recommendation, startup vs enterprise • Startup (< 1K users): single server, Docker Compose, PostgreSQL + Redis + Qdrant only
Drop: Kafka (use direct API calls), Cassandra (use PostgreSQL for analytics), MongoDB (use PostgreSQL JSONB)
Cost: ~$50/month on a single VPS<br>• **Enterprise (> 100K users):** full architecture as designed + managed services + Kubernetes<br> Add: API gateway, service mesh, multi-region, dedicated GPU fleet<br> Cost: ~$5,000-15,000/month on AWS
• Key insight: design for enterprise, deploy for startup — scale up as needed

Section 27: Final Comprehensive Review (50 Questions)¶

Key Concepts: System Recap · Architecture Summary · Key Metrics · Integration Points · Critical Decisions · Quick Recall · Interview Closers

# Question Keywords Answer
951 Summarize the entire EnsureStudy platform in 30 seconds. summary, elevator pitch • EnsureStudy is an AI-powered educational platform with: 9 LangGraph agents, polyglot persistence (5 databases), dual-backend architecture (Flask + FastAPI), RAG-based tutoring, 8-detector proctoring, real-time video meetings via LiveKit, and adaptive learning via spaced repetition. It enables teachers to upload course materials and students to learn with personalized AI tutoring grounded in those materials.
952 List all 9 agents and their one-line purpose. agents, summary • Orchestrator: routes queries to correct agent
• TutorAgent: answers questions with RAG and topic awareness
• ResearchAgent: enriches knowledge from web sources
• CurriculumAgent: generates study plans from syllabi
• AssessmentAgent: creates adaptive quizzes
• LearningAgent: analyzes performance and adapts difficulty
• QuestionPoolAgent: maintains large question banks
• RevisionAgent: schedules spaced repetition reviews
• InterviewAgent: simulates technical interviews
953 List all 5 databases and their primary purpose. databases, summary • PostgreSQL: users, classrooms, enrollments, assessments (relational, ACID)
• Qdrant: vector embeddings for semantic search (768-dim, cosine)
• Redis: caching, sessions, rate limiting (in-memory, sub-ms)
• MongoDB: proctoring frame data and reports (flexible schema)
• Cassandra: time-series analytics and event aggregation (high-write)
954 What are the 3 most important API endpoints? API, critical, endpoints • POST /ai/tutor/chat — core tutoring (SSE streaming, RAG-powered)
• POST /api/documents/upload — file upload triggering AI processing pipeline
• POST /api/auth/login — authentication gateway returning JWT tokens
955 What are the 3 most critical infrastructure components? infrastructure, critical • PostgreSQL: if down, entire system fails (user data, auth, enrollments)
• Nginx: if down, no external traffic reaches any service
• Gemini API: if down, no AI generation (tutoring, assessment, curriculum) — Groq fallback provides partial
956 What are the 3 key innovations in this architecture? innovations, key • ABCR Routing: intelligent query classification saves 40% of unnecessary RAG calls
• Topic Anchor Layer: maintains conversation context across multi-turn sessions
• 8-Detector Proctoring: comprehensive AI-powered exam integrity with LightGBM + LSTM fusion
957 What are the 3 biggest scalability challenges? scalability, challenges • LLM Rate Limits: Gemini/Groq API quotas limit concurrent AI generation
• Embedding Model Memory: 500MB per AI service instance for model
• Qdrant Growth: vector storage grows linearly with documents (750+ bytes per vector)
958 What are the total number of files across the codebase? files, count, total • Core Service: 178 files (Flask app)
• AI Service: 230+ files (FastAPI + agents)
• Frontend: 200+ files (Next.js + React)
• ML: 15+ notebooks + model code
• Infrastructure: 10+ config files (Docker, Nginx, Makefile)
• Total: ~650+ files across the entire project
959 What are the key API contracts between Core and AI services? API contract, inter-service • Core → AI: POST /ai/tutor/chat (streaming), POST /ai/assessment/generate, POST /ai/documents/process
• AI → Core: POST /api/documents/{id}/status (callback), POST /api/assessments/{id}/ai-results
• Auth: both validate same JWT format (shared secret)
• Health: GET /ai/health called by Core for dependency check
960 What are the critical security measures? security, summary • Authentication: JWT with bcrypt passwords, token blacklisting
• Authorization: RBAC (student/teacher/admin), classroom-level data isolation
• Data protection: TLS in transit, encryption at rest, CORS whitelist
• Input validation: Marshmallow (Flask) + Pydantic (FastAPI) schemas
• Content safety: ModerationAgent for query/response filtering
961 What happens in the first 30 seconds after a student sends a query? timeline, 30 seconds • 0s: query hits Nginx → routed to AI Service
• 0.1s: ModerationAgent checks safety (Groq, ~200ms)
• 0.3s: ABCR classifies query type (Groq, ~200ms)
• 0.5s: TAL updates topic anchor (local processing)
• 0.6s: embedding generated for query (local model, ~50ms)
• 0.7s: Qdrant retrieves top-10 chunks (~20ms)
• 0.8s: chunks re-ranked and top-5 selected
• 1.0s: prompt assembled with context + history + query
• 1.0-4s: Gemini generates streaming response (first token ~1s)
• 4-5s: full response received, follow-ups generated
962 What are the 5 most important Pydantic/Marshmallow schemas? schemas, validation • UserRegistrationSchema: email, password, name, role (registration validation)
• TutorRequest: query, session_id, classroom_id (tutor chat input)
• AssessmentCreateSchema: topic, difficulty, question_count, format (assessment generation)
• DocumentUploadSchema: file, classroom_id, title (document processing input)
• SearchRequest: query, classroom_id, top_k, filters (semantic search parameters)
963 Draw the request flow for document upload to searchable state. flow, document, diagram • User → Nginx → Core Service (POST /api/documents/upload) → store file + create DB record → return 202 Accepted
• Core → AI Service (POST /ai/documents/process) → 7-stage pipeline
• Stage 1-3: validate → extract text → OCR (if needed)
• Stage 4-5: chunk → embed (SentenceTransformer)
• Stage 6: upsert vectors to Qdrant
• Stage 7: callback to Core → update status to "completed"
• Total: 1-15 minutes depending on document size
964 What configuration values change between dev and production? config, dev vs prod • DEBUG: True (dev) → False (prod)
• DATABASE_URL: localhost:5432 → RDS endpoint
• REDIS_URL: localhost:6379 → ElastiCache endpoint
• CORS_ORIGINS: localhost:3000 → production domain
• JWT_SECRET_KEY: dev-secret → strong random secret from Secrets Manager
• GEMINI_API_KEY: personal dev key → production quota key
965 What are the Docker Compose service dependencies? dependencies, Docker • nginx depends on: frontend, core-service, ai-service
• core-service depends on: postgres, redis
• ai-service depends on: redis, qdrant, kafka
• kafka depends on: zookeeper
• frontend: no dependencies (static build)
• Startup order enforced by depends_on + healthcheck
966 What are the 5 key environment variables? env vars, important • DATABASE_URL: PostgreSQL connection string
• REDIS_URL: Redis connection string
• GEMINI_API_KEY: Google AI API key for generation
• JWT_SECRET_KEY: secret for signing auth tokens
• QDRANT_URL: vector database connection URL
967 What would you test in a 1-hour technical interview demo? demo, interview • 1. Start full stack with make dev (show Docker running all 13 services)
• 2. Create classroom and upload a PDF document

968 What's the one thing that makes this project stand out? standout, unique • End-to-end AI-native design: not just "add AI to existing platform" but "build platform around AI"
• Every feature leverages AI: tutoring, assessment generation, curriculum design, proctoring, learning adaptation
• Technical depth: 9 LangGraph agents, polyglot databases, ML models, real-time streaming
• Shows: systems design thinking, ML engineering, full-stack development, DevOps — all in one project
969 How would you explain RAG in an interview without jargon? RAG, simple explanation • "Instead of asking the AI to guess the answer, we first search through the teacher's actual course materials to find relevant passages. Then we show those passages to the AI and say 'answer the student's question using these materials.' This way, the AI's answer is based on real content, not just what it memorized during training. It's like open-book vs closed-book exam for the AI."
970 How would you explain LangGraph agents in an interview? LangGraph, simple explanation • "Think of each agent as a mini-program with clear steps. For example, the Tutor Agent: (1) check if the question is safe, (2) figure out what type of question it is, (3) search for relevant materials, (4) write an answer using those materials. Each step can make decisions — like skipping the search step if it's a simple greeting. LangGraph lets us define these steps and decisions as a graph, making it easy to trace and debug."
# Question Keywords Answer
971 What are the 3 hardest parts of the RAG pipeline? RAG, hardest • Chunking quality: bad chunk boundaries = bad retrieval (splitting mid-paragraph loses context)
• Relevance ranking: embedding similarity isn't perfect → irrelevant chunks pollute LLM context
• Hallucination prevention: even with good context, LLM may generate unsupported claims
972 What are the 3 hardest parts of the agent system? agent, hardest • ABCR accuracy: misclassification routes queries to wrong pipeline (waste or poor quality)
• State management: keeping session context across turns without bloating prompt
• Agent coordination: Orchestrator must reliably route to correct agent (9 options)
973 How does the MCP (Multi-Context Protocol) data isolation guarantee work in one sentence? MCP, one sentence Every database query, vector search, and API response is filtered by the classroom_id from the authenticated user's JWT token, ensuring students can only access data from their enrolled classrooms.
974 What would break if you removed Redis? remove Redis, impact • All caching disabled (ABCR, session, web content, response) → every request recomputes from scratch
• Conversation history lost → tutor has no memory between turns
• JWT blacklist gone → logged-out users can use old tokens (security risk)
• Rate limiting disabled → no protection against abuse
• System works but 40-50% slower and less secure
975 What would break if you removed Kafka? remove Kafka, impact • Async events stop: assessment completion doesn't trigger LearningAgent
• Document processing: must be synchronous (user waits for entire pipeline)
• Analytics: no streaming aggregation to Cassandra
• Alternative: direct API calls between services (synchronous coupling)
• System works but loses decoupling and event replay benefits
976 What's the cost breakdown per student per month? cost, per student • LLM (Gemini): ~$0.50/student (50 tutor queries × ~$0.01/query)
• LLM (Groq): ~$0.05/student (50 classifications × ~$0.001/classification)
• Infrastructure: ~$0.20/student (proportional compute, storage, networking)<br>• Total: ~$0.75/student/month at 50 queries/month
• Target price: $5-10/student/month (healthy margin)
977 What are the top 3 metrics to monitor in production? monitoring, top 3 • P95 tutor response time: if > 5s, users feel the system is broken
• Error rate: if > 1%, something is systematically failing
• LLM API budget burn rate: if doubling daily, potential abuse or regression
978 What happens to data when a classroom is deleted? deletion, cascade • Cascade: all Documents, Assessments, Enrollments, Curriculum records deleted
• External cleanup: Qdrant vectors for that classroom_id removed via background job
• Kafka: completion events may still exist (processed as no-ops if classroom gone)
• Redis: session caches expire naturally via TTL
• Soft delete option: admin can soft-delete (recoverable) vs hard-delete (permanent)
979 What is the single most complex file in the codebase? complex file, codebase • web_ingest_service.py at ~60,000 characters — handles web content fetching, parsing, cleaning, chunking, and embedding. Should be refactored into 5-6 smaller services (fetcher, parser, cleaner, chunker, embedder). The size indicates scope creep during feature development.
980 What differentiates this from ChatGPT for education? differentiation, ChatGPT • Grounded answers: responses based on teacher-uploaded materials (not general knowledge)
• Classroom isolation: student in Physics class gets Physics answers, not generic
• Citations: every answer includes source document and page reference
• Progress tracking: learning analytics, adaptive difficulty, spaced repetition
• Assessment integration: AI generates tests from the same materials
• Proctoring: built-in exam integrity (ChatGPT has none)
981 What is the deployment checklist for production? deployment, checklist • 1. Run all unit and integration tests (CI pipeline)
• 2. Run database migrations on staging
• 3. Deploy to staging, run smoke tests
• 4. Deploy to production (blue-green or canary)
• 5. Verify health checks pass for all services
• 6. Monitor error rate and latency for 30 minutes
• 7. Ready to rollback if metrics degrade
982 What are the 5 design principles you followed? principles, design • 1. AI-native: every feature leverages AI capabilities
• 2. Data-isolated: classroom boundaries enforced at every layer
• 3. Async-first: background processing for heavy tasks (documents, analytics)
• 4. Cache-everywhere: reduce latency and cost at every opportunity
• 5. Fail-gracefully: never block the student from learning due to component failure
983 Quick: what's the difference between ABCR routes? ABCR, difference, quick • Academic: needs RAG + deep answer (e.g., "Explain binary search")
• Beyond-scope: outside classroom scope → polite redirect (e.g., "How to cook pasta?")
• Casual: social/greeting → quick response without RAG (e.g., "Hello!")
• RAG-needed: might need documents → trigger selective retrieval
984 Quick: what's the difference between TutorAgent and ResearchAgent? difference, agents • TutorAgent: answers from existing classroom documents (RAG over uploaded materials)
• ResearchAgent: fetches NEW information from the web (Google, Wikipedia, Arxiv) and adds to knowledge base
985 Quick: what's the difference between LearningAgent and RevisionAgent? difference, learning vs revision • LearningAgent: analyzes performance to identify weak topics and adjust difficulty
• RevisionAgent: schedules spaced repetition reviews using SM-2 algorithm
986 Quick: what's the difference between AssessmentAgent and QuestionPoolAgent? difference, assessment agents • AssessmentAgent: generates specific assessments on-demand (teacher requests quiz on topic X)
• QuestionPoolAgent: maintains large question bank across topics (generates in bulk, stores for reuse)
987 How does the system handle 24/7 availability? availability, 24/7 • Docker restart policy: restart: unless-stopped for automatic recovery
• Health checks: detect and restart unhealthy containers
• No maintenance window: zero-downtime deployments via blue-green
• External APIs: LLM providers have their own uptime SLAs (~99.9%)
• Target: 99.5% uptime (allows ~4 hours downtime per month)
988 What logging levels are used and when? logging, levels • DEBUG: detailed trace information (development only)
• INFO: request received, processing started, completed successfully
• WARNING: non-fatal issues (cache miss, retry attempt, slow query)
• ERROR: operation failed but system continues (LLM timeout, parsing error)
• CRITICAL: system-level failure (database connection lost, out of memory)
989 What are the inter-service communication patterns? communication, patterns • Synchronous REST: Core → AI for tutoring, assessment, search (real-time)
• Asynchronous Kafka: events for background processing (document, analytics)
• Callback REST: AI → Core for status updates (document processing complete)
• WebSocket: LiveKit for real-time video/audio
• SSE: AI → Frontend for streaming tutor responses

• "How would you debug a slow tutor response?" (tests troubleshooting methodology)
• "Why 5 databases instead of just PostgreSQL?" (tests design reasoning)
• "What breaks first under 10x load?" (tests scalability thinking)
• "How would you add a new agent to the system?" (tests extensibility understanding)

996 Name every Docker volume and what it stores. volumes, storage, complete • postgres_data: user accounts, classrooms, assessments, all relational data
• redis_data: persistent cache, session state, JWT blacklist
• qdrant_data: vector embeddings for all documents, notes, meetings
• mongo_data: proctoring frame analysis, detection results

• kafka_data: event log retention (7 days)
• livekit_data: room recordings
• nginx_certs: TLS certificates
• uploads_data: uploaded files (shared between core & AI)
• ml_models: trained model weights
997 What are the 3 riskiest parts of this architecture? risk, architecture • 1. Single PostgreSQL: SPOF with no automatic failover (need RDS or manual replication)
• 2. LLM dependency: external API determines core feature quality (rate limits, outages, pricing changes)
• 3. 60K-line WebIngestService: maintenance risk, single developer bottleneck, hard to test
998 What are the 3 most reusable components for other projects? reusable, components • 1. ABCR classification pattern: any chatbot can use query classification to route efficiently
• 2. RAG pipeline (6-node LangGraph): document → chunk → embed → search → generate (generic pattern)
• 3. Proctoring detector framework: lazy-loaded ML detectors with temporal fusion (any exam platform)
999 If you had 2 more weeks, what 3 features would you add? wishlist, features • 1. Distributed tracing (OpenTelemetry): trace requests across all services for debugging
• 2. Comprehensive integration test suite: end-to-end tests for every user workflow
• 3. Admin analytics dashboard: institution-level metrics, cost tracking, usage trends
1000 Final question: summarize EVERYTHING about EnsureStudy in one comprehensive answer. final, everything, summary • EnsureStudy is a full-stack AI-powered educational platform built with Flask (Core), FastAPI (AI), Next.js (Frontend), and 5 databases (PostgreSQL, Qdrant, Redis, MongoDB, Cassandra).
• 9 LangGraph agents (Orchestrator, Tutor, Research, Curriculum, Assessment, Learning, QuestionPool, Revision, Interview) process student queries through specialized pipelines.
• ABCR routing classifies queries into Academic/Beyond-scope/Casual/RAG-needed, saving 40% of unnecessary compute.
• RAG pipeline embeds teacher-uploaded documents (all-mpnet-base-v2, 768-dim) → indexes in Qdrant → retrieves via hybrid search (vector + keyword with RRF).
• 7-stage document processing handles PDF/DOCX/images with OCR, semantic chunking, and vector indexing.
• 8-detector proctoring (face, gaze, object, audio, tab-switch, head-pose, lip, multi-face) uses LightGBM + LSTM for real-time integrity scoring.
• Infrastructure: Docker Compose orchestration, Kafka for async events, Nginx reverse proxy, LiveKit for video meetings, Spark for analytics streaming.
• Security: JWT auth, RBAC, classroom-level MCP data isolation, bcrypt passwords, TLS, content moderation.
• Key trade-offs: polyglot databases (performance vs complexity), dual-LLM (cost vs quality), RAG vs fine-tuning (dynamic vs static), eventual consistency (speed vs guarantees).
• Total: 650+ files, 1000 interview questions, built to serve from startup (1K users) to enterprise (100K+ users).
